# Catch2 test binary. Picks up every *_test.cpp adjacent to source files in
# src/Pace so adding a test file is a one-step operation: drop it next
# to the implementation and re-configure.

file(GLOB_RECURSE PACE_TEST_SOURCES
    CONFIGURE_DEPENDS
    "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/src/Pace/*_test.cpp"
)

add_executable(PaceTest
    test_main.cpp
    # The shared test helpers' own cases: not under src/Pace, so the glob above cannot reach them.
    ScratchPath_test.cpp
    ${PACE_TEST_SOURCES}
)

target_link_libraries(PaceTest
    PRIVATE
        Pace
        Catch2::Catch2
)

# The checkout's paths -- the repository root, for the cases that check a document
# against the code it describes (`MetricsDocumentation_test` reads the daemon's
# per-series table off `docs/operations/deployment.md`), and the checked-in fixtures --
# come from <tests/CheckoutPaths.hpp>, generated by the top-level CMakeLists. Never a
# target_compile_definitions here: a definition reaches every unit of this target and
# puts the checkout into every unit's cache key (the header says what that cost).

# Catch.cmake (catch_discover_tests) is placed on CMAKE_MODULE_PATH by the
# top-level CMakeLists, regardless of whether Catch2 came from CPM source or a
# system find_package.
include(Catch)
# A Catch2 case that calls `SKIP(...)` exits **4**, and `SKIP_RETURN_CODE` is the
# only thing that tells ctest an exit code means *skipped* rather than *failed*.
# Without it the binary prints `1 skipped` and ctest prints `***Failed` for the very
# same run -- measured, not inferred (#499).
#
# That is a false RED, which is the insidious direction: the seven `SKIP` sites in
# this tree are all environment-conditional -- three on `AvailableCodecs().size() < 2`
# and four on being able to bind loopback -- so they fire on a constrained runner or a
# build configured without codecs, and report a regression that is not there. Nobody
# has hit it because those conditions have not arisen on CI, which is luck rather than
# coverage.
#
# It also makes `SKIP` unusable as a tool: whoever adds one and watches the suite go
# red concludes the skip is wrong and deletes it, rather than suspecting the
# registration. The wrong lesson, learned permanently.
#
# The repository already knew this. Eleven script-driven `add_test` registrations
# below set `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77`; it was simply never carried to the five Catch2
# registrations, which are the ones discovering thousands of cases.
#
# `catch-skip-return-code` covers the door this comment cannot: a sixth test binary
# added without the property would reopen the hole by omission, exactly as the fifth
# did.
#
# **On Catch2 3.6.0 it was broken in the other direction.** `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 4` closed
# that false RED and opened a false GREEN (#1128): the exit code was then the
# failed-assertion count, so a case failing exactly four assertions was scored
# *skipped*, and so was a case that skipped one `SECTION` and failed four assertions in
# another. The repair for one state collapse was the site of the next.
#
# Catch2 3.7.1 standardised its exit codes -- every case skipped exits 4, any failure 42
# whatever the count -- and under the Catch2 the top-level CMakeLists pins EXACTLY the
# property is exact. The argument is there, one place pointed at rather than restated,
# because this comment exists five times over; `catch-skip-exit-contract` below runs the
# shapes against the linked Catch2, so the contract cannot rot into a false rule.
catch_discover_tests(PaceTest PROPERTIES SKIP_RETURN_CODE 4 ENVIRONMENT_MODIFICATION "${PACE_ERROR_DIALOG_ENVIRONMENT}")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The ThreadSanitizer canary: a program that MUST fail, so that a green sanitizer
# job means something. See src/tests/TsanCanary.cpp for why it contains a
# deliberate data race, and scripts/tsan-gate.sh for what refuses to run without
# it.
#
# Built here, by the same CMake that instruments everything else, rather than
# compiled by the gate script with flags of its own. That is the entire point: it
# inherits `-fsanitize=thread` from the same `add_compile_options` the real
# targets get, so it detects a Sanitizers.cmake that has stopped applying them --
# the defect that module's own comment records. A canary built with hand-written
# flags would keep passing through exactly that failure.
#
# Deliberately NOT linked against Pace: it answers "is the toolchain
# instrumenting and reporting", which has to stay answerable even when the
# library does not build.
#
# Guarded, so no other configuration builds a knowingly-racy binary. The guard
# does not weaken the check -- when the option is ON but the flags never reach the
# compile line, this target still exists and still comes out uninstrumented,
# which is precisely the case the gate is looking for.
if(ENABLE_SANITIZER_THREAD)
    add_executable(tsan-canary TsanCanary.cpp)
    # No Catch2 and no catch_discover_tests. CTest could express "this must fail"
    # -- `WILL_FAIL`, or `PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "data race"` -- so that is not
    # the reason; the reason is that the canary's verdict has to be read BEFORE
    # the suite is believed, and a sibling ctest case that merely also passes
    # proves nothing about the run that reported clean. scripts/tsan-gate.sh
    # sequences the two, and is the only thing that should run this.
    find_package(Threads REQUIRED)
    target_link_libraries(tsan-canary PRIVATE Threads::Threads)
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The MSVC checked-iterator canary: the Windows Debug counterpart of the one
# above, and deliberately the same shape rather than a second idiom.
#
# A Windows Debug leg is worth running for `_ITERATOR_DEBUG_LEVEL=2` -- it traps
# invalidated iterators, out-of-range indexing and mismatched container iterators
# at the point they happen, which is exactly what a Release build tolerates in
# silence. But nothing here STATES that level: it follows from `_DEBUG`, which
# follows from the runtime library, which follows from `CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE` and
# `CMAKE_MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY`. Any of those moving takes the checks away with no
# warning, and the leg would still build, still run the whole suite, and still
# report green while checking nothing it was added to check.
#
# So it is asserted at run time by a program that must die (#315).
#
# Unlike `tsan-canary` this one IS registered as a ctest, and the difference is
# worth stating: the TSan canary has to be read BEFORE the suite is believed, so a
# shell gate sequences the two. Here the canary answers a question about the
# BUILD, not about a suite run, so it is just another check -- and registering it
# means it runs for anyone building `cl-debug` locally rather than only in CI,
# which is where this defect would otherwise be found.
#
# `scripts/iterator-debug-gate.ps1` and not `WILL_FAIL`, because a bare inversion
# cannot say WHY a green result is meaningless, and because a non-zero exit is not
# proof on its own -- the gate requires the runtime's own "subscript out of range"
# diagnostic, the same way the TSan gate requires "data race".
if(MSVC AND CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE STREQUAL "Debug")
    add_executable(iterator-debug-canary IteratorDebugCanary.cpp)
    add_test(
        NAME "iterator-debug-canary"
        COMMAND pwsh -NoProfile -File "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/iterator-debug-gate.ps1"
                -Canary "$<TARGET_FILE:iterator-debug-canary>"
    )
    set_tests_properties("iterator-debug-canary" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# How a `cmake -P` check reports FAILURE.
#
# Not with an exit code alone, and the reason is not the one written here for
# months. This paragraph said `message(FATAL_ERROR)` in script mode "prints
# `CMake Error ...` and then exits **0** on CMake 3.28 -- measured, not inferred:
# 3.28.3 exits 0, 4.3.1 exits 1", and thirteen registrations rest on it.
#
# **It does not reproduce** (#565): exit 1 on 3.22.6, 3.25.2, 3.27.9, 3.28.3,
# 3.31.6 and 4.3.0, across six script shapes, with controls proving the harness
# could read a zero. `ctest -R fatal-error-exit` asks it now on every platform CI
# builds, so the claim is a measurement rather than a sentence.
#
# The likely mechanism is a TRUE neighbouring fact carried one clause too far. The
# paragraph below says a `-P` script cannot CHOOSE its own exit code before 3.29 --
# `cmake_language(EXIT)` is 3.29, and on 3.28.3 it is an unknown command -- and
# concludes SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION for the skip direction, which is right.
# "Cannot choose its exit code" then became "cannot signal failure by exit code",
# and `message(FATAL_ERROR)` hands you exactly one code you did not choose, 1,
# which is the only one a failure needs.
#
# **The property stays, on the two reasons that are actually true.**
# `message(WARNING)` exits 0 on every version above while printing `CMake Warning`,
# so a check that WARNS is audible only through the output (#517) -- which is why
# the pattern covers both words. And a check that shells out to another CMake
# without reading RESULT_VARIABLE exits 0 with its child's error on the output;
# that is the shape `script-check-canary` is now built out of, because carried by
# an exit code it proved nothing at all.
#
# So the verdict is read from the output, which every CMake version produces
# identically. One spelling, here, rather than thirteen literals: a pattern that
# drifts between checks is a check that stops matching without saying so.
# `script-check-canary` below is a deliberately failing script that must be seen
# to fail, and `script-check-signals` refuses a registration that omits this.
# It also covers `CMake Warning`, because a check that WARNS is a check nobody
# reads. `check-net-boundary` emitted 629 warnings across 6291 lines on a clean
# tree while passing, from a single invalid escape sequence -- and the diagnostic
# naming that defect was buried inside the wall the defect produced (#517). The
# guard lives in the SHARED spelling rather than on the check where it was noticed,
# because a guard scoped to the file where a problem was found is how the next
# instance goes unnoticed.
#
# What it catches and what it does not, because the next reader will assume it is
# broader than it is: it catches a check that EMITS CMake warnings. It says nothing
# about a check whose output is enormous for some other reason, and it does not make
# an over-noisy check correct -- a check can be perfectly quiet and still print more
# than anyone will read.
#
# Measured before it was added: all twenty checks and all four selftests were
# already warning-clean once `check-net-boundary` was fixed, so this costs nothing
# today and closes the door for every script check rather than for the one that
# happened to be noticed.
#
# `script-check-warning-canary` below is the matching must-be-seen-to-warn half.
set(PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED "CMake Error|CMake Warning")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The interpreter every script-driven test runs under (#379).
#
# `/bin/bash` and never a bare `bash`, because a bare one resolves through PATH.
# `.agent/rules/build-and-toolchain.md` requires these scripts to work on bash
# **3.2**, which is what `/bin/bash` on macOS is. The rule had no executor: it was
# enforced by careful reading, and a `mapfile` had already taken
# `merge-queue-contexts` red on `macOS-clang-release` once before anyone noticed.
#
# This paragraph used to open by stating that Homebrew's bash 5.x sits ahead of Apple's
# 2007 `/bin/bash` on GitHub's image, so every script test there ran under bash 5. That
# is **measured false** and was already known to be, in this same tree:
# `check-script-interpreter.cmake` records both spellings registered side by side and run
# on `macOS-clang-release` (PR #806, 2026-09-05), where `COMMAND bash` and
# `COMMAND /bin/bash` BOTH resolved to `/bin/bash` 3.2.57. Two files in one repository
# asserting opposite things about one fact is how the wrong one survives -- #560's lesson,
# and the reason the claim is corrected here rather than left for a reader to reconcile.
#
# What the pin below buys is therefore not a repair. CMake does not resolve a bare command
# name at configure time, so `CTestTestfile.cmake` keeps the literal `bash` and the
# interpreter is chosen from PATH at RUN time; naming `/bin/bash` removes a live
# dependency on PATH ordering rather than fixing a present defect.
#
# Windows has no `/bin/bash`, and it does NOT keep the PATH lookup either (#1355). This
# paragraph used to say Git for Windows' bash "is the only one there and nothing is being
# selected between", and that is MEASURED FALSE: a Windows host with WSL carries
# `C:\Windows\System32\bash.exe` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WindowsApps\bash.exe`, both
# launchers for LINUX bash, and on this project's development machine both come ahead of
# Git on the PATH a PowerShell or a Visual Studio developer shell hands `ctest`. Every script
# test then starts under Linux bash with a Windows path and answers "No such file or
# directory" -- 53 of 136 `hygiene` entries in one run, measured, each reading as a defect in
# the tree. CI's Windows legs run `ctest` from `pwsh` as well (the step names no shell) and
# pass, which says that image puts Git first; that is inferred from their being green, not
# read from a log.
#
# So Windows asks for Git's own `bin/bash.exe`, found beside the git `cmake/Version.cmake`
# already located. `bin/` and not `usr/bin/`: the first is Git's launcher, which sets
# `MSYSTEM` and puts `/usr/bin` on PATH, while the second started from PowerShell finds no
# `sed`, `grep` or `mktemp` at all (measured). Each candidate is ASKED for `MSYSTEM`, which
# Linux bash and a bare `usr/bin/bash.exe` both leave empty, because an existing file is not
# an interpreter that runs. Nothing found keeps the bare name and says so at configure.
# The bash 3.2 rule is about macOS's system interpreter, and this is the one place that
# says so.
#
# One variable rather than twenty-nine literals, and `check-script-interpreter`
# asserts every registration uses it. A literal `/bin/bash` is refused too: it
# would be correct today and break the Windows rows the day somebody adds one.
if(WIN32)
    set(PACE_BASH bash)
    file(TO_CMAKE_PATH "$ENV{ProgramFiles}" _programFiles)
    set(_gitBashCandidates "${_programFiles}/Git/bin/bash.exe")
    if(GIT_EXECUTABLE)
        # `cmd/git.exe` and `bin/git.exe` sit one level below Git's root, `mingw64/bin/git.exe` two.
        get_filename_component(_gitDir "${GIT_EXECUTABLE}" DIRECTORY)
        list(PREPEND _gitBashCandidates "${_gitDir}/../bin/bash.exe" "${_gitDir}/../../bin/bash.exe")
    endif()
    foreach(_gitBash IN LISTS _gitBashCandidates)
        if(NOT EXISTS "${_gitBash}")
            continue()
        endif()
        set(_gitBashSystem "")
        execute_process(
            COMMAND "${_gitBash}" -c "printf '%s' \"\${MSYSTEM:-}\""
            OUTPUT_VARIABLE _gitBashSystem
            ERROR_QUIET
            ENCODING NONE
            # A configure blocked in a subprocess writes nothing and reads as a hang.
            TIMEOUT 30
        )
        if(_gitBashSystem)
            get_filename_component(PACE_BASH "${_gitBash}" ABSOLUTE)
            break()
        endif()
    endforeach()
    if(PACE_BASH STREQUAL "bash")
        message(STATUS "[bash] no Git for Windows bin/bash.exe answered, so script tests take `bash` from PATH at run time -- where a WSL launcher can come first")
    else()
        message(STATUS "[bash] script tests run under ${PACE_BASH}")
    endif()

    # And no test inherits the caller's MSYS path-conversion switch (#1355). A Git Bash that
    # exported `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` / `MSYS2_ARG_CONV_EXCL='*'` -- which the rulebook tells a
    # developer driving MSVC to do -- handed it to every script `ctest` started, and a script
    # spelling a POSIX path to a native program depends on the conversion it switches off.
    # Measured: `pacectl-e2e` started `pace-kv.exe` on `/tmp/.../empty.yaml` and
    # reported the daemon DIED, deterministically, and only with the switch exported.
    #
    # Every test in this directory rather than a list of the ones seen failing: a list is exact
    # about what it knows and silent about the next. DEFERRED to the end of this directory, so a
    # registration appended below this line is covered too. A script that needs the switch pins
    # it itself (`check-banner-probe-identity.sh`), and `run-check.sh` unsets it as well, for a
    # check run by hand outside `ctest`.
    function(_pace_unset_msys_conversion)
        get_property(_tests DIRECTORY "${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}" PROPERTY TESTS)
        foreach(_test IN LISTS _tests)
            set_property(TEST "${_test}" APPEND PROPERTY ENVIRONMENT_MODIFICATION
                "MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=unset:" "MSYS2_ARG_CONV_EXCL=unset:")
        endforeach()
    endfunction()
    cmake_language(DEFER CALL _pace_unset_msys_conversion)
else()
    set(PACE_BASH /bin/bash)
endif()

# The PowerShell interpreter, resolved once, for the same reason `PACE_BASH` is.
#
# A bare `pwsh` in a COMMAND is left unresolved in `CTestTestfile.cmake` and picked from
# PATH at RUN time, so which interpreter a `.ps1` test gets is a property of the image.
# Eight sites spelled it bare. `check-script-interpreter.cmake` reads `.sh` only, so
# nothing would ever report the drift.
#
# `powershell` (Windows PowerShell 5.1) is deliberately NOT on this list, and the reason
# is a mistake worth recording. It WAS, on the evidence that
# `node-scratch-isolation-e2e.ps1 -SelfTest` passes under both pwsh 7.6.5 and Windows
# PowerShell 5.1 -- measured, and true. But this variable gates TWO scripts, and the
# second was never asked. Measured:
#
#     pwsh        dist-compile-e2e.ps1 -SelfTest  ->  exit 0, PASSED
#     powershell  dist-compile-e2e.ps1 -SelfTest  ->  exit 1, MethodNotFound
#
# `[System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::HashData` is .NET 5+, so 5.1 cannot run it. A
# host with no pwsh -- an ordinary developer Windows box, where the hosted images all
# ship one -- would resolve `powershell` and take that test RED for a reason unrelated to
# the change under test. That is the exact criterion the block below states ("an
# interpreter that could not run it would turn this skip into a failure, which is worse
# than the `if(WIN32)` it replaces"), failed by carrying one script's measurement over to
# another. Err NARROW: a 5.1-only host now SKIPS both, which is honest, where the wider
# list failed one of them.
find_program(PACE_PWSH NAMES pwsh pwsh-preview)

# Register a test that could NOT be run here, as a SKIP.
#
# Absent is not skipped. A registration omitted because a host lacks a prerequisite
# vanishes from the run with nothing saying it was ever expected, and no count carries
# the difference. This puts the row in every run and makes it say which of the two it is.
#
# The `SKIP: ` sentinel and the `SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` that reads it are spelled HERE,
# once, and that is the point rather than tidiness: written per site they drift apart by
# OMISSION, and the omission is silent in the worst direction. `cmake -E echo` exits 0, so
# a site that loses the property reports **Passed** for a property nothing established --
# #685's SUCCEED-standing-in-for-a-skip defect, reachable by forgetting one line.
function(pace_register_skipped_test name reason)
    add_test(NAME "${name}" COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -E echo "SKIP: ${reason}")
    set_tests_properties("${name}" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "SKIP: "
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
endfunction()

# The interpreter for the one script that is NOT bound by the bash-3.2 rule (#598).
#
# The pin above exists to enforce that rule. `tidy-sweep.sh` is exempt from it by
# construction: it declares a bash-4.4 floor and exits 77 below one, so running it
# through the 3.2 pin asserts a rule it is not bound by and reports SKIPPED on macOS in
# perpetuity -- new coverage that is Linux-only with nothing saying so.
#
# #598 proposed adding `bash` to the macOS `brew install` line, on the reasoning that
# "Homebrew's bin precedes /bin, so the newer bash is what `COMMAND bash` resolves to".
# That was true when it was filed on 2026-09-02 and stopped being true on 2026-09-05,
# when c414428a (#809) pinned every registration to `/bin/bash` precisely so macOS runs
# the 2007 interpreter. Brew installs to `/opt/homebrew/bin` and cannot touch a
# SIP-protected `/bin/bash`, so the one-word fix would have left the leg SKIPPED while
# looking like it worked -- which is the failure #598's own acceptance clause warns
# about. The brew line is still needed, so that a 4.4 exists to be found at all.
#
# VERSION-CHECKED and not merely found, and searched by CANDIDATE rather than by PATH.
#
# Both halves are load-bearing on macOS specifically, and the second is easy to get
# wrong because a claim in this tree said otherwise. `check-script-interpreter.cmake`
# carries the measurement that settles it -- both spellings registered side by side and
# run on `macOS-clang-release` (PR #806, 2026-09-05):
#
#     COMMAND bash       -> /bin/bash  3.2.57(1)-release
#     COMMAND /bin/bash  -> /bin/bash  3.2.57(1)-release
#
# Homebrew's `bin` is NOT ahead of `/bin` on that image. So `find_program(NAMES bash)`
# there returns Apple's 3.2 even after `brew install bash` puts a 5.x on disk, and a
# PATH-order search would report "no 4.4 available" on a runner that has one. The
# candidates are therefore named, most-specific first, and each is ASKED its version --
# registering an unchecked find would put the interpreter question back where it started
# behind a variable named as though it had been answered.
#
# NOT ASKED ON WINDOWS (#1355). Its one consumer, `tidy-sweep-selftest`, is registered only
# off Windows, and PATH's `bash` there can be a WSL launcher: measured, the probe started a
# Linux interpreter at configure and printed `[bash44] C:/Windows/System32/bash.exe is 5.2`
# for a variable nothing on that host reads.
set(PACE_BASH44 "")
set(PACE_BASH44_VERSION "")
set(_bashCandidates "")
if(NOT WIN32)
    find_program(PACE_BASH44_ON_PATH NAMES bash)
    set(_bashCandidates
        /opt/homebrew/bin/bash          # Homebrew, Apple Silicon
        /usr/local/bin/bash             # Homebrew, Intel
        "${PACE_BASH44_ON_PATH}"  # whatever PATH offers
        /bin/bash)                      # the system interpreter, last
endif()
# On a stock macOS the third and fourth are the SAME binary -- PATH's `bash` IS
# `/bin/bash` there, which is the measurement this whole block rests on -- so without
# this the 3.2 interpreter is spawned and version-probed twice, on exactly the host
# where the answer is "no candidate qualifies" and all four are tried.
if(_bashCandidates)
    list(REMOVE_DUPLICATES _bashCandidates)
endif()
foreach(_bashCandidate IN LISTS _bashCandidates)
    if(NOT _bashCandidate OR NOT EXISTS "${_bashCandidate}")
        continue()
    endif()
    execute_process(
        COMMAND "${_bashCandidate}" -c "printf '%s.%s' \"\${BASH_VERSINFO[0]}\" \"\${BASH_VERSINFO[1]}\""
        OUTPUT_VARIABLE _candidateVersion
        ERROR_QUIET
        # ENCODING NONE: `execute_process` defaults to AUTO and decodes the child through
        # the console code page.
        ENCODING NONE
    )
    if(_candidateVersion AND NOT _candidateVersion VERSION_LESS "4.4")
        set(PACE_BASH44 "${_bashCandidate}")
        set(PACE_BASH44_VERSION "${_candidateVersion}")
        break()
    endif()
endforeach()

if(WIN32)
    message(STATUS "[bash44] not probed on Windows, where tidy-sweep's self-test is not registered")
elseif(PACE_BASH44)
    message(STATUS "[bash44] ${PACE_BASH44} is ${PACE_BASH44_VERSION}")
else()
    message(STATUS
        "[bash44] no bash >= 4.4 among the candidates, so tidy-sweep's self-test will "
        "report SKIPPED on this host")
endif()

# The configure refusal of a build tree configured before the rename to Pace
# (cmake/RetiredOptions.cmake, #1713): its decision names a FASTCACHED_ entry and passes
# PACE_ ones. Judged by output, as every script check is.
add_test(
    NAME "retired-cmake-options"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR} -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-retired-options.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("retired-cmake-options" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# The canary: a check that must be seen to fail. See scripts/script-check-canary.cmake
# for why a property needs one. WILL_FAIL inverts the verdict, so this reports
# green exactly while the mechanism above works, and red the moment it does not.
#
# That script exits **0** and prints a real `CMake Error` from a nested `cmake -P`,
# which is not a curiosity but the only shape in which this canary means anything
# (#565). A test fails when its exit code is non-zero OR the pattern matches, and
# WILL_FAIL inverts the whole verdict -- so the bare `message(FATAL_ERROR)` this
# used to be passed on its exit code alone, and would have gone on reporting green
# with FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION deleted from every registration in this file.
# Measured under ctest, three cases: the current shape passes with the property and
# FAILS without it; the old shape passes without it.
#
# In the default set with the checks it guards, and cheap for the same reason they
# are: it reads nothing, and the one process it starts is a second `cmake -P` of
# itself, which compiles nothing.
add_test(
    NAME "script-check-canary"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/script-check-canary.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("script-check-canary" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    WILL_FAIL TRUE
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And the warning half of the same mechanism, which is silent forever on a clean
# tree and would therefore rot unnoticed. See scripts/script-check-warning-canary.cmake.
#
# It warns and does NOT fail, deliberately: a script that also printed `CMake Error`
# would trip the other branch of the alternation and stay green even if the warning
# branch were deleted -- proving nothing while looking like proof.
add_test(
    NAME "script-check-warning-canary"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/script-check-warning-canary.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("script-check-warning-canary" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    WILL_FAIL TRUE
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And the omission half. The canary proves the mechanism works; it says nothing
# about whether a given registration USES it, and a check registered without the
# property is inert in exactly the way all thirteen were. Derived from this file
# rather than from a list restated in the checker -- a second copy of the set is
# not a cross-check, it is a second thing to be wrong.
#
# THREE rules over that one derived set. This comment named only the first until
# #497 added the second, and only two until #565 added the third:
#
#   * every registration carries FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION, so ctest can hear it
#     fail at all;
#   * every script behind one declares `cmake_minimum_required`, because script
#     mode sets no policies -- so `if(... IN_LIST ...)` errors out on CMake 3.28
#     and answers on 4.x, and `if(TRUE)` is FALSE on 3.28 and true on 4.x. The
#     check's own header carries the measurements.
#   * every WILL_FAIL registration's script exits 0. WILL_FAIL inverts ctest's
#     whole verdict and that verdict is `non-zero exit OR the pattern matched`, so
#     a canary exiting non-zero passes on its status alone and would stay green
#     with the property deleted from every registration above. The canaries avoid
#     that by exiting 0, and this is what stops "they exit 0" being one more
#     sentence somebody has to remember. It runs them, which is two `cmake -P`
#     spawns and the only thing this check starts.
#
# Both are properties of a registration that reading it cannot reveal, which is
# why they are asserted rather than remembered -- and why a comment here that
# describes half of what the check does is the same defect in miniature.
add_test(
    NAME "script-check-signals"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-script-check-signals.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("script-check-signals" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And the third question about the same mechanism, which is neither "does it work"
# nor "does every registration use it" but *is the reason we need it still true*.
#
# `FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` was required on the stated ground that
# `message(FATAL_ERROR)` in script mode exits 0 on CMake 3.28, this project's
# declared minimum. That does not reproduce: exit 1 on 3.22.6, 3.25.2, 3.27.9,
# 3.28.3, 3.31.6 and 4.3.0 (#565). The property stays, because two OTHER reasons
# are true and were never the stated one -- `message(WARNING)` exits 0 on every
# version while printing `CMake Warning`, which only an output verdict can hear
# (#517), and a check that shells out to another CMake without reading
# RESULT_VARIABLE exits 0 while its child's error is on the output.
#
# A correction that is itself only a sentence would sit exactly where the wrong
# sentence sat, so this asks the question on every platform CI builds, on every
# run. It is three `cmake -P` invocations that compile nothing. A red one is a
# discovery rather than a defect: it means the note's condition has been found on
# some platform, and the property is load-bearing there rather than defence in
# depth.
add_test(
    NAME "fatal-error-exit"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-fatal-error-exit.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("fatal-error-exit" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# Its verdict, driven against staged readings in both directions. The acquisition
# half needs a real CMake and a real machine, so only the DECISION is driven here
# -- the split `.agent/rules/testing.md` records. A check that has only ever been
# seen to pass has told you nothing, and this one would otherwise be green on
# every machine forever.
add_test(
    NAME "fatal-error-exit-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-fatal-error-exit-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("fatal-error-exit-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And that check watched REFUSING, which it had never been (#680).
#
# It has run green since #497 and nothing had ever made it object. #680 widened
# its pass 3 to the two CPack hook scripts -- `cmake/MacOSSignBinaries.cmake` and
# `cmake/MacOSNotarizePkg.cmake`, which no registration in this file names and
# which CPack runs in script mode exactly as ctest runs a check. A widening whose
# failure mode is silence needs the refusal shown: a scan that stops matching
# reports the same "all clear" as one that matched everything.
#
# Five synthesised trees -- a control that must PASS, then a hook stripped of its
# declaration, the hook VARIABLE renamed so the scan matches nothing, the variable
# pointing at a script that is not there, and a `;` in the hook path. The last is
# there because the check was caught miscounting its own output: CMake splits a
# semicolon inside `list(APPEND)` into two elements, so one violation reported as
# two. Each arm asserts the finding COUNT, not just that something was refused.
#
# Subject to the rule it tests, like everything else here: FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION.
# NOT for the "FATAL_ERROR exits 0 on 3.28" reason, which #565 refuted and #788
# corrected two blocks up -- for the reason that actually applies to this script:
# it runs the check under test through `execute_process` and reads its OUTPUT, so
# its own exit status says nothing about what it found.
add_test(
    NAME "script-check-signals-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/script-check-signals-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-script-check-signals-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("script-check-signals-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
)

# Every script-driven test runs through `${PACE_BASH}` (#379).
#
# The rule requiring bash 3.2 compatibility had nothing running it -- or so #379
# said. Measured, it did: on `macOS-clang-release` a bare `bash` and `/bin/bash`
# are the SAME 3.2.57 interpreter, so the constraint was already enforced and the
# premise does not reproduce. What this pins is that it stays that way: CMake
# leaves a bare command name unresolved in `CTestTestfile.cmake`, so the
# interpreter comes from PATH at RUN time and would move the day the image gains
# a newer bash.
#
# The set of sites is DERIVED here rather than listed anywhere. #379's body named
# six; the tree has thirty, and one of them is not a `COMMAND` at all --
# `sccache-smoke.sh` is reached through a `set(_smoke_driver ...)`, which is the
# form a reader scanning for `COMMAND bash` walks past, and it backs three
# REQUIRED contexts. Converting the six and reporting the rule enforced would
# have left twenty-four sites untouched with every test green.
add_test(
    NAME "script-interpreter"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-script-interpreter.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("script-interpreter" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And that check watched refusing, in BOTH directions (#379's acceptance).
#
# Six synthesised trees: a control that is the REAL registration file copied
# unmodified and must PASS, then a bare `bash`, a literal `/bin/bash` (correct
# today and wrong on Windows the day a row moves, so what is pinned is the
# VARIABLE), a site with no interpreter token at all, the definition removed, and
# a scan matching nothing.
#
# The control is not decoration: a check that refused everything would also
# refuse a bare `bash`, and only a case that must pass separates a guard from a
# tripwire. It is the real file rather than a fixture because the site this check
# exists for -- `set(_smoke_driver ...)`, backing three required contexts -- is
# exactly what a hand-written fixture would not have thought to include.
#
# Every arm asserts the finding COUNT. A defect reported as several findings is
# the check miscounting its own output (#796), and this check's own violation
# strings did it once already.
add_test(
    NAME "script-interpreter-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/script-interpreter-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-script-interpreter-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("script-interpreter-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A hygiene check traverses each directory once, not once per file pattern.
#
# `file(GLOB_RECURSE var a b c)` traverses the tree once PER PATTERN, and the call site
# reads as a single glob. `check-sccache-backend-caveat` handed it ~19 patterns per
# root across two calls and took 78.7 s against a 60 s budget on a DrvFs checkout, so
# the documented local gate could not go green for anyone working that way (#502).
# One traversal of `src/` costs 2.09 s there and is effectively free on ext4, which is
# why CI never saw it.
#
# The rule is `GLOB_RECURSE` receives exactly one pattern, and that pattern is not an
# unquoted variable expansion -- the original defect was `${rootGlobs}`, ONE argument
# and nineteen traversals, which an argument count alone would have passed.
#
# It is a PROXY for traversals-per-root and the check's header says so: nineteen
# single-pattern calls in a loop cost the same and pass. A guard whose limits are
# written down is worth having; one that reads as complete is not.
#
# In the default set with the other hygiene checks: it reads files and compares
# strings.
add_test(
    NAME "glob-traversals"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-glob-traversals.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("glob-traversals" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And that check must be seen to refuse, on each thing it claims and nothing else.
#
# Seven synthetic trees, one verdict each: one pattern passes, two are refused with the
# count named, an unquoted list expansion is refused, the `RELATIVE` /
# `LIST_DIRECTORIES` / `CONFIGURE_DEPENDS` keywords are not counted as patterns, a
# `GLOB_RECURSE` inside a `message()` string is prose and not a call, a tree with none
# at all reports a broken scan, and a call after a stray `]` in a comment is reported at
# its REAL line -- CMake's list grouping merges lines, and this check once named line
# 217 for a call at line 307.
#
# The unbalanced brackets in this comment block are DELIBERATE and load-bearing. One of
# them caught `check-target-file-guards.cmake` reading this file through a splitter that
# escaped `;` and ignored brackets: that check then saw none of this file's 25
# `$<TARGET_FILE:>` references and refused as vacuous rather than passing over a file it
# could no longer read. Do not "tidy" them away -- they are what exercises that splitter,
# and they cost nothing now that it is fixed.
#
# The last two cases exist because the check got them wrong while it was written, which
# is the best reason a case can have.
add_test(
    NAME "glob-traversals-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/glob-traversals-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-glob-traversals-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("glob-traversals-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Every `catch_discover_tests` registration tells ctest what a SKIP looks like, and
# tells it by the case's EXIT CODE, `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 4`.
#
# A Catch2 case that calls `SKIP(...)` exits 4, and a registration that says nothing
# leaves ctest scoring that as a FAILURE -- so the binary prints `1 skipped` and ctest
# prints `***Failed` for the very same run. Measured on a forced skip, not inferred
# (#499). None of the five Catch2 registrations said anything, while script-driven
# `add_test` registrations below were already setting `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77` one
# directory away -- so the mechanism was known and simply never carried to the
# registrations that discover thousands of cases.
#
# **And 4, not 77.** The script-driven `77` registrations below stay: a shell script
# chooses its own exit code, and a Catch2 binary does not -- it exits 4 when every case
# skipped and 42 on any failure. On 3.6.0 it spent the status on its failed-assertion
# count instead, which made 4 a collision with a four-failure case (#1128); the exact
# Catch2 pin and `catch-skip-exit-contract` are what keep it from being one again.
#
# A comment is not the fix and a check is: a SIXTH test binary would reopen this by
# omission exactly as the fifth did. Same argument as `script-check-signals`, and the
# set of registrations is READ from the tree.
#
# In the default set with the other hygiene checks: it reads files and compares
# strings.
add_test(
    NAME "catch-skip-return-code"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-catch-skip-return-code.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("catch-skip-return-code" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And that check must be SEEN to fail, on each direction it claims.
#
# Synthetic trees, one verdict asserted each -- the script prints how many it drove,
# derived, because a total written beside a list of cases is a second source of truth
# and this one had already drifted. A registration carrying `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 4` passes;
# a bare one fails and NAMES the file; `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77` fails, since a Catch2 binary
# never exits 77; the property on a continuation line passes; a tree with no
# registrations reports a broken scan rather than a clean tree; a registration inside a
# build tree is ignored -- the first version of the check walked into `_deps/catch2-src`
# and reported vendored code nobody here can edit; and the git mode CI actually takes is
# exercised in both directions. The script's header names every case.
#
# Per case rather than `WILL_FAIL`, because a check that stopped refusing and one
# that refuses everything are opposite defects a bare inversion reports identically.
add_test(
    NAME "catch-skip-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/catch-skip-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-catch-skip-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("catch-skip-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The reader for the exit contract `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 4` above rests on.
#
# The property is exact only because Catch2, since 3.7.1, exits 4 when every case
# skipped and one fixed failure code on ANY failure, whatever the count. On 3.6.0 the
# exit code was the failed-assertion count and a case failing exactly four assertions
# was scored *skipped* (#1128). The top-level CMakeLists pins Catch2 EXACTLY, and this
# runs every shape of the canary against the Catch2 actually linked: every skip-only
# shape must exit 4, every failing one -- one failure, four, three hundred, a skip
# beside failures -- one value that is neither 0 nor 4. A red is a discovery about the
# linked Catch2, not a defect in the tree.
#
# The canary is deliberately NOT registered with `catch_discover_tests`: several of its
# cases fail on purpose. It also `static_assert`s the 4 against Catch2's own
# `AllTestsSkippedExitCode`, so the literal every registration carries is pinned to the
# API at compile time.
add_executable(catch-skip-canary CatchSkipCanary.cpp)
target_link_libraries(catch-skip-canary PRIVATE Catch2::Catch2WithMain)

add_test(
    NAME "catch-skip-exit-contract"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DCANARY=$<TARGET_FILE:catch-skip-canary>"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-catch-skip-exit-contract.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("catch-skip-exit-contract" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A case that could not RUN its check does not report a PASS.
#
# The converse of `catch-skip-return-code` above, and the insidious direction.
# `SUCCEED("...")` records a passing assertion, so a case that bails out because its
# environment could not be arranged -- no loopback listener, no IPv6 stack, no symlink
# privilege -- reports green for a property nothing established (#685). A false RED gets
# investigated; a false GREEN is believed, and it fires exactly on the constrained
# runners where coverage is thinnest.
#
# Twenty-one sites were doing it, and the idiom spread by IMITATION: the launcher lane
# copied it out of `DirectManifest_test.cpp`, comment and all. The comment there was
# already correct, which is why a comment is not the fix and this is.
#
# In the default set with the other hygiene checks: it reads files and compares strings.
add_test(
    NAME "succeed-not-skip"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-succeed-not-skip.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("succeed-not-skip" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And that check must be SEEN to fail, on each direction it claims.
#
# Thirteen synthetic trees, one verdict asserted each: a SUCCEED that ends a case passes,
# so does `SUCCEED()` with no message and the idiom quoted in a `//` comment or inside a
# `/* ... */` block; a SUCCEED followed by `return;` fails and NAMES the file, and so does
# the same defect written on ONE line, which a scan starting at the following line walks
# past; a skip-vocabulary message with no return fails on its own, so neither signal is
# decoration; a SUCCEED the scan cannot read on one line fails as its own outcome; no
# SUCCEED at all and no test source at all each fail as a broken scan, in different words
# because they are different repairs; a violation under a build tree is ignored; and the
# git path -- the one CI takes -- ignores an untracked vendored violation, still catches a
# tracked one, and does not turn an index entry whose file was deleted into an unreadable
# path this registration cannot tell from a real finding.
#
# Per case rather than `WILL_FAIL`, because a check that stopped refusing and one that
# refuses everything are opposite defects a bare inversion reports identically. Measured
# against six mutants of the check: removing each of the bail-out, one-line, block-comment
# and deleted-file guards reddens exactly its own case, emptying the vocabulary reddens
# the message-driven ones, and making it refuse everything reddens all seven that assert
# silence.
add_test(
    NAME "succeed-not-skip-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/succeed-not-skip-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-succeed-not-skip-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("succeed-not-skip-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# Not labelled "smoke": it starts nothing, needs no compiler and no launcher, and

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Whether a set-but-EMPTY PACE_ADDR opts out of the launcher, as the
# operator documentation promises. The module folded it into the default, so
# `export PACE_ADDR=` kept caching on every shell and every platform (#372).
#
# Driven as a pure computation for a reason the fixture could not work around:
# the two inputs that matter differ ONLY in whether the environment defines the
# name, the value being empty either way -- and on PowerShell that case cannot be
# expressed at all, since `$env:X = ""` keeps the name in PowerShell's own view
# while the child never receives it. A configure-based fixture would there be
# asserting the wrong thing while looking right.
#
# Not labelled "smoke": it starts nothing and finishes in milliseconds.
add_test(

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A version-mismatched pace-cc falling through to sccache must say so where
# the fallback happens, not eighty lines from the warning about what sccache
# costs under MSVC (#658). Narrow: only that reason, only that winner, and only
# where the caveat exists at all.
#
# Pure, because the real situation needs two binaries at disagreeing wire
# versions plus an MSVC-family compiler -- a second machine and a downgrade
# rather than a fixture.
add_test(
    NAME "version-fallback-warning"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-version-fallback-warning.cmake"
)

    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# A git query that does not answer is never read as its negative: an unanswered dirty check
# is `-dirty-unknown` and never "clean", an unanswered distance fails the git-tag row and never
# yields the bare tag, and either makes the version Provisional, so
# PACE_REQUIRE_EXACT_VERSION refuses it. Drives the real GetVersionInformation against a
# stub git (a `.cmd` on Windows, a shell script elsewhere) that holds one chosen query past an
# injected bound. The all-answer cases are the positive control that the stub ran. Its
# `classifications` table is the cross-version guard: it hands every measured CMake spelling
# straight to the classifier, so a word only CMake 3.28 prints is judged on every CMake. PASS as
# well as FAIL: the script refuses when fewer cases ran than it calls, and the count line must
# be printed at all, so a run that stopped before judging anything is not a pass.
add_test(
    NAME "version-git-unanswered"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/version-git-unanswered"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-version-git-unanswered.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("version-git-unanswered" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "version-git-unanswered: [0-9]+ case\\(s\\) run"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Repository hygiene. Fails when a path that must never be committed is tracked
# by git. Today that is version.txt, whose committed presence outranked every git
# tag and pinned every build, every wire banner and every package to whatever it
# said. scripts/check-repository-hygiene.cmake carries the table of forbidden
# paths and the reason each one is forbidden, and prints the reason on failure.
#
# Deliberately NOT labelled "smoke": it starts no daemon, opens no socket and
# needs no compiler, so it belongs in the default `ctest` set and must survive
# `ctest -LE smoke`. It finishes in milliseconds.
#
# GIT_EXECUTABLE is located by cmake/Version.cmake and passed in rather than
# looked up again, so the check and the version resolution cannot disagree about
# which git they mean.
# Every tracked shell script carrying a shebang is mode 100755 (#720).
#
# `scripts/local-gate.sh` was 100644 while AGENT.md documents running it
# DIRECTLY, so the gate this project tells every contributor to run refused on a
# fresh checkout with `Permission denied`. Thirteen siblings shared the mode and
# did not care, because every one of them is registered here as
# `COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} <path>` -- so the interpreter is explicit and the
# bit is irrelevant. The one script with NO automated caller is the one that bit,
# and its not being run leaves no trace anywhere.
#
# Derived from the SHEBANG rather than a list of files, for #492's reason: a list
# is exact about what it knows and silent about what it does not. A sourced
# library has neither a shebang nor the bit and falls out of the rule instead of
# needing an exemption.
#
# It reads the INDEX, not the filesystem: the checked-in mode is what a fresh
# clone gets, and a local chmod that was never staged is not a fix.
# A workflow's script invocation agrees with that script's file mode (#723).
#
# A `run:` line naming `scripts/x.sh` either spells an interpreter or relies on the
# executable bit; relying on it while the file is 644 exits 126 on the runner, and
# that is a CI-ONLY failure a local run never reproduces, because a human types
# `bash` in front of the script by habit. It has happened twice, once inside a step
# whose `name:` is a REQUIRED context.
#
# The rule is AGREEMENT, not a spelling: `scripts/ci-scope.sh` is 755 and invoked
# bare, which is correct and must keep passing.
#
# `script-modes` beside it is not the same check -- that one asks whether a
# shebanged script carries the bit, this one asks whether a CALL SITE depends on it.
# A hygiene check that was KILLED and one that FOUND A PROBLEM are the same ctest
# `***Failed`, and the only tell is the absence of output nobody is looking for
# (#1079). `run-check.sh` is what tells them apart; these three are what keep it
# honest and keep it applied.
#
# The wrapper's own outcomes, driven against STAGED checks rather than waited for:
# a killed run is produced with `kill -PIPE`/`kill -KILL`, and the case with no
# terminal marker at all is produced by a staged check killing its own parent. That
# is deterministic and costs no sleep, which is what #1079 asks for -- stage the
# kill rather than wait to catch one.
add_test(
    NAME "run-check-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("run-check-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# And that every registered check actually goes through it. A check registered
# directly is valid CMake, correct in itself, and silently collapses the two states
# again -- so the set is DERIVED from the registrations rather than listed (#492).
add_test(
    NAME "run-check-coverage"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-run-check-coverage.sh"
)
set_tests_properties("run-check-coverage" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# Both directions, including the two shapes that make a coverage scan lie: a
# registration file naming NO check (which must refuse rather than report every
# check wrapped) and an unwrapped mention inside a COMMENT, which is not a call site.
add_test(
    NAME "run-check-coverage-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-run-check-coverage.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("run-check-coverage-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

add_test(
    NAME "workflow-script-invocations"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-workflow-script-invocations.sh"
)
set_tests_properties("workflow-script-invocations" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# Both directions plus the shapes that make a scan lie: a commented mention, a tree
# with no workflows, a tree with no invocations, and a bare call with NO word before
# it -- which an `IFS=$'\t' read` collapses, shifting the path out of its field and
# dropping the entry silently.
add_test(
    NAME "workflow-script-invocations-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-workflow-script-invocations.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("workflow-script-invocations-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# `scripts/flake-rate.sh` keeps a tally, and its own self-test proves it (#735).
#
# `ctest --repeat until-fail:N` reports the LAST iteration, so a 1% flake reads as
# `100% tests passed` -- measured, six consecutive green full-suite runs over a
# live flake, which a counting loop found on the first try. The tool used to HUNT
# flakes reports success in proportion to how rare the defect is.
#
# The cases stage a deterministic 1-in-5 failure, which is the one input where a
# tally and a last-read DISAGREE: over `true` or over `false` the two agree, so a
# self-test built on either would pass under the bug.
add_test(
    NAME "flake-rate-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/flake-rate.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("flake-rate-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# No workflow calls `apt-get update` directly (#550).
#
# The runner image ships `packages.microsoft.com` preinstalled, and `apt-get
# update` exits non-zero when ANY configured source is unreachable -- so a 403
# from a mirror nothing here uses failed a step that only wanted the Ubuntu
# archive, and read as an unrelated red check on whatever branch was building.
# Established as a flake by an A/B inside ONE run: two jobs with byte-identical
# install steps, same image, same moment, one 403.
#
# `scripts/ci-apt-update.sh` removes the unused sources FIRST and leaves an Ubuntu
# archive failure fatal -- ignoring errors afterwards is how a real archive outage
# becomes a silent one. This refuses a call site that skips the wrapper, which is
# what gives it a chance of surviving: eleven sites exist today and the twelfth is
# written by somebody who has never read this.
add_test(
    NAME "apt-update-wrapped"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-apt-update.sh"
)
set_tests_properties("apt-update-wrapped" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# Both directions, plus the two ways this scan could lie: a commented mention, and
# a tree that calls NEITHER -- which must refuse rather than report clean over a
# check that had stopped meaning anything.
# The macOS parse gate's own logic, watched refusing AND accepting (#1100).
#
# The GATE itself is a step of the macOS job and is deliberately not registered here: it
# parses with the interpreter it is RUN under, so on any host whose bash is 4.0 or newer
# it refuses by name rather than reporting a green it cannot justify. Registering it in
# the default set would make it red on every platform but one.
#
# The SELF-TEST runs anywhere, because it drives the checker's decisions rather than the
# 3.2 parser: the accepting arm, an unparseable script named and refused, the message
# qualifying its reported line as the point of FAILURE rather than of cause, the version
# gate refusing a modern bash, and an empty walk refused instead of reported clean.
#
# It also stages #1096's REAL construct -- an apostrophe inside a comment within `$( )` --
# and asserts it is ACCEPTED here, which is not a weaker case than it sounds: that
# construct parses on bash 4+, and that is exactly why the gate must run on 3.2 and why a
# green from a modern bash is worth nothing. The red on that file is observed on the
# macOS runner, which is the only real 3.2 in this project's world.
add_test(
    NAME "bash32-parse-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-bash32-parse.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("bash32-parse-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

add_test(
    NAME "apt-update-wrapped-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-apt-update.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("apt-update-wrapped-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# The SWEEP's own self-test, which is a different subject from the scan above: that one
# asserts every workflow goes THROUGH the wrapper, this one asserts the wrapper decides
# correctly about a source once it is reached.
#
# It was unregistered until #1160. Twelve cases existed and nothing ran any of them, so
# the allowlist shipped with the runner's actual layout untested and took every Linux job
# down at `Install build tools` -- the ticket's acceptance asks that something FAIL when a
# source is present and not removed, and a self-test no target invokes is not that
# something. `ctest -R apt-sweep-selftest`.
#
# `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77` is load-bearing rather than copied from its neighbours (#499):
# case 4 arranges guard one with a read-only directory, which does not bite as root or on
# a filesystem that ignores modes, and the run reports 77 when it could not be arranged.
# Without the property that is a false RED on exactly those runners -- and on Windows,
# where this suite runs and NTFS is one of them.
add_test(
    NAME "apt-sweep-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/ci-apt-update.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("apt-sweep-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# `mkdocs build --strict` must be able to fail the way two files promise it does
# (#560).
#
# mkdocs 1.6 defaults `validation.nav.omitted_files` to `info`, so a page left out
# of `nav:` was reported at INFO and the build SUCCEEDED -- while `mkdocs.yml` and
# `.github/workflows/docs.yml` both said `--strict` fails on exactly that. Two
# files stating the same wrong thing in almost the same sentence is how it stayed
# wrong for as long as it did.
#
# This asserts the PARSED configuration and not a grep, and it asserts the
# `not_in_nav` exemption too -- without that clause the validation fails the build
# on three snippets that are correctly absent from nav, which is how a validation
# flag gets turned back off by whoever meets it.
#
# It does not RUN mkdocs: that is the docs workflow's job and needs an install this
# suite cannot assume. What it guards is the configuration that makes that job able
# to fail at all. It SKIPs when PyYAML is absent rather than passing quietly.
add_test(
    NAME "mkdocs-validation"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-mkdocs-validation.sh"
)
set_tests_properties("mkdocs-validation" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "SKIP: "
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# A script offering `--self-test` must be REGISTERED to run it (#596).
#
# An unrun self-test is the most expensive green there is: the cases exist, they are
# written carefully, they are cited in review, and nothing says no target invokes them.
# `scripts/ci-apt-update.sh` grew twelve cases covering the decision that had just broken
# CI, no `add_test` named it, and the allowlist shipped with the runner's real layout
# untested -- taking every Linux job down at `Install build tools` (#1160).
#
# The rule is stricter than #596's literal wording, which asks only that the script
# "appear in an add_test COMMAND". The tree held the counterexample: at the time of
# writing `check-mkdocs-validation.sh` appeared in the table, in its plain mode, and its
# self-test had still never run. So the registration must PASS the flag.
add_test(
    NAME "selftest-registered"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-selftest-registered.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("selftest-registered" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And that check must be seen to refuse AND to accept -- eight synthetic trees, one
# verdict each.
#
# Both directions, because #596 asks for both and because they are different edits: a
# self-test ADDED without a registration, and a registration REMOVED from one that had
# it. The accepting direction is not decoration either (#1031): a guard that fails closed
# and unconditionally reads as "my branch is bad" rather than "the gate is broken", and
# can stand for days.
#
# Every case here has been shown to FAIL under a mutation of the check, one case per
# mutation, which is the only evidence that any of them constrains anything. That is not
# a formality: case 5 originally staged a PROSE mention of the flag, which matches no
# dispatch shape whether comments are skipped or not, so deleting the comment skip left
# it green -- a duplicate of case 6 wearing case 5's name. Reading it could not show
# that; only neutering the check did.
add_test(
    NAME "selftest-registered-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_CHECK=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-selftest-registered.cmake"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-selftest-registered-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("selftest-registered-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# The `.clang-format` row that keeps `<windows.h>` ahead of every other Windows SDK
# header (#1135) still does what it was added to do.
#
# The remedy for #1135 was a CONFIGURATION row, which makes the wrong ordering
# impossible rather than detected -- and the configuration is then the guard, with
# nothing watching the guard. A row can be deleted, renumbered, or overtaken by one
# added above it, and the only symptom is Windows CI going red at the Build step on a
# change that has nothing to do with it, which is exactly how #1135 arrived.
#
# **Not the scan that was rejected, and the difference decides the shape.** A scan over
# source files cannot work here: whether an SDK header tolerates being included first is
# per-header and not knowable from its name, so it would refuse the three correct files
# standing in the tree today and still admit tomorrow's. This asks whether the ROW sorts
# `<windows.h>` first, reads no source file, and has nothing to say about any of them.
#
# `smoke` and `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77` because it needs a real clang-format, which is the
# shape this repository already uses for an external prerequisite. It judges only with
# the declared formatter BUILD, resolved by `check-clang-format-version.sh --resolve`
# (#1349); a machine without that build SKIPS rather than judging with another, whose
# verdict would describe a tool nobody runs and be wrong in the reassuring direction.
add_test(
    NAME "windows-header-order"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-windows-header-order.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
)
set_tests_properties("windows-header-order" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene;smoke"
    SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# Both directions, because a guard nobody has watched refuse is not a guard and one
# nobody has watched accept is not known to work. The shipped configuration must ACCEPT,
# and a configuration with the row deleted -- restored to what master spelled before
# #1135, not merely the row absent -- must REFUSE. Without the second case this check
# passes with the row gone and reports nothing, which is the failure it exists to
# prevent, one level up.
add_test(
    NAME "windows-header-order-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-windows-header-order.sh" --self-test "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
)
set_tests_properties("windows-header-order-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene;smoke"
    SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# `.clang-format-version` is the ONE statement of the clang-format build: exactly one
# bare PyPI release banner, `clang-format version X.Y.Z`, whose major is the
# `CLANG_TOOLS_VERSION` build.yml pins (#1349, #1407). A second declared build is one the
# format-on-edit hook would write with, a snapshot banner names a build no installer can ask
# for, and a major bump that leaves the formatter behind is refused on the pull request that
# makes it. `Check C++ style` installs what `--requirement` derives from the file, so no
# workflow restates the version. Needs no formatter, so it is in the default set.
#
# Whether the INSTALLED formatter is the declared build is the script's `--installed`
# mode, and deliberately not a ctest: only `Check C++ style` installs the pinned
# formatter, so a registration would skip on every leg it ran on (#1135). That job runs it.
add_test(
    NAME "clang-format-version"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-clang-format-version.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
)
set_tests_properties("clang-format-version" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# Both directions, with fake formatters: a PLANTED major mismatch as the positive control,
# a second declared build, the snapshot / vendor / suffixed / short banners, the requirement
# derived and refused, the #1349 case itself -- the pinned release number from another
# source -- the resolver finding the declared build behind another clang-format, honouring
# and never stepping over PACE_CLANG_FORMAT, and naming the install commands (a
# pip-less host's included) when there is none; the shipped declaration accepts.
add_test(
    NAME "clang-format-version-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-clang-format-version.sh" --self-test "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
)
set_tests_properties("clang-format-version-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# `.clang-tidy-version` names the ONE clang-tidy build both tidy jobs and the local gate judge
# with -- an exact PyPI release, because apt's `clang-tidy-<major>` names a family of snapshots
# that print one banner and disagree about findings (#1404, #1198). This refuses a declaration
# that is not an exact release, names another package, or has a major other than
# `CLANG_TOOLS_VERSION`'s. Needs no analyser, so it is in the default set.
#
# Whether an INSTALLED analyser is that build is the `--installed` mode -- its wheel's METADATA
# version and the binary's sha256 against RECORD -- and deliberately not a ctest: only the two
# tidy jobs and the gate have the analyser, so a registration would skip everywhere (#1135).
add_test(

)
set_tests_properties("clang-tidy-version" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# Both directions over synthetic wheel layouts: the declared wheel accepted (and its Windows

# lists, the wheel's binary copied out of its tree, a distribution's analyser, nothing installed.
# Shown to bite: disarming the digest, version, layout, pin or no-fallback clause each reddens

    TIMEOUT 120
)

# A crash the declared clang-tidy build is KNOWN to have is a row of `check-clang-tidy-known-defects.sh` (#1410):
# the tree names the value first at every site of the crashing shape rather than disable the check. This
# default mode needs no analyser: every source line naming a row's check names that row's issue too, so the
# change that retires a row finds every site. A row is its own record, so its issue need not be open, and a
# table with no rows passes.

    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120

# The guard's own both-directions proof: a clean tree passes, a shebanged 644
# script is refused, an EMPTY scan is refused, and a tree whose only script has
# no shebang is refused. The last two matter because two empty lists agree
# perfectly -- a scan that matched nothing would otherwise report success.
add_test(
    NAME "script-modes-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-script-modes.sh" --self-test
