add_test(
    NAME "repository-hygiene"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-repository-hygiene.cmake"
)
# An exported source tarball has no git index to inspect, and the script reports
# that by printing `SKIP: ...` and exiting 0. SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION and not the
# SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77 the drivers below use, because a `cmake -P` script cannot
# choose its own exit code before CMake 3.29 (cmake_language(EXIT)) while this
# project supports 3.28 — a SKIP_RETURN_CODE it could never return would be
# decoration. See the script header. TIMEOUT 60 rather than CTest's 1500s
# default: a wedged git should report in a minute, not stall the suite.
set_tests_properties("repository-hygiene" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "SKIP: "
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# `.tsan-suppressions` says what it is for, and nothing checked it (#1266).
#
# The file's header states two rules -- every entry names the open issue it tracks,
# and removing an entry is part of fixing that issue -- and both were prose. The
# DATA has been on the gate's stdout the whole time: `tsan-gate.sh` prints TSan's
# `Matched N suppressions` header and the per-entry lines beneath it, and nothing
# anywhere read either. A suppression matching NOTHING is a rule that has stopped
# applying, and no total can show it: 5 matches, and 5 matches with a dead entry
# beside them, are the same number.
#
# This registration checks the ENTRY rules and reports the reconciliation as NOT RUN
# (`NO-DATA`), because no sanitizer log exists in an ordinary build. The gate itself
# calls the same script with `--log` once every target is green. On master the file
# holds no entries at all, so what this prints today is `NOTHING-TO-RECONCILE` --
# named rather than dressed up as a pass, since that is the state most easily
# mistaken for one.
add_test(
    NAME "tsan-suppressions"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tsan-suppressions.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
)
set_tests_properties("tsan-suppressions" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# Its self-test, and staging input is not a convenience: the tree supplies NONE of
# the four outcomes. `.tsan-suppressions` is empty, so a self-test run against the
# repository would exercise one arm of four and pass.
add_test(
    NAME "tsan-suppressions-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tsan-suppressions.sh" --self-test "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
)
set_tests_properties("tsan-suppressions-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# No tracked text file carries a C0 control byte other than TAB or LF (#595).
#
# #593 was one BEL in one shell script, from a `printf '\a'` that should have been
# `printf '\\a'`. What made it expensive is that nothing could have found it: it is
# not whitespace, so no formatter normalises it; not a comment introducer, so no
# comment stripper sees it; valid UTF-8, so the text gates pass it. The instance is
# gone and the class had no guard at all.
#
# In the default set for the reason test-name-hygiene gives: it reads tracked files
# and reports, needs no daemon, socket or compiler, and finishes in well under a
# second over 1,463 files.
add_test(
    NAME "control-bytes"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-control-bytes.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("control-bytes" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# Its self-test. The accepting direction is load-bearing here rather than decoration:
# this repository carries no control byte, so the check reports clean on the real tree
# and a check that had stopped reading files would look exactly the same. The cases
# that expect acceptance therefore assert the file COUNT.
add_test(
    NAME "control-bytes-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/control-bytes-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-control-bytes-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("control-bytes-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 300
)

# The four ways that check can fail to have an index, driven apart (#1565).
#
# Two of them printed different sentences and returned 0 from both, so ctest scored
# them identically -- and one of the two is a checkout whose `.git` this host cannot
# follow, where a skip means the check reported NOTHING about a tree it was pointed
# straight at. `git rev-parse` answers "not a git repository" to that and to an
# exported tarball alike, so the cases here arrange all five states and assert which
# sentence each one reached, never merely that it skipped.
add_test(
    NAME "repository-hygiene-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/repository-hygiene-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-repository-hygiene-selftest.cmake"
)
# No SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION: this one drives the skip paths as SUBJECTS, so its own
# output quotes `SKIP: ` and a skip marker here would score the driver as skipped on
# the very run in which it judged them.
set_tests_properties("repository-hygiene-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# A stall is retried; a verdict is not.
#
# `cmake/MacOSNotarizePkg.cmake` bounds `notarytool submit --wait` and retries it
# ([#376](https://github.com/LASTRADA-Software/pace/issues/376)). The property
# that needs a guard is not the timeout -- it is that Apple answering "Invalid" is an
# ANSWER, so retrying it spends half an hour of the step budget to be told the same
# thing. That condition is exactly what gets simplified away while making a retry
# "more robust", and nothing else in this tree would notice.
#
# Watched refusing: neutering the `_rc` separation fails the two refusal cases and
# ONLY those two, with the other three still green -- the asymmetry being the
# evidence rather than a red run.
#
# It drives the real function out of the real file with a fake `xcrun` on PATH, so
# it needs no macOS and no Apple credentials, and what it exercises is what ships.
# NOT on Windows, and for the SUBJECT rather than the mechanics. The fake `xcrun`
# is a POSIX shell stub and the hook spawns it through `execute_process`, which on
# Windows is `CreateProcess` and cannot start a shebang script -- the third time
# that ENOEXEC has been written into this tree in one day, twice by the session
# that added the rulebook entry about it.
#
# Staging a `.cmd` would fix the mechanics and would be the wrong fix:
# `MacOSNotarizePkg.cmake` is a macOS packaging hook that never runs on Windows,
# so validating it there would exercise a configuration that does not exist. The
# subject decides where a check belongs; the mechanics only decide whether it can
# run at all.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "notarize-retry"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-notarize-retry.sh"
    )
    set_tests_properties("notarize-retry" PROPERTIES
        FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
endif()

# `find_package(Python3)` and not a `find_program`: it VALIDATES the interpreter by
# running it, which is what screens out Windows' App Execution Alias -- a zero-byte
# reparse point on PATH named `python3.exe` that a `find_program` returns happily and
# that does not run Python. A third spelling of "find python" would also disagree with
# `cmake/Coverage.cmake`'s about which interpreter it means.
#
# Resolved HERE rather than at the first registration that needed it, because that
# registration is no longer the first: `tidy-blind-spots-table` below is. Every later
# consumer reuses this result.
find_package(Python3 COMPONENTS Interpreter QUIET)

# Which translation units the analyser never sees.
#
# The `clang-tidy` job runs on `ubuntu-24.04` and `scripts/local-gate.sh` tidies
# through the Linux presets, so a TU whose whole body sits behind a guard that is
# false on Linux preprocesses to nothing and is reported CLEAN without being read
# ([#682](https://github.com/LASTRADA-Software/pace/issues/682)).
#
# **Registered on Linux only, and that is about the SUBJECT rather than about
# portability.** The question is what the CI sweep cannot see, and that sweep runs on
# Linux. The same check on Windows would measure the mirror set -- the Iocp units
# analysed, the Epoll ones blind -- which is a different and currently unasked
# question. It is not a skip standing in for a pass; on other platforms there is
# nothing here to answer.
#
# It reads the compile database and the OBJECTS, so it runs after a build like
# `tsan-gate.sh` does. A missing `nm` is a refusal, never a skip.
# It also needs the SWEEP's configuration, not merely a Linux one. The empty-object
# signature is two symbols because AddressSanitizer adds them; without ASan an empty
# unit defines nothing and so does one that defines only inline or template entities,
# and the check would report both alike -- measured, 11 in the sweep's configuration
# against 26 in a Release build of the same tree. `PACE_ENABLE_TLS` matters for
# the same reason one level up: with TLS off, two TLS test units are legitimately
# empty and the blind set is a different set than the table records.
#
# In CI that configuration is the `clang-asan-ubsan` job, which configures
# `clang-debug` with TLS on and runs `ctest` -- the same configuration the `clang-tidy`
# job sweeps, which is what makes its answer the one the sweep would give.
#
# That residual -- if the job's flags change, this stops being registered and reports
# nothing, which reads like a pass -- is #589, and it is closed by two checks rather
# than one, because it was two claims:
#
#   * `conditional-check-reach` derives the option-gated registrations in this file
#     from the `if()` nesting around them and asks which `build.yml` job configures a
#     build satisfying each one AND runs `ctest`. `tidy-blind-spots` is a row of its
#     derived set, not a name written into it, so a flag change here or there refuses.
#   * `tidy-blind-spots-table` below runs the half of this check that needs no build,
#     everywhere, in the default set. A compiler is what the MEASUREMENT needs; the
#     table's own rules are properties of two text files and the workflow, and gating
#     those behind an ASan Linux build asserted them on one configuration and on none
#     of the others.
#
# `--python3` is not passed here on purpose: this registration is Linux-only under a
# configuration that has `python3` on PATH by construction, and the flag exists for the
# registration below, which runs on Windows too.
if(CMAKE_SYSTEM_NAME STREQUAL "Linux" AND ENABLE_SANITIZER_ADDRESS AND PACE_ENABLE_TLS)
    add_test(
        NAME "tidy-blind-spots"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tidy-blind-spots.sh"
                "${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}"
    )
    set_tests_properties("tidy-blind-spots" PROPERTIES
        FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 300
    )
endif()

# The half that makes the above a guard rather than a hope: eight staged outcomes,
# each asserted for WHICH refusal it gets. Needs no build and no real `nm` -- it
# stages a fake one.
#
# NOT on Windows, and for the subject rather than for convenience. The fake `nm` is a
# POSIX shell stub, and the check spawns it through Python, which on Windows is
# `CreateProcess` and cannot start a shebang script -- the same ENOEXEC that broke
# #668's gate selftest. Staging a `.cmd` would fix the mechanics, but it would be
# validating a check that can never run on this platform: `tidy-blind-spots` is
# registered on Linux only, because the question is what the Linux sweep cannot see.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "tidy-blind-spots-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tidy-blind-spots-selftest.sh"
    )
    set_tests_properties("tidy-blind-spots-selftest" PROPERTIES
        FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
endif()

# The table's own rules, with no build (#589).
#
# `tidy-blind-spots` above needs a compile database, the objects and `nm`, so it is
# registered on Linux with ASan and TLS on and NOWHERE else. Two of its rules need
# none of that: every reached-by column must name a clang-tidy leg that still exists
# in `.github/workflows/build.yml`, and every row -- a `none` row above all, where the
# reason is the only account of why nothing reads the unit -- must carry a reason.
# Those were enforced on exactly one configuration and on no other, so a row naming a
# renamed leg, or a reason deleted in a tidy-up, went unremarked everywhere the
# measuring registration does not exist.
#
# Registered UNCONDITIONALLY, which is the point rather than a convenience: the legs
# come from the workflow and the rows from a text file, and neither is a property of
# the host. A platform guard here would reintroduce the hole one level down.
#
# It does not measure, and it says so in its own verdict line. A mode that names its
# set may not report clean over a member it could not cover -- `all accounted for in
# scripts/tidy-blind-spots.txt` is a claim about the OBJECTS, and a run that opened
# none must not borrow it.
if(NOT Python3_Interpreter_FOUND)
    # Absent is not passing, and a WARNING alone is written once into a log nobody
    # reads twice while the RUN contains no row at all (#1570). The honest skip keeps
    # `ctest -N` returning the same total with and without a python3 on PATH.
    message(WARNING
        "tidy-blind-spots-table: no Python 3 interpreter found, so the blind-spot table's "
        "own rules (#589) are NOT guarded in this build tree")
    pace_register_skipped_test("tidy-blind-spots-table"
        "no Python 3 interpreter found, so the blind-spot table's own rules (#589) are NOT guarded in this build tree")
else()
    add_test(
        NAME "tidy-blind-spots-table"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tidy-blind-spots.sh"
                --table-only --python3 "${Python3_EXECUTABLE}"
    )
    set_tests_properties("tidy-blind-spots-table" PROPERTIES
        FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The startup diagnostics `docs/operations/corrupt-store.md` quotes VERBATIM are
# still composable by the source that composes them (#633).
#
# That page exists so an operator meeting `Corrupt` does NOT delete a healthy
# cache, and it only works if they recognise their own console output in it.
# Measured when #633 was filed: of the diagnostics it quotes, exactly one was
# asserted anywhere -- `CowTreeStorage_test.cpp` compares the `StorageError(...)`
# rendering, and `grep -rn "failed to open" --include=*_test.cpp src/` returned
# nothing at all.
#
# A unit test cannot reach the other two. The daemon's `StorageOpenFailure` is in
# an ANONYMOUS NAMESPACE in `main.cpp`, behind `add_executable(pace-kv
# main.cpp)` with no test target; the node's sentence is composed in a third
# file in a different binary. A check that READS BOTH SIDES needs no linkage,
# which is the STORE lane's recommendation and the reason this is a check rather
# than a test.
#
# It asserts PRESENCE in the composing source, never equality with a formatted
# line -- #633 is explicit that a whole-line assertion is the change-detector it
# exists to avoid. The one exception is the `StorageError(...)` rendering, where
# the whole format string IS the page's verbatim quote, so a change-detector is
# exactly what it needs.
#
# Labelled `docs-subject`, so `scripts/doc-subject-checks.sh` runs it on
# documentation-only pull requests -- the change these exist to catch and the one
# they used to be skipped on (#687). The runner derives its set from this label,
# so adding this row is the whole wiring.
add_test(
    NAME "corrupt-store-diagnostics"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-corrupt-store-diagnostics.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("corrupt-store-diagnostics" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene;docs-subject"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And the half that makes it a guard. Nine cases: a baseline, a false-positive
# guard proving that rewording the prose AROUND a quote is free, five refusals on
# the fields, one on a quoted diagnostic no fragment row covers, and one on a
# page that quotes nothing at all.
#
# Each has been WATCHED to fail. Removing the uncovered-diagnostic refusal reddens
# that case alone; disabling the vacuity refusal reddens its own; making the
# per-fragment literal assertion a no-op reddens four -- and deliberately NOT the
# `what`-argument case, which goes through a separate loop, so the two are shown
# to be independent.
#
# Its harness distinguishes INCONCLUSIVE from a verdict: a spawn that produced no
# output and a non-zero status could not run, and is reported as itself rather
# than as a rule regression. That is #747's subject, and this harness is born with
# the state rather than becoming a third instance of the defect.
add_test(
    NAME "corrupt-store-diagnostics-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/corrupt-store-diagnostics-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-corrupt-store-diagnostics-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("corrupt-store-diagnostics-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Test-name hygiene. Fails when a Catch2 case is named something the CTest
# registration cannot hand back to the runner.
#
# `catch_discover_tests` registers each case as `add_test(NAME <case> COMMAND
# <exe> <case>)`, so the name arrives at Catch2 as a command-line ARGUMENT and a
# leading `-` is read as an option rather than as a test spec. A recognised one
# makes CTest report a pass for a case that never ran; an unrecognised one makes
# it report a failure the case does not have, which then passes when run by hand.
# Both were live in this tree, and the first had been silently not running since
# the day it was written.
#
# Not labelled "smoke", for the reason the two above are not: it reads source
# files and reports, so it belongs in the default `ctest` set and must survive
# `ctest -LE smoke`. It has no skip path -- the source tree is present in an
# exported tarball exactly as it is in a checkout.
#
# The timeout is 120 rather than 60 because #1261 widened its glob from
# `src/*_test.cpp` to every C++ source under `src/`: it now READS about four times
# as many files, and walks the newlines of only the ~318 that hold a candidate. The
# walk is what costs, so the measured move is small (3.4 s to 4.2 s here), but the
# read count tripled and these are the file counts a Windows checkout is slowest at.
add_test(
    NAME "test-name-hygiene"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-test-names.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("test-name-hygiene" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# The check above, driven against staged trees in both directions (#1261).
#
# It had no self-test, and three holes sat in it: a case whose name wrapped to the
# next line left the reader entirely, a case in a file not named `*_test.cpp` was
# never globbed, and two of Catch2's case-declaring macros were missing from a
# pattern whose own comment claimed completeness. None of them lowers a number
# anybody compares -- a case that is never seen cannot -- so all three reported
# clean. The accepting cases here assert the case COUNT for that reason.
add_test(
    NAME "test-name-hygiene-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/test-name-hygiene-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-test-names-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("test-name-hygiene-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 300
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The tests' loops: no C-style `for`, and no `while` that polls an atomic (#1446).
#
# The first is a rule `AGENT.md` has always stated and 76 test files still broke. The
# second is the ticket's subject: a test waiting on another thread through a polled atomic
# either counts iterations, which a loaded host loses, or is unbounded, which turns a red
# into a hang -- and the tree's one test wait is `src/tests/BoundedWait.hpp`. The scope is
# a table row in the script, so #1452 widens it to non-test sources by adding one.
#
# In the default set, for the reason test-name-hygiene gives above. The timeout is wider:
# the comment-stripping walk reads every test source, measured at 16 s on Windows.
add_test(
    NAME "test-loops"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-test-loops.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("test-loops" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
)

# And that check must be SEEN to refuse each shape it names and to stay quiet over the ones
# it does not: synthetic trees in both enumeration modes and nested inside another checkout
# (how this registration stages them, under the build directory), exact `file:line`s,
# exemptions in both directions, and the two fail-closed arms. It prints how many cases ran.
# Each of seven neuters of the check was watched turning a named case red.
add_test(
    NAME "test-loops-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/test-loops-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-test-loops-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("test-loops-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    # 600 rather than 120, and the number comes from a measurement rather than from a red run.
    # Fifty cases, each staging a tree and spawning a `cmake -P`:
    #
    #     ext4 (/tmp, and every CI runner)                    1179 ms
    #     DrvFs (a build tree on /mnt/d reached from WSL)     23667 ms
    #
    # Twenty times, and the scratch is `${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}` -- the portable choice --
    # so on a DrvFs build tree this starts from 23.7 s. Under `ctest --parallel 32` that crossed
    # 120 s: `***Timeout 120.34 sec`, twice, the second time on an idle machine, so it was not
    # contention. Measured NOT to be #1452's doing either: 23667 ms with that change and
    # 23699 ms without it, same worktree, only the scripts stashed.
    #
    # A budget catches a HANG, and a hang is unbounded -- 600 s catches every one 120 s did.
    # Policing COST is not its job, and relocating the scratch to a fast path is the wrong trade:
    # the self-test `git init`s its own outer repository so it COULD live anywhere, but /tmp is
    # not a path the native-Windows legs have, and the slowness belongs to one machine's layout
    # rather than to the test.
    #
    # It grows: the cost is O(cases x exemption rows), because one file per exemption row is
    # staged in EVERY tree so each case re-proves the rows still match. A sixth exemption row
    # slows all fifty cases.
    TIMEOUT 600
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A test file does not define its own `IMembershipOracle` -- the fakes are shared (#1497).
#
# Six test files carried their own copy of one of two membership fakes, four byte-identical.
# A duplicated FAKE is worse than duplicated production code because its bugs point the wrong
# way: a fake that is wrong makes its cases PASS, so an assertion review finds nothing and a
# green suite says nothing. And the cost is the number of facts each copy must get right, which
# #1471 moved from zero to one -- each copy now names which admission route it stands for.
#
# In the default set for the reason test-name-hygiene gives above: it needs no daemon, no
# socket and no compiler. The timeout is test-loops' 180 for the same reason -- it reads the
# same file set. Measured at 7346 ms over 284 test files (WSL, DrvFs worktree on /mnt/d), where
# the cost is the reads rather than the pattern: a whole-file filter means only the files that
# mention the interface are comment-stripped.
add_test(
    NAME "membership-fakes"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-membership-fakes.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("membership-fakes" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
)

# And that check must be SEEN to refuse and to ACCEPT, because a guard nobody has watched
# accept is not known to work either. Ten staged trees: a duplicated fake plain and WRAPPED
# across lines (the shape a line-based reader fails open on), a mention in a comment and a
# parameter of the interface type both staying quiet, the check refusing when its own pattern
# finds no shared fake in the header it points everybody at, refusing an enumeration that
# selected nothing, and an exemption row honoured in one tree and refused as stale in another.
# Both enumeration modes are exercised and asserted per case. It prints how many cases ran.
add_test(
    NAME "membership-fakes-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/membership-fakes-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-membership-fakes-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("membership-fakes-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    # The default 120 s, and the number comes from a measurement rather than from a red run.
    # Ten cases, each staging a tree, a `git init` and a `cmake -P`:
    #
    #     ext4 (/tmp, and every CI runner)                     412 ms
    #     DrvFs (a build tree on /mnt/d reached from WSL)      2995 ms
    #
    # Seven times apart, and the scratch is `${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}`, so a DrvFs build tree
    # starts from 3.0 s -- two orders inside the budget even under `ctest --parallel 32`. These
    # conditions are PINNED rather than pointed at a source: they are the world at one instant,
    # and a figure that tracked its subject would silently re-attribute a real measurement to
    # conditions it was never taken under. Unlike test-loops-selftest this does not grow with the
    # exemption table -- the rows are staged into a COPY of the check, one tree per case.
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# `std::random_device` is not a uniqueness source, and a temp path may not bet on it.
#
# #1507: `StorageTestUtils.hpp`'s `TempFile` named its file from
# `std::mt19937_64 { std::random_device {}() }()`, and on the host it was reproduced on that
# device answers ZERO for 57% of draws -- so 31 of 32 concurrent processes agreed on one
# filename and the second opener was refused `InUse` by a `flock` doing its job. The symptom
# named the STORAGE layer; the defect was four lines in a test helper, and four more copies of
# the idiom had already spread.
#
# The unique-path helper has been written six times. Five used a counter, which the rulebook
# already warns about; this one used something that merely LOOKS stronger, so none of that
# reasoning applied to it. Nothing in the type system makes a test reach for the seam, so the
# guard is a scan.
add_test(
    NAME "unique-temp-paths"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-unique-temp-paths.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("unique-temp-paths" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
)

# And in BOTH directions, because the accepting half is the one this check is likely to lose:
# the four files its rule was written for now DISCUSS `std::random_device` in prose, so a reader
# that counted comments would refuse the tree it had just fixed. Seven staged trees: a clean
# one, a real draw, the same words in a comment, an exemption row honoured and the same row
# refused as stale, a missing seam, and a seam that has stopped reading the device -- the last
# being the positive anchor, without which a clean report would mean only that nothing matched.
# Each exemption case asserts it was judged by the STAGED check. It prints how many cases ran.
add_test(
    NAME "unique-temp-paths-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/unique-temp-paths-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-unique-temp-paths-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("unique-temp-paths-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Separate PROCESSES draw separate handshake nonces (#1527).
#
# Nonces were drawn from an engine seeded once per process from `std::random_device`, which
# on the host #1507 was measured on answers zero for 57% of draws -- and a process seeded zero
# draws the same stream as every other one, while never repeating within itself. So no
# in-process case can see the defect, and this asks processes: `secure-random-probe` is run
# eight times and a nonce seen twice is refused. `scripts/secure-random-gate.cmake` first runs
# the pre-#1527 construction seeded zero in two processes and requires the SAME detector to
# fire, because a detector nobody has watched refuse proves nothing by staying quiet.
#
# Beside the check above because both answer #1507's measurement: that one for temp-path
# names, this one for nonces.
# Not guarded to Debug, unlike the canary gates it is shaped like: nothing here is an
# `assert`, and the generator a Release build ships is the one worth asking.
add_executable(secure-random-probe SecureRandomProbe.cpp)
target_link_libraries(secure-random-probe PRIVATE Pace)
add_test(
    NAME "secure-random-cross-process"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_PROBE=$<TARGET_FILE:secure-random-probe>"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/secure-random-gate.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("secure-random-cross-process" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A test may not decide anything from the architecture it was COMPILED for.
#
# `#if defined(_M_ARM64)` in a test picks an expected value from a fact about the
# BUILD, while the implementation picks its answer from the same fact -- so both
# flip together and the case is green under every build, unable to fail for the
# reason it exists. That is #146: the MSVC bindir chosen by the architecture the
# binary was compiled for rather than the machine it runs on, with a test pointed
# straight at it that could not have caught it.
#
# Scoped to architecture macros and NOT to `_WIN32`, and the distinction is the
# whole reason this check can exist: a Windows build runs on Windows, so an
# operating-system condition is a fact and a per-platform literal is sound, while
# an x64 build runs on ARM64 Windows routinely, so an architecture condition is a
# PROXY. Measured when this landed: 35 of 215 test files carry a platform or
# architecture directive and 33 are the legitimate shape, so a scan over platform
# directives generally would refuse 33 correct files -- and a guard that refuses
# correct code gets disabled, taking the real finding with it. The script's header
# carries the full reasoning; do not widen it.
#
# Default set rather than "smoke": it reads source files, needs no daemon, socket
# or compiler, and has no skip path.
#
# **50 ms, and the figure carries its conditions** because they differ by 168x.
# Measured on one machine, one afternoon, same check and same 215 files: 0.050 s
# on ext4, 8.4 s idle on a DrvFs checkout under WSL, 11.9 s there at load average
# 27, and one outright TIMEOUT at 60 s while a full gate ran alongside it. The
# cost is the filesystem's per-file open, not this check -- a whole-file filter
# before the line read, the optimisation that took `worker-refusals-counted` from
# 2.9 s to 208 ms, was tried here and moved nothing (8.385 s against 8.420 s),
# because there is no line-matching cost to remove. So a slow local run is the
# checkout, and anyone meeting one should not optimise a 50 ms check or raise this
# timeout to silence it.
#
# **No `SKIP_RETURN_CODE` here, and its absence is load-bearing.** It reads as a
# redundant observation until #1128: a status this registration does not claim is
# a status CTest is free to reinterpret. On Catch2 3.6.0 the exit code was the
# failed-assertion count, so a case failing exactly four assertions exited 4, collided
# with the `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 4` the Catch2 registrations must carry, and ctest reported
# `100% tests passed` over a red binary. This check exits 1 on a refusal, claims
# no skip code, and has no skip path to want one -- so its refusal cannot be
# silently rescored into a skip. Verified through the real harness rather than
# read off the idiom: with a violation planted, ctest scores it **Failed**.
add_test(
    NAME "architecture-conditioned-tests"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DSOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/src"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-architecture-conditioned-tests.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("architecture-conditioned-tests" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Byte-order qualifier hygiene. Fails when a source file writes `::htonl(...)`
# or any of its siblings.
#
# Those names are function-like macros on every platform this project builds on.
# The difference is what they expand to: on glibc a bare identifier, so the `::`
# has something to attach to and the line compiles; on Darwin an expression, so
# it does not and clang reports `expected unqualified-id`. The rule is therefore
# flat and lexical rather than platform-aware -- see the script's header, which
# corrects an earlier explanation in this tree that had it as "macros on macOS,
# functions on glibc" and would have made this check need to read `#if` guards.
#
# It has happened three times (#469), the second and third hours apart in
# different lanes, each costing a CI cycle on an otherwise-green branch. Nothing
# but CI's macOS leg can see it: local builds compile it, and clang-tidy reads
# the files it lands in as EMPTY translation units because they sit behind
# `#if defined(__APPLE__)` or `#if defined(_WIN32)` (#466). The rule was already
# a comment three lines above the third occurrence, which is why it is a check.
#
# Not labelled "smoke", for the reason the checks above are not: it reads source
# files and reports, so it belongs in the default `ctest` set and must survive
# `ctest -LE smoke`. It starts nothing.
#
# WHAT IT COSTS IS A PROPERTY OF THE FILESYSTEM, NOT OF THIS CHECK, so no number
# lives here (#665). This comment used to say it "finishes in well under a
# second", which is true on ext4 and on CI and is two orders of magnitude wrong on
# a 9p checkout, where it has been measured at 60.9 s against this 60 s budget --
# and only ever exceeds it while a SECOND lane's gate runs, which is ordinary
# practice here. A reader meeting a timeout was then told by the most confident
# statement in the tree that the duration they had just observed was impossible,
# and went looking at the machine.
#
# The conditions and the figures are one table, in
# `.agent/rules/build-and-toolchain.md` under "Read that figure with its
# conditions". Point at it; never copy a number out of it, which is what produced
# three live figures for one check in #479 and this comment in #665.
add_test(
    NAME "byte-order-qualifier"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-byte-order-qualifier.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("byte-order-qualifier" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# `VSLANG` is set on a probe spawn and on nothing else.
#
# The launcher asks `cl` to speak English where it PARSES the answer: the
# /showIncludes notes (#692) and the version banner, which is the compiler's
# identity (#200). Both of those spawns are read by nobody but this process. The
# real compile is different in kind -- its diagnostics belong to the developer,
# and forcing English there repairs a parse by anglicizing every warning they see.
#
# The rule was written in `.agent/rules/compile-cache.md` and on both entry
# points, and NOTHING READ IT: every test asserted that the variable reaches a
# probe, which a change that also set it on the compile passes unchanged. That is
# the #683 shape -- a plausible claim nothing can make fail -- so the census is
# the reader.
add_test(
    NAME "vslang-probe-only"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-vslang-probe-only.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("vslang-probe-only" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# `PEDANTIC_COMPILER_WERROR` decides whether warnings are FATAL, and may not
# decide which warnings EXIST.
#
# `-pedantic` was added under `PEDANTIC_COMPILER` while the suppressions it makes
# necessary sat inside the `PEDANTIC_COMPILER_WERROR` block, so the four presets
# that inherit `PEDANTIC_COMPILER=ON` from `base` and leave `WERROR` off --
# clang-coverage, clang-asan-ubsan, clang-tsan, clang-tracy -- got the warnings
# and none of the suppressions: 7049 `-Wc2y-extensions` each, measured (#611).
#
# Nothing could have reported it. Those presets have `WERROR` off, so the builds
# exit 0; the preset people actually run has `WERROR` on and is therefore correct;
# and the one place it becomes visible is a clang-tidy database from a build
# directory configured that way, where it reads as a stale cache rather than as a
# rule -- which is how #454 came to be believed unreproducible.
#
# The check includes `cmake/portable/PedanticCompiler.cmake` at both `WERROR`
# settings and refuses any difference that is not purely about fatality. Driving
# the file rather than scanning it, because the defect was never a spelling: it was
# which `if()` a line sat inside, and a hand-written model of CMake's nesting would
# fail in the confident direction.
add_test(
    NAME "pedantic-suppressions"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-pedantic-suppressions.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("pedantic-suppressions" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And the half that proves the above can refuse. It was watched failing on
# 0db96dc8, naming exactly the four suppressions once per GNU-style persona -- but
# that is a fact about one commit and stops reproducing the moment the fix lands.
# This runs fourteen cases instead -- twelve synthetic subjects plus two absent
# inputs -- and names each one on every run, because a stated figure that disagrees
# with what the tool prints is the defect #723 exists to prevent. Four are shapes an
# unwatched guard accepts: the suppression COPIED outside rather than moved, one
# added outside every condition (in four spellings: `-Wno-X`, `-wdN`, `/w`, `-w`), a
# subject that no longer adds `-pedantic`, and an MSVC arm that adds nothing.
#
# The copied one is why the check compares flag COUNTS rather than membership: a
# suppression added outside AND left inside is present at both settings, so a set
# difference is empty and it passes, while the refusal it would have shown says
# "move the flag, do not copy it". A guard that reads as enforcing something it
# cannot is worse than no guard.
#
# `PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED` is handed over rather than respelled in the
# script, so the selftest judges a refusal by the same pattern ctest does. A second
# spelling of it could drift from the property that actually decides these tests,
# and this file would then certify a verdict nobody reads.
add_test(
    NAME "pedantic-suppressions-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SELFTEST_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/pedantic-suppressions-selftest"
        "-DPACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED=${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-pedantic-suppressions-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("pedantic-suppressions-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The shared e2e helper library. Fails when `scripts/lib/e2e-common.sh` stops
# drawing ports the way it says, stops distinguishing a dead process from a slow
# one, stops keeping an HTTP body's last chunk, or stops ending the run from
# inside a subshell.
#
# It exists because that file is a shared fake in the sense
# `src/tests/ScriptedSocket.hpp` records: seven fixtures now depend on it, so a
# defect in it is a defect in all of them at once, and #449 is precisely the
# ticket about a fixture passing for a reason unrelated to what it guards. A
# helper library with no test that can go red would be that failure one level up.
#
# Not labelled "smoke": it starts no daemon, needs no compiler and binds nothing
# but the loopback listener it stages for itself, so it belongs in the default
# `ctest` set where whoever edits the helpers is the one who sees it break.
#
# Not RUN on Windows -- registered there as a named skip -- for the reason `local-gate-selftest`
# and `ci-scope` carry a guard -- the library serves POSIX fixtures and is POSIX itself. Measured
# rather than assumed, and worth recording: on msys2 bash a `/dev/tcp` connect to
# an unbound loopback port can SUCCEED, so the socket cases there report a wait
# of 0s against a listener that binds a second later. The same cases read 1s on
# Linux. A green run on Windows would be the wrong kind of green.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "e2e-helpers-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-e2e-helpers.sh"
    )
    # Neither guard against a background helper running the fixture's cleanup may be
    # removed (#1084).
    #
    # A background subshell inherits the shell's traps, and in a fixture the EXIT trap IS
    # the cleanup -- so a helper signalled catchably `rm -rf`s the workdir the caller is
    # still using. It presented as `node-ready-waits-for-marker` failing about a third of
    # the time with `cannot arm a 15s deadline: not a directory`, which names no trap, no
    # timer and no cleanup.
    #
    # The fix is TWO guards for TWO windows and only the second is load-bearing, so the
    # first looks redundant and is what a reader deletes: measured interleaved and
    # order-alternated, `trap -` ALONE is 10 pass / 2 fail against an unfixed 9 / 3, while
    # `kill -KILL` is 12 / 0 against 7 / 5. This asserts both are still there.
    #
    # It is a PRESENCE scan and not a behavioural test, stated rather than glossed: the
    # inheritance is version-dependent, and on bash 5.2 a background subshell runs no
    # inherited EXIT trap at all, so the shape cannot be reproduced on any Linux or
    # Windows host here. macOS's 3.2 is where it lives. Both guards were shown red by
    # removing each from the real library in turn.
    add_test(
        NAME "fixture-cleanup-guards"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-fixture-cleanup-guards.sh"
    )
    set_tests_properties("fixture-cleanup-guards" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
    add_test(
        NAME "fixture-cleanup-guards-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-fixture-cleanup-guards.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("fixture-cleanup-guards-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
    # It stages several deliberate timeouts of a couple of seconds each, one
    # four-second wait that must really take four seconds -- the check that the
    # bound comes from a clock rather than from an iteration count -- and one
    # four-second staged DEFECT, which is `bounded-fast-path-bites` proving that
    # the fast-path ceiling still separates a healthy `run_bounded` from a broken
    # one on the machine actually running it. Bounded so a helper that stops being
    # bounded reports here rather than as a suite timeout naming nothing.
    #
    # MEASURED, so that a timeout here is diagnosable rather than a guess (#678
    # was reported as one failure in four runs with no output captured, and a
    # ctest timeout and a failed assertion are indistinguishable from the count
    # alone). On a 32-core Linux host, local ext4:
    #
    #     1-min load 5.04 -> 1.90     37.2s / 37.0s / 37.0s   (three runs, 172 checks)
    #     1-min load 1.65 -> 7.42     40.6-41.1s              (six runs,   174 checks)
    #     1-min load 55.0 -> 57.8     42.9-44.3s              (four runs,  174 checks)
    #
    # The check COUNT is part of each row for the reason the load is: 172 and 174 are
    # different trees, and 37s against 41s is mostly that difference rather than the
    # host. A total is comparable only against the same tree.
    #
    # The stray-listener floor was `pgrep -x perl` = 0 at BOTH ends of both batches,
    # so these readings are not sitting on accumulated orphans from earlier runs --
    # `pgrep -x`, never `pgrep -f`, which matches the shell running the census and
    # reports a non-zero floor on a host with no perl at all.
    #
    # **The load is part of the reading, and an earlier version of this block got
    # that wrong in the direction that matters.** It recorded the same shape as
    # `idle 40s (37.2 / 38.9 / 40.1)` and the runs were never verified idle -- this
    # host's 15-minute average was 47.51 at the time, with other lanes gating
    # throughout. A loaded figure wearing an idle label is worse than no figure,
    # because the conclusion drawn from it was *contention barely moves this*, which
    # is exactly what you cannot conclude by comparing a loaded number to a loaded
    # number while calling one of them quiet. The row above states the load it was
    # taken at because that is the only form in which it can be checked.
    #
    # **A loaded band IS measured now** -- the previous version of this block said
    # there was none, deliberately, and invited whoever landed last to add one from
    # the WORST band rather than the best. This is that lane. The two rows above are
    # the same tree at two load levels, and the headroom below is taken from the
    # slowest reading of the slower band, not from the typical.
    #
    # `check-e2e-helpers.sh` was contended by three lanes while this was written and
    # each cost stays separately attributed, because a single total cannot be
    # re-derived when the next case arrives:
    #
    #   * #824  ~6s  `http-silence-inconclusive` stages a listener that accepts and
    #                then neither speaks nor closes, so it costs `_http_drain_fd3`'s
    #                whole `read -t` bound -- that expiry is the thing it asserts on.
    #   * #678  ~4s  `bounded-fast-path-bites` stages the flat-pause defect, which is
    #                `FastPathCommands` polls of `_e2e_poll_pause`.
    #
    # Both by CONSTRUCTION rather than by measurement: each is a bound the case
    # deliberately spends, so the figure follows from the constant and does not need
    # re-timing when the host changes.
    #
    # So the cost is almost entirely the deliberate SLEEPS rather than CPU work,
    # which is the REASON to expect contention to move it little -- an argument from
    # what the run spends its time on, and one the loaded row now supports rather
    # than merely asserting: eight times the load average cost about 3s. 2.7x
    # headroom against this bound at the worst reading of 44.3s -- taken from the
    # slowest run of the loaded band, not the typical of the quiet one; a run
    # approaching 120s means something other than a busy machine. A figure
    # here rather than "well inside": #665 is the same shape, a registration's
    # stated cost against its measured one.
    set_tests_properties("e2e-helpers-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
else()
    # REGISTERED and skipped, never omitted (#1355): absent is not skipped. A Windows run had
    # no row for this at all, and the ticket that found it named the test anyway -- from a
    # run by hand -- which is the absence reading as a result. The reason is the measured one
    # above, so the skip says what a green here would have meant.
    pace_register_skipped_test("e2e-helpers-selftest"
        "the helper library is POSIX and serves POSIX fixtures; on msys2 bash a /dev/tcp connect to an unbound loopback port can SUCCEED, so its socket cases would pass against a listener that is not there")
endif()

# Every fixture handed a launcher -- `$<TARGET_FILE:pace-cc>` on its registration, or
# a launcher parameter it declares -- calls the launcher-state seam, so the launcher
# records into the run's own state directory rather than the developer's. The Windows
# launcher e2e deleted the developer's statistics (`-z`) on every run, and three fixtures
# appended their records to them.
#
# On EVERY platform, and deliberately not a case of `e2e-helpers-selftest`, which is not
# run on Windows: the `.ps1` fixtures this is mostly about run nowhere else. Neither needs
# a build. TWO registrations of one script, because they answer two questions:
# `launcher-state-isolation` scans the REAL tree and holds the two readers of Stats.cpp's
# state variables -- the bash one and the PowerShell one -- to one answer per platform;
# `launcher-state-isolation-selftest` is the check's proof that it can fail -- a planted
# tree that must earn every verdict -- and DRIVES both seams against a stand-in launcher
# and a caller log that is present, empty and absent. The PowerShell halves need pwsh and
# are named skips without it.
#
# Their cost is PROCESS SPAWNS, which Git Bash makes expensive: a version that ran a `grep`
# per CMake file and each seam case one after another took 37-47s on an idle Windows host
# and 114-175s with 24 busy loops on its 32 cores. One `awk`/`grep` per pass over all the
# files, the seam cases in lanes and one reader subshell brought the whole to 11s idle and
# 22-29s under the same load (Git Bash 5.2); a reviewer then measured 182s at 32 busy
# loops, and the split is the answer to that rather than a larger bound. Judging every
# `.sh` fixture in ONE awk rather than a handful of processes each then measured 21s for
# the self-test and 8s for the tree under 32 busy loops with 2-4 other compilers running
# (39.8s and 11.9s before it). With the other lanes' builds on top -- up to ~30 compilers
# as well as the loops -- the self-test measured 80-126s, AT its bound, before and after:
# on a host that saturated, every Git Bash spawn costs an order of magnitude more, and the
# self-test is hundreds of them. That is harsher than any real run. The load that DECIDES
# is the gate's own shape, `ctest -L hygiene -j32` beside it on the same 32-CPU host, and
# there the self-test measured up to 83s while its seam cases cost ~1,170 processes. With
# the seam library cut to ~830, five such runs on Windows measured the self-test at
# 14-48s and the tree at 7-20s, started anywhere from 79th to 173rd of 202 and with 0 to
# 34 other lanes' compilers running; in WSL 2s (the pwsh half a named skip) and 10s.
# Each bound is for a check that stops ending; if a run approaches it, make the check
# cheaper again rather than raising it.
add_test(
    NAME "launcher-state-isolation"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-launcher-state-isolation.sh"
)
set_tests_properties("launcher-state-isolation" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)
add_test(
    NAME "launcher-state-isolation-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-launcher-state-isolation.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("launcher-state-isolation-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The ELF guard that keeps a vendored library out of what ships (#1538).
#
# The guard itself runs in the `Package (Linux .deb/.rpm)` job, over the binaries that
# job is about to package -- there is no ELF here to point it at and no reason to build
# one. What is registered is its DECISION, which is a pure function over a dynamic-section
# dump and is driven in both directions plus the two ways of not concluding.
#
# Both directions, because the refusing one alone says nothing about a guard that refuses
# everything: the self-test asserts a system-only NEEDED list is ACCEPTED, and a name that
# merely CONTAINS a vendored prefix without starting with it is accepted too.
#
# Registered outside the WIN32 guard above but still skipped there: the script is POSIX and
# the subject is ELF, so a green Windows run would answer a question nobody asked.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "vendored-libraries-static-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-vendored-libraries-static.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("vendored-libraries-static-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
else()
    pace_register_skipped_test("vendored-libraries-static-selftest"
        "the script is POSIX and its subject is ELF; Windows ships no shared yaml-cpp and the guard runs only in the Linux package job")
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Target-file guard hygiene. Fails when a `$<TARGET_FILE:>` names an executable
# the app table can gate off, without any enclosing condition asking whether
# that target exists.
#
# Such a reference is not a test that gets skipped -- it is a hard error at
# GENERATE time, so the whole configure fails and the message names CMake rather
# than the option the operator set. #390: `tls-smoke` was guarded on
# `PACE_ENABLE_TLS` and the sccache smokes on `SCCACHE`, so
# `-DPACE_BUILD_DAEMON=OFF` could not be configured at all.
#
# It ran green for everyone who does not have sccache installed, because the
# sccache rows are themselves gated on finding the binary. That is why this
# belongs in CI rather than in a reviewer's head: the tree alone does not say
# whether the bug is reachable, the developer's machine does.
#
# In the default set, for the reason test-name-hygiene gives above: it reads
# source files and reports.
add_test(
    NAME "target-file-guards"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-target-file-guards.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("target-file-guards" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And that check must be SEEN to refuse. It never had been.
#
# #510 exists because a stray `]` somebody left in a comment caught this check going
# blind -- an accident, not the check reporting a problem. Before #510 one `]` on an
# app table row took it from 25 references across 5 optional targets to 10 across 1,
# and it PASSED: the old refusal was a floor of ZERO and 1 is not 0.
#
# Five synthetic trees, one verdict asserted each: a guarded reference passes, an
# unguarded one is refused and NAMES file:line, a tree with no reference at all is
# refused as vacuous rather than reported as clean, a stray `]` on a table ROW changes
# nothing, and a build option the reader never turned into a target is refused as lost
# rows rather than silently dropped.
#
# Two more pin the frame walker the reference scan uses: a condition spelled across lines
# is followed, and an `endif()` with no open `if()` is reported where it happened. They are
# what is left of the fifteen #1369 added for a SECOND source of optional targets -- a
# directory the root added only under a `PACE_BUILD_*` option outside `src/`, which
# was `vendor/` -- and #1596 removed with that directory and its reader. The run states
# how many trees it built: 7.
#
# The bracket sits on a ROW and not in a comment, which is the difference between a
# regression test and the appearance of one: a REGEX-filtered `file(STRINGS)` discards
# non-matching lines BEFORE building its list, so the first version of that case put
# the bracket where it reached nothing and PASSED against the unfixed check.
#
# Shown to bite: run against master's reader, two of the five cases fail.
#
# In the default set with the other hygiene checks: it reads files and compares
# strings.
add_test(
    NAME "target-file-guards-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/target-file-guards-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-target-file-guards-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("target-file-guards-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Every refusal a `0xFC` surface answers with is routed, and says which fact it is
# asserting.
#
# Six sites in `WorkerProtocol.cpp` answered on the wire and incremented nothing
# (#327), which is not six oversights but the default: `EncodeErrorReply` takes a code
# and knows nothing about a sink, so counting was something each author had to
# remember. A refusal answered while nothing rises is indistinguishable, on
# `/metrics`, from a port nobody is talking to.
#
# This check used to scan a hand-maintained LIST of the source files somebody had
# remembered, and grew by hand twice -- the second time after `FrameEndpoint.cpp` had
# accumulated five uncounted sites through an entire surface migration with the check
# green throughout (#447). A list is exact about the files it knows and silent about
# the ones it does not, and silence reads identically to complete coverage (#492). It
# now GLOBS `src/`, headers included, so a new file answering a refusal fails it on
# the first bare call with nobody editing anything.
#
# It also reads the `RefuseUntriaged` backlog, because "deliberately not counted" and
# "forgot to count" were the same text until #492 gave each its own spelling. That
# reading was a printed total while the backlog was non-empty; #494 drove it to zero,
# so it is now an ASSERTION -- a count can only be watched, and watching is what let
# five sites accumulate behind a green check. See the script's header.
#
# In the default set with the other hygiene checks: it reads files and compares
# strings.
add_test(
    NAME "worker-refusals-counted"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-worker-refusals-counted.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("worker-refusals-counted" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And the check above must be SEEN to fail, on a file nobody listed.
#
# A glob is only worth more than the list it replaced if it actually bites on a file
# that was never named, and a guard nobody has seen fire is not a guard. This drives
# the real check against seven synthetic source trees and asserts each verdict
# separately -- clean, a new `.cpp`, a new `.hpp`, an untriaged site that must now FAIL
# as a backlog rather than as an unknown spelling, an untriaged site naming no issue, a
# FOURTH refusal spelling added to the allowed header, and a tree whose spellings have
# been renamed away.
#
# It asserts each direction rather than inverting the whole test with `WILL_FAIL`: a
# check that stopped refusing and a check that started refusing everything are
# opposite defects and a bare inversion reports them identically. Same argument as
# `node-scratch-isolation-e2e-selftest`.
#
# In the default set: it writes a handful of small files and runs `cmake -P` seven
# times.
add_test(
    NAME "worker-refusals-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/worker-refusals-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-worker-refusals-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("worker-refusals-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Every surface the compile node opens reaches its bind-failure verdict through the
# row, and no opener decides one itself.
#
# `NodeSurfaceTable()` carries a `BindFailurePolicy` column and the sentence saying
# why, and a `static_assert` refuses a row that omits either (#352). That guard is
# exact about the TABLE and silent about the CODE: a fifth opener can bind a port and
# answer `std::unexpected { std::format("cannot bind ...") }`, which compiles, links,
# passes every test, and puts the verdict back in the place the ticket was filed
# about while the row it ignores sits there stating a policy.
#
# So the second half is a scan, on `worker-refusals-counted`'s model -- the precedent
# for a rule enforced by the type system on one side of a seam and by nothing on the
# other. It globs the node's own sources rather than listing them, because a list is
# exact about the files it knows and silent about the ones it does not (#492).
#
# In the default set with the other hygiene checks: it reads files and compares
# strings.
add_test(
    NAME "bind-failure-seam"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-bind-failure-seam.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("bind-failure-seam" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And that check must be SEEN to refuse, in each of the ways it can.
#
# A source scan nobody has watched bite is indistinguishable from one that matches
# nothing, which is the defect it was written to close. Six synthetic trees, each
# verdict asserted separately rather than inverting the whole test with `WILL_FAIL`:
# clean, an opener that bypasses the seam, a primitive named in PROSE that must NOT
# be reported, a scan matching no file, a bind primitive nobody calls, and a renamed
# seam nothing reaches.
#
# The prose case is there because the check's first two findings were both doc
# comments -- a checker that fails on documentation of the thing it checks is one
# nobody can document.
#
# In the default set: it writes a handful of small files and runs `cmake -P` six times.
add_test(
    NAME "bind-failure-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/bind-failure-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-bind-failure-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("bind-failure-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# The DAEMON's shipped reference configuration against its option table.
#
# The same question the check above asks about the worker, asked about the binary
# most operators actually run -- which had no such check

# reasoning was true here too: `CliOptions()` drives the parse, the YAML is prose,

# documented in TWO commented blocks, the second opening "This is also how to...",
# which invites uncommenting it in ADDITION. That is a duplicate YAML mapping key,
# which the parser resolves by silently keeping one -- and the endpoint most likely
# to be lost is the TLS one, which is not a startup failure. The daemon comes up,
# serves plaintext, and reports nothing (#769).

# in two paths is the copy-paste this project treats as a defect. The older
# `check-node-config-reference.cmake` is deliberately NOT repointed here: that is a
# change to a guard other work depends on, and absorbing it would close one ticket
# by swallowing another. The follow-up is small precisely because this is
# parameterised.

        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-config-reference.cmake"
)

    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"

    TIMEOUT 60
)

# What the Windows packaging job asserts that service table THROUGH: the expected rows,
# the verdict on one service, the bounded wait, the package reader and the log checks
# (scripts/lib/MsiServiceTable.psm1). Only that job runs a package, so a helper that
# could not say "wrong" would pass every transaction there unseen. This drives each one
# in both directions against the real service control manager and an MSI database it
# creates, and installs nothing. Windows only: those two exist nowhere else.
if(WIN32 AND PACE_PWSH)
    add_test(
        NAME "msi-service-table-selftest"

                "Import-Module '${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/lib/MsiServiceTable.psm1'; Invoke-MsiServiceTableSelfTest"

        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
elseif(WIN32)
    pace_register_skipped_test("msi-service-table-selftest"
        "no PowerShell 7 interpreter found (pwsh, pwsh-preview), so the MSI service table helpers were not exercised on this host")
else()
    pace_register_skipped_test("msi-service-table-selftest"
        "not Windows: the MSI service table helpers read the service control manager and Windows Installer")
endif()

# Every custom action of the MSI, run the way Windows Installer runs it, installing nothing

# both directions. The WiX build looks at none of that, and an action started in an install root an
# upgrade had just removed failed the 0.3.0 upgrade on CI in under 100 ms (round 5).

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The operator port docs against `NodeSurfaceTable()`. Fails when a document
# claims a surface the table does not declare, omits one it does, or states a
# count of the port set that the table contradicts.
# scripts/check-node-surface-docs.cmake carries the tables and the reasoning.
#
# A port table is what an operator writes firewall rules from, so a wrong one
# closes a port that was needed or opens one that was not. One of the documents
# is a hand-pasted `--print-surfaces` transcript, which looks like machine output
# and is a string in a markdown file -- a reader has no way to tell it from the
# real thing, which is why the transcript is checked harder than the prose around
# it rather than being de-transcripted (#462).
#
# It checks BOTH directions. A scan that only walks the table passes a document
# that has grown a port the binary never opens, and on a firewall worksheet an
# extra row reads as authorisation to open something.
#
# Every markdown page under docs/ is GLOBBED rather than listed, for the reason
# `DocumentedCommandLines_test.cpp` records over these same documents: a hand
# list is exact about the pages it knows and silent about the ones it does not,
# and silence reads identically to complete coverage. Measured, globbing costs
# zero exemption rows and buys the pages a list would have missed.
#
# It also holds each surface's own flag -- the third mechanical column of that
# table -- so `--listen-raft` cannot be renamed while every page keeps the old
# spelling and this check stays green.
#
# It also carries a COUNT rule, which is the half that found the live defects:
# #290 stage 3 corrected every port NUMBER by hand and left "the full six-surface
# table" and "Five TCP rules" behind it, neither of which a row-to-row comparison
# can see. See the script's header for why that rule is anchored narrowly -- the
# word `surface` names both the four ports and the policy roles sharing one of
# them, so an unclassified collocation is refused rather than guessed at.
#
# Not labelled "smoke", for the reason node-config-reference is not: it reads
# source and markdown and compares strings, so it belongs in the default `ctest`
# set and must survive `ctest -LE smoke`. Every file it reads ships in an exported
# tarball, so it has no skip path.
add_test(
    NAME "node-surface-docs"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-node-surface-docs.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("node-surface-docs" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene;docs-subject"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The check above must be SEEN to refuse each thing it claims, and seen to stay
# quiet on the shape that merely looks like a violation.
#
# It is registered because four of that check's rules did not implement what
# their own comments said -- a digit count claim accepted in silence, a residue
# rule reading only the leftmost collocation on a line, an invocation-only fence
# held to the whole table, and only the first of a surface's flags extracted.
# All four produced a green run, and three were written WITH a comment asserting
# the opposite. A rule nobody has watched refuse is not a rule.
#
# Synthesised trees, never the real source root: mutations go to a copy, so there
# is no revert step and nothing can be left in the worktree. The pages a count
# exemption names are staged by READING that table out of the check rather than
# restating it here -- a second copy would redden this fixture every time an
# unrelated exemption row is added, which is the maintenance cost that gets a
# fixture deleted rather than fixed.
#
# The baseline case is the load-bearing one. Every refusal is evidence only if
# the unmutated tree passes; otherwise each case merely re-observes one failure
# and would go on passing with the rule it names deleted.
add_test(
    NAME "node-surface-docs-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/node-surface-docs-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-node-surface-docs-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("node-surface-docs-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    # Ten `cmake -P` spawns, each over a synthesised tree of about ten small
    # files. Measured across three runs on the bridged filesystem this repository
    # is checked out on: 1.59 s, 2.57 s, 2.42 s, against 1.48-1.60 s for the check
    # itself over the real 87-page corpus. The spread is the measurement -- a
    # single figure here would be one draw from it presented as the cost. 120 s is
    # the backstop the other selftests carry and is well clear of the top of it.
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# ThreadSanitizer scope hygiene. Fails when a test file in a concurrency-bearing
# directory carries no tag `scripts/tsan-gate.sh` selects on, so the gate's scope
# is enforced rather than merely asserted.
#
# It is registered here, in the DEFAULT set, and deliberately not under
# `if(ENABLE_SANITIZER_THREAD)`. The scope rots in ordinary builds -- somebody adds
# `Async/Foo_test.cpp` tagged `[queue][mpsc]`, which no row of the gate's table
# selects, and nothing says otherwise -- so a check that only runs in the
# sanitizer preset would report the drift to the one configuration nobody runs
# locally. This costs milliseconds and reads source files, so it survives
# `ctest -LE smoke` like the checks above.
#
# The failure it exists for has already happened once, before the gate merged.
# The reasoning, and why the check reads its tags out of the gate rather than
# keeping a second copy of them, is in the header of
# scripts/check-tsan-scope.cmake.
add_test(
    NAME "tsan-scope-hygiene"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tsan-scope.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("tsan-scope-hygiene" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The scope check's own decisions, driven over synthetic trees. `tsan-scope-hygiene`
# above runs it against THIS tree, where a green run is equally consistent with the
# check working and with the check having quietly stopped reading -- #317 is exactly
# that, a file-level match reporting COVERED over a case that had left the scope.
#
# The tree is staged from the check's own `PaceTsanScope` and copies of the
# real `scripts/tsan-gate.sh` and root `CMakeLists.txt`, so the cases drive the
# shipped table and the shipped tag expression rather than a second copy of either.
# It is staged once and restored after each case (#1423).
add_test(
    NAME "tsan-scope-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/tsan-scope-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tsan-scope-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("tsan-scope-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# CI scope classification. `scripts/ci-scope.sh` decides whether a pull request
# compiles anything at all: the `changes` job in .github/workflows/build.yml reads
# its `code=` output, and every heavy job is gated on it.
#
# That makes a wrong answer in one direction very expensive and completely silent.
# `code=false` for a path that does reach a build is a change merged with every
# required check reporting green and no job having compiled it -- the failure this
# repository keeps a list about, arriving through the workflow rather than through
# the code. So the test asserts BOTH directions and, separately, that every way of
# not knowing (an unresolvable ref, a failed diff, an empty diff, a path matching
# no row) escalates to `code=true`.
#
# Not labelled "smoke": it starts no daemon, opens no socket and needs no
# compiler. It builds a throwaway git repository in a scratch directory, so it
# asserts nothing about the tree it runs in and works from an exported tarball --
# and, unlike `repository-hygiene`, needs no readable git worktree of its own.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason `fleet-dashboard-e2e` carries the same guard: the script
# is POSIX and a Windows counterpart would be a translation rather than new
# coverage. The rule it protects is platform-independent and the Linux jobs run it.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "ci-scope"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/ci-scope-test.sh"
    )
    set_tests_properties("ci-scope" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
