# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Merge-queue reportability. Fails when a REQUIRED status check could not report
# a conclusion inside a `merge_group` event.
#
# The failure it exists for is silent and total: a workflow that does not listen
# for `merge_group` produces no check run for it, so the required context never
# reports and a queued pull request does not fail — it SITS THERE. Every workflow
# stays valid, every job stays correct, and nothing anywhere disagrees. It is the
# same never-arrives shape as a `paths-ignore` filter on a required check, which
# this repository has already paid for once, reached through a different door.
#
# It also cannot be demonstrated before the fact: a `merge_group` event only
# exists once a queue is enabled, and enabling a queue that stalls is the
# outcome. So the property is asserted from the workflow files instead.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason `ci-scope` carries the same guard: the script is
# POSIX and a Windows counterpart would be a translation rather than new coverage.
# What it checks is platform-independent and the Linux and macOS jobs run it.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "merge-queue-contexts"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-merge-queue-contexts.sh"
    )
    set_tests_properties("merge-queue-contexts" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )

    # And the half that decides whether the above is a guard at all (#408/#629).
    # That check now asserts every context every workflow produces carries
    # exactly ONE verdict -- required, deliberately not binding, or undecided
    # with the issue that will settle it -- and the arm that matters is a leg
    # ADDED with no row, because that is how a job joins the workflow unrequired
    # with nobody told. On a clean tree there is no such leg, so that arm can
    # never fire during an ordinary run and had never been seen to.
    #
    # `--self-test` synthesises one, plus five other defects, and a baseline that
    # must PASS. It drives a COPY of the real script against a copy of the
    # workflow tree rather than reimplementing the parser: a self-test with its
    # own parser tests the parser it ships with, not the one that runs.
    add_test(
        NAME "merge-queue-contexts-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-merge-queue-contexts.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("merge-queue-contexts-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The scope gate fails safe. Fails when a job gated on `scripts/ci-scope.sh`
# would be SKIPPED, rather than run, if the classifier itself failed.
#
# `ci-scope.sh` states at length that every way of not knowing escalates to
# `code=true`, because "we could not tell what changed" must never read as
# "nothing did". The workflow reading it inverted that one level up: sixteen jobs
# were gated `== 'true'`, so a `changes` job that FAILED published no output, the
# comparison was false, the jobs were skipped -- and a skipped required context is
# read by the ruleset as PASSING. A green, mergeable pull request that nothing had
# compiled.
#
# Sixteen hand-edits with nothing asserting them is how that comes back. Both
# rules are derived from the workflow rather than tabulated, so the SEVENTEENTH
# job cannot be added wrong; `release` needs `changes` too and is excluded by
# construction, because it never reads the classifier's output.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason `ci-scope` carries the same guard.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "gated-jobs-fail-safe"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-gated-jobs.sh"
    )
    set_tests_properties("gated-jobs-fail-safe" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )

    # And the half that had never existed: for two years those rules had only
    # ever been seen to PASS, which is the state this whole area is about one
    # level up. `--self-test` drives each of them against GENERATED workflows in
    # both directions.
    #
    # It earned itself immediately. Written with a bare `"$0"` to re-invoke, and
    # this file is mode 644 -- ctest runs it as `bash <path>` -- so every
    # negative case exited **126, Permission denied** and passed because the
    # SHELL refused rather than because the rule fired. Entirely green, testing
    # nothing. Caught only by the one case that expects a PASS, which is the
    # argument for a baseline case rather than only negative ones.
    add_test(
        NAME "gated-jobs-fail-safe-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-gated-jobs.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("gated-jobs-fail-safe-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# What a red Windows `Test` step says about the tree (#1515). The spawn probe after a
# red used to tell the reader to "read the failures as being about the tree" whenever
# spawning still worked -- and said so over a 170 ms check that had TIMED OUT at 60 s
# on a runner that could still spawn. A probe of one capability cannot clear a runner
# that degraded in another, and a timeout is not a verdict about the tree: a hang in
# the tree and a stalled runner both end in one.
#
# `scripts/ci-ctest-red-reading.sh` now draws that verdict from the spawn reading AND
# the ctest log, and this drives every verdict against staged logs, CR-terminated ones
# included -- and asserts that only the `tree` verdict tells a reader to blame the tree.
#
# Registered on EVERY platform, Windows included, unlike the POSIX-only workflow
# checks above: it is pure text processing, and Git Bash on a Windows runner is where
# it actually runs.
add_test(
    NAME "ctest-red-reading-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/ci-ctest-red-reading.sh" --self-test
)

    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# A non-required job failing inside a merge group reaches somebody (#684).
#
# Measured over every failing `merge_group` `Build` run the API still held on
# 2026-09-04: five of six failed a job that is NOT one of the required
# contexts -- `Code coverage`, `Windows-cl-debug` twice, `Package (macOS .pkg)`
# twice -- and all five pull requests merged with nothing anywhere reporting it.
# The pull request page is green, the queue reports success, and master is green
# afterwards because the same job passes on the merge commit. The one required
# failure in that set behaved correctly, which is what makes this a REPORTING gap
# rather than a gating one; nothing here changes which contexts are required.
#
# The notifier cannot be exercised before it merges: `workflow_run` only ever
# runs the copy of a workflow file on the DEFAULT branch. So the decision is a
# script and this drives it, while the wiring is asserted statically -- the same
# split `local-gate.sh`'s pure renderer and `node-scratch-isolation-e2e`'s
# readings record are examples of.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason `ci-scope` carries the same guard: the script is
# POSIX and a Windows counterpart would be a translation rather than new

# The shared workflow walk (#1456), driven directly. Its consumers reach three of its eight event

# FOURTH get it -- and `Package` jobs are not required contexts, so its absence is green everywhere.
# The selector is the `-DPACE_BUILD_TESTS=OFF` flag rather than three job names, and its

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The checks whose SUBJECT is documentation are still runnable from a bare
# checkout, and there is still at least one of them (#687).
#
# Those checks were skipped on exactly the pull requests that change
# documentation: `scripts/ci-scope.sh` answers `code=false` for a `docs/**`,
# `.agent/**` or `*.md` change, every heavy job is gated on it, and so a
# docs-only pull request ran no `ctest` at all. Right for a compiler, backwards
# for a check that reads prose -- prose drifts by being EDITED, and a prose-only
# edit is the change they never saw.
#
# `.github/workflows/build.yml` now runs `scripts/doc-subject-checks.sh` from an
# UNGATED step of the `Check C++ style` job, which is a required context, so a
# doc check that fails on a docs-only pull request blocks the merge rather than
# merely reporting -- reporting without gating is #684, and fixing one ticket by
# reintroducing the other is not a fix. `gated-jobs-fail-safe` asserts both
# halves of that wiring.
#
# What this registration adds is the third leg. The runner derives its set from
# the `docs-subject` LABEL above rather than from a list of its own, and this
# drives that derivation against the REAL tree: every labelled check resolves to
# a script that exists and to a command with no build-tree variable left in it.
# The runner itself is NOT registered to run for real here -- it would be a
# second execution of the three checks this file already registers.
#
# It has been seen to refuse: `sccache-backend-caveat` was labelled while its
# `-DPACE_SCAN_BUDGET_SECONDS=${PaceSccacheCaveatBudgetSeconds}`
# could not be resolved, and this reported it BY NAME rather than skipping it.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "doc-subject-checks-derivation"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/doc-subject-checks.sh" --list
    )
    set_tests_properties("doc-subject-checks-derivation" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )

    # And the verdicts, against generated trees. Every refusal this runner makes
    # is a refusal that has been WATCHED: the empty scan, the build-tree
    # argument, the missing script, the missing fail-regex, the all-skipped run.
    #
    # Two of its cases exist because a mutation exposed them. `message(FATAL_ERROR)`
    # exits **1**, so a fixture built on it cannot test the rule that verdicts are
    # read from OUTPUT: the status alone is sufficient and deleting that half left
    # the self-test green. There is now a fixture for each arm. (This said 0 on
    # 3.28 and 1 on 4.3 until #565 measured it; the correction makes the finding
    # stronger -- the fixture was insufficient on every machine, not only a modern
    # one.)
    add_test(
        NAME "doc-subject-checks-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/doc-subject-checks.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("doc-subject-checks-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 180
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A rulebook table's stated total against the table it describes (#780).
#
# A hand-maintained number describing a hand-maintained list is two sources of
# truth wearing one hat. Measured on ONE table across THREE consecutive commits:
# each commit fixed the previous count and introduced the next, all three by the
# same author, in the same file, on the same day, with the rule about instruments
# that miscount open in the next tab. Prose did not prevent it and re-reading did
# not prevent it, which is why it is a derivation and not a better sentence --
# the `RowsInEnumeratorOrder` argument, arriving in markdown.
#
# Labelled `docs-subject`: its subject IS prose, so it must run on the change
# that has no code in it. It is the first check in that set that is not a
# `cmake -P` script, and `doc-subject-checks.sh` was widened to name the `bash`
# spelling rather than this being written in CMake -- a markdown table is dense
# with `[#780](...)`, and a `file(STRINGS)` reader merges list elements on
# brackets, which is the hazard `.agent/rules/build-and-toolchain.md` records
# against six readers. A reader chosen to dodge a known trap is not a style
# preference.
#
# Not "smoke": it reads markdown and compares numbers, so it belongs in the
# default set where whoever edits a rulebook table is the one who sees it break.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason the checks above carry it -- the script is POSIX
# and what it checks is platform-independent.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "table-totals"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-table-totals.sh"
                "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
    )
    set_tests_properties("table-totals" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene;docs-subject"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )

    # And the verdicts, against synthetic trees. A suite with only a
    # correct-count case passes under every bug this check exists to catch, which
    # is the ticket's own acceptance clause -- so each of the two ways it can
    # fail to LOOK (no table at all; a marker syntax that has stopped matching)
    # is watched refusing, alongside a wrong count, an unmarked table, an
    # unreadable multiplier and a mode it cannot name.
    add_test(
        NAME "table-totals-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-table-totals.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("table-totals-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
endif()

# claim is answered exists.

# `RedisResp.hpp` has said for a long time that `CommandTable` is the source of

# RESP3 map, `unsupported.md` named `SADD` and `SMEMBERS` as examples of an
# absent family that is in fact served, and `COMMAND` was documented as always
# answering `*0` against a live `:66`. None of it could fail, so none of it did.
#
# `docs-subject` as well as `hygiene`: the subject IS documentation, and #687 is
# what happens when a check about prose is gated on a code change -- prose
# drifts by being edited, and by the code beneath it moving.

# POSIX and what it checks is platform-independent.

    # work, which is #1031. Both ways the reader can fail to LOOK (an unreadable

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A config file this package used to ship is retired from operators' disks.
#
# Deleting the asset row stops NEW packages carrying the file and does nothing
# about the copy already installed -- dpkg keeps a conffile forever once a
# package has claimed one. So retiring a config file is two edits, and the
# second one has no symptom: the tree looks finished, every test passes, and
# `compile-node.env` sat in /etc on every upgraded machine for four releases
# (#291, #383).
#
# This is the source half and it runs everywhere, because it reads two CMake
# files and needs no package manager: a path is never both shipped and retired
# (dpkg-deb refuses to BUILD such a package, so the mistake otherwise surfaces
# only in the packaging job, as a message about the archive), the export is
# written on every configure, and cmake/Packaging.cmake actually emits the flag
# rather than computing a list nothing reads.
#
# The behavioural half is `--deb` and `--rpm`, in the packaging job, where real
# packages exist to upgrade -- both managers across both operator states. It is
# not registered here because the default build produces neither package, and a
# ctest case that could never run is dead configuration rather than coverage.
#
# Not "smoke": it starts no daemon, opens no socket and finishes in
# milliseconds, so it must survive `ctest -LE smoke`. `if(NOT WIN32)` for the
# reason its neighbours carry it.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "obsolete-conffiles"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-obsolete-conffiles.sh"
                "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
    )
    set_tests_properties("obsolete-conffiles" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )

    # Each verdict watched refusing, against synthetic trees. Every assertion was
    # neutered in turn and exactly one case flipped for each -- including the
    # reads-the-export one, which was NOT exercised until its case stopped also
    # tripping the emits-the-flag assertion. Both controls are here too: a tree
    # whose config rows the reader cannot see, and one whose obsolete table was
    # deleted, since each reads exactly like a clean tree.
    add_test(
        NAME "obsolete-conffiles-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-obsolete-conffiles.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("obsolete-conffiles-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A rulebook `## Open work` entry still names OPEN work.
#
# `.agent/rules/` exists because every rule in it has already been a bug, so an
# entry that has gone false is a rule that has gone false. The cheap version costs
# a reader a minute; the expensive one is #395, where the entry asserted a borrow
# "cannot be tested" with a plausible argument attached, in a file `AGENT.md`
# routes every session to before they touch `Net/`. Seven of thirty-nine entries
# named closed issues when this was written.
#
# THREE registrations, because they answer three different questions and one of
# them needs the network:
#
#   * `rulebook-open-work` is the GRAMMAR -- the sections, the bullets, the
#     link/URL agreement, the renamed-heading and emptied-section refusals and the
#     non-empty assertions. It opens no socket, so it is in the default set.
#   * `rulebook-open-work-state` RESOLVES each entry, and is the only part that
#     can skip. Folding it into the one above would make a machine without `gh`
#     skip the assertions that need nothing -- a skipped required thing reading as
#     a passed one.
#   * `rulebook-open-work-selftest` drives a case per outcome against a stub `gh`.
#     A suite whose only negative case is a closed issue passes under all three of
#     the bugs this check is built around (the pull-request fallback, three faults
#     sharing one exit status, and the checker blaming its subject for its own
#     fault), so the cases are per OUTCOME and two of them must PASS.
#
# The GRAMMAR half is labelled `docs-subject` and the RESOLVING half is not, and
# the split is the point rather than a compromise.
#
# `ci-scope.sh` classifies `.agent/**` as docs-only and every `linux` job is
# gated on that, so a pull request editing only `.agent/rules/*.md` runs no ctest
# at all -- the ungated `doc-subject-checks.sh` step is the only thing that runs.
# This check's subject IS `.agent/rules/*.md`, so without the label it would be
# green-by-absence on exactly the change class that edits an Open work entry,
# which is #687 word for word.
#
# `rulebook-open-work-state` must NOT join it: that step is inside the required
# `Check C++ style` context, and a required context that needs `gh`, credentials
# and a network is a required context that fails for reasons unrelated to the
# tree. The grammar half opens no socket, which is what makes it eligible.
#
# This is the first check in that set whose subject is prose AND whose
# implementation is bash. That combination was impossible until #812 taught
# `doc-subject-checks.sh` the `bash <script>` spelling alongside `cmake -P`;
# #814 was filed for it here and #812 closed it first.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason `ci-scope` and `e2e-helpers-selftest` carry it:
# the script is POSIX, and a Windows counterpart would be a translation rather
# than new coverage.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "rulebook-open-work"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-rulebook-open-work.sh" --extract
    )
    set_tests_properties("rulebook-open-work" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene;docs-subject"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )

    # `smoke`, because it is the one registration that needs `gh`, credentials and
    # a network, and it must survive `ctest -LE smoke` being unavailable to it.
    #
    # `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77` is load-bearing rather than copied from its
    # neighbours: without it ctest scores the missing-prerequisite exit as a
    # FAILURE, which is a false RED on exactly the constrained runners where the
    # prerequisite is absent -- and whoever meets it deletes the skip rather than
    # suspecting the registration (#499).
    #
    # MEASURED: 39 entries, one repository probe and one liveness anchor cost
    # 19s wall on a warm connection from this host on 2026-09-05, almost all of it
    # round-trip latency rather than CPU (2.4s user). The bound is 5x that, since
    # the quantity that varies is the network and not the tree.
    add_test(
        NAME "rulebook-open-work-state"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-rulebook-open-work.sh" --resolve
    )
    set_tests_properties("rulebook-open-work-state" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene;smoke"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        TIMEOUT 100
    )

    add_test(
        NAME "rulebook-open-work-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-rulebook-open-work.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("rulebook-open-work-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
endif()


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Every `##` section of a rulebook file says whether `AGENT.md` tripwires it, and
# names the phrase (#876). Why a nominated phrase rather than a structural
# correspondence, why the marker is mandatory, and why there are THREE spellings
# are all in `scripts/check-rulebook-tripwires.sh`'s header and in
# `.agent/rules/README.md` -- not restated here, which is the rule the branch that
# added this check exists to enforce.
#
# Labelled `docs-subject`: its subject IS prose, so it must run on the change that
# has no code in it. `ci-scope.sh` classifies `.agent/**` as docs-only and every
# `linux` job is gated on that, so without the label a pull request editing only
# `.agent/rules/*.md` -- exactly the change class that adds a rule and forgets the
# tripwire -- would run no ctest at all and be green by absence, which is #687
# word for word.
#
# Not `smoke`: it reads markdown and opens no socket, so it belongs in the default
# set where whoever edits a rulebook file is the one who sees it break. Written in
# bash rather than `cmake -P` for `check-table-totals.sh`'s reason: these files are
# dense with `[#876](...)`, and a `file(STRINGS)` reader merges list elements on
# brackets.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason its neighbours carry it -- the script is POSIX and
# what it checks is platform-independent.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "rulebook-tripwires"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-rulebook-tripwires.sh"
                "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
    )
    set_tests_properties("rulebook-tripwires" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene;docs-subject"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )

    # And the verdicts, against synthetic trees, in BOTH directions -- a guard
    # nobody has watched ACCEPT is not known to work either, and a suite whose
    # only case is a refusal passes under a check that refuses everything. So the
    # first case is the accepting one, and each of the ways this check can fail to
    # LOOK (no AGENT.md, an AGENT.md that normalises to nothing, a marker syntax
    # that has stopped matching) is watched refusing, because all three read
    # exactly like a clean tree.
    add_test(
        NAME "rulebook-tripwires-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-rulebook-tripwires.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("rulebook-tripwires-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    # `AGENT.md` stays within `scripts/agent-md-budget.txt` (#876's neighbour).
    #
    # The two measures, why either alone is gameable, why the figures live in one
    # file and why `.agent/rules/*.md` is deliberately unbudgeted are all in
    # `scripts/check-agent-md-budget.sh`'s header and in the budget file itself --
    # not restated here, for the same reason the check above is not.
    #
    # `docs-subject` for its neighbours' reason: the subject IS prose, and
    # `ci-scope.sh` classifies a docs-only change as running no ctest at all, so
    # without the label the change class that regrows a bullet is green by
    # absence. Default set, not `smoke` -- it opens no socket.
    add_test(
        NAME "agent-md-budget"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-agent-md-budget.sh"
                "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
    )
    set_tests_properties("agent-md-budget" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene;docs-subject"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )

    # Both directions, and the accepting one first: a ratchet nobody has watched
    # ACCEPT is not known to work, and a suite whose only case is a refusal
    # passes under a check that refuses everything. Both ends of the ratchet are
    # watched (a rise AND an unrecorded fall, which are fixed in different places
    # and so carry different messages), along with the per-bullet ceiling, the
    # self-size prohibition and its issue-reference counterpart, and each way the
    # check can fail to LOOK.
    add_test(
        NAME "agent-md-budget-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-agent-md-budget.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("agent-md-budget-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
endif()


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A full clang-tidy sweep still happens somewhere, and it still reaches a person.
#
# Since #554 a pull request and a merge-queue entry both diff-scope, and the
# `push` on master is the only full sweep left. Both halves of what makes that
# sound are invisible in a run. Take the `--all` off the push -- or respell its
# guard as `event_name == 'push'`, so a trigger added later diff-scopes against a
# base whose diff on master is empty -- and every run stays green while the
# analysis is permanently narrower than the rulebook says. Delete the report step
# and the full sweep still runs, still fails and still reaches nobody: 18 of 18
# failed master-push runs were never once re-run.
#
# `scripts/tidy-sweep.sh --self-test` covers the scope COMPUTATION and, since
# #257, the CANARY's verdict. Nothing covered which events reach it with which
# scope, which is the half #554 changed.
#
# Two registrations for the reason `target-file-guards` carries: the check reads
# the real workflow, and the self-test drives every rule against synthetic ones in
# BOTH directions -- a rule that cannot be seen to fail has told you nothing, and
# one that fails on a correct tree is worse than absent.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason `ci-scope` carries the same guard: the script is
# POSIX and a Windows counterpart would be a translation rather than new coverage.
# What it checks is platform-independent and the Linux and macOS jobs run it.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "tidy-sweep-scope"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tidy-sweep-scope.sh"
    )
    set_tests_properties("tidy-sweep-scope" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )

    add_test(
        NAME "tidy-sweep-scope-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tidy-sweep-scope.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("tidy-sweep-scope-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The documented way to build the tidy sweep's compile database is the way CI
# builds it. Fails when `scripts/tidy-sweep.sh` or
# `.agent/rules/build-and-toolchain.md` documents a configure line that differs
# from the `clang-tidy` job's `Configure` step.
#
# `tidy-sweep.sh` opens by saying it is "for reproducing what CI will say", and
# until #454 the line it documented was hand-rolled and had drifted — in the
# flags that decide what the sweep reports and in the target set it can see at
# all, in both directions at once, every one of them reading like a clean tree.
# The measurements are in `.agent/rules/build-and-toolchain.md` beside the
# configure line; restating them here is how the number in one place stops
# matching the number in another.
#
# That is the same defect as the target-set rule the rulebook already carried —
# twelve lines above a code block that violated it, which is why "the comment
# says so" is not enough here.
#
# Derived from the workflow rather than tabulated, for `check-tsan-scope.cmake`'s
# reason: a second copy of CI's line would not be a cross-check, it would be a
# second thing to be wrong. WHICH files document a line is discovered by scanning
# too, per #492 — a hand-kept list is silent about the site it does not know, and
# silence reads identically to complete coverage.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason `ci-scope` carries the same guard: the script is
# POSIX and what it checks is platform-independent.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "tidy-sweep-database"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tidy-sweep-database.sh"
    )
    set_tests_properties("tidy-sweep-database" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )

    # And the must-be-seen-to-bite half. A comparison nobody has watched refuse is
    # not a check -- it passes identically whether it is comparing the right two
    # things or nothing at all, which is precisely how the drift it now catches
    # survived.
    #
    # All three moving parts are driven, because two of them decide what the third
    # even sees: the COMPARISON (a changed preset, a dropped `-D`, an added one, a
    # changed `-D` VALUE, an empty line, the pre-#454 hand-rolled line itself, and
    # the OTHER spelling of each token — `--preset=NAME`, `-D NAME=VALUE` — on both
    # sides at once, which is the arrangement in which an unrecognised token hides
    # a disagreement instead of manufacturing one), the DOCUMENTATION extractor
    # (fenced blocks, shell headers, an earlier unrelated `cmake --preset` line
    # that must not be mistaken for the anchor, a SECOND copy in the same file,
    # and the two ways a line fails to name this database), and the WORKFLOW
    # extractor (the right job's step among three that all have one, added steps
    # and an `id:` around it, an UNNAMED step after it that must not extend it, a
    # blank line inside the block scalar that must not truncate it, an unfolded
    # `run:`, and the two renames that must yield the empty string the main path
    # refuses).
    add_test(
        NAME "tidy-sweep-database-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tidy-sweep-database.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("tidy-sweep-database-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The local gate pins its analyser and refuses a compiler cache. Fails when
# `scripts/local-gate.sh` would run clang-tidy at a version nobody chose, would
# accept a build directory that already caches one, or would build its objects
# through a compiler-cache launcher.
#
# The gate itself cannot be a test -- it builds two whole configurations -- but the
# parts that can be WRONG are decisions over a build directory, and those are pure.
# `--self-test` drives them against synthetic ones: caches on the wrong analyser,
# one whose `find_program` never found any (`CLANG_TIDY_EXE-NOTFOUND`), one with no
# entry at all, one already correct, ones with compiler caching on, off and unset,
# and -- beside a cache the gate is entirely happy with -- a generated build that is
# launcher-fronted, and none at all. Those last two are what a FAILED configure
# leaves behind, and they are the cases where the gate has to be able to repair the
# state it refuses rather than refuse it forever.
#
# The failure it exists for reads exactly like success, in both halves.
# `find_program` never revisits a filled cache entry and the gate only configured
# when `CMakeCache.txt` was ABSENT, so a build directory kept whichever clang-tidy
# it first found forever and re-running the gate could not fix it -- re-running the
# gate is what skipped the configure. The same shape as the stale
# `PACE_CC-NOTFOUND` that kept build trees on sccache silently. And
# `USE_COMPILER_CACHE` defaults to ON, so a gate that never mentioned it built both
# configurations through whatever launcher was installed -- 148 and 618
# launcher-fronted edges when it was measured (#471), against the standing
# `-DUSE_COMPILER_CACHE=OFF` rule that `scripts/launcher-replay-e2e.sh` already
# names and #319 already paid for.
#
# Not labelled "smoke": it starts no daemon, opens no socket, and needs neither
# cmake nor clang-tidy. `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason `ci-scope` carries the same
# guard -- the script is POSIX, and what it checks is platform-independent.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "local-gate-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/local-gate.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("local-gate-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The TSan gate's instrumentation proof. Fails when `scripts/tsan-gate.sh` would
# accept an artefact whose objects were built without `-fsanitize=thread`.
#
# The gate's whole purpose is that a sanitizer job proves nothing until something
# proves the sanitizer -- and until #472 its proof could not refuse anything. It
# looked for `__tsan_init` in the BINARY, where the symbol is DEFINED by the
# runtime that the link pulls in whenever `-fsanitize=thread` is on the link line.
# Measured: a canary whose translation unit was compiled with no sanitizer flag at
# all produced a binary carrying `__tsan_init`, and passed. So this is a guard
# that had never been seen to fire because it structurally could not, which is the
# shape this repository keeps finding.
#
# `--self-test` drives the verdicts against staged object trees: a suite binary
# with one uninstrumented object, an uninstrumented canary, a fully instrumented
# target, an instrumented translation unit with no functions (the six
# platform-gated files in PaceTest, which carry `__tsan_init` and no
# per-function hooks -- a rule built on `__tsan_func_entry` would refuse them), an
# unreadable object, a missing object directory, two candidate directories, an
# empty one, and a binary that was never built.
#
# The suite case is the one that matters and is deliberately first. An
# uninstrumented CANARY already failed closed -- it exits 0, reports no race, and
# the gate refuses. The test binaries had no backstop at all, so an uninstrumented
# `PaceTest` ran, reported assertions, exited 0, and the gate printed
# "all scoped targets clean". A guard demonstrated only on the backstopped half
# would be a guard nobody has seen fire where it matters.
#
# Needs no compiler, no sanitizer runtime and no build: a staged object is a text
# file holding what `nm --undefined-only` should print, read through a stub `nm`
# on PATH. Only the reading of the symbol table is stood in for, and that half has
# no branches. So it belongs in the default `ctest` set, where whoever edits the
# gate is the one who sees it break -- rather than only on a machine with a TSan
# toolchain, which is how a gate this important would otherwise go unchecked.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason `local-gate-selftest` above carries it: the
# script is POSIX and what it checks is platform-independent.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "tsan-gate-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/tsan-gate.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("tsan-gate-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The tidy sweep's scope computation. Registered here because the only thing
# that ran it before was the `clang-tidy` job whose scope it computes (#588).
#
# That circularity is the point. The sweep decides WHICH translation units get
# analysed, and the rulebook already records that a sweep is only as complete as
# its database's targets -- so if the scope computation broke such that the job
# did less work, the job would still pass, and its self-test, living inside it,
# is not an independent check of that. Every comparable hygiene check here is in
# the default set for this reason; this one was the odd one out.
#
# Needs no compile database, no clang-tidy, no daemon and no socket: it builds a
# synthetic tree in a scratch directory and asks the include graph about it.
#
# `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77` is load-bearing rather than copied from its neighbours.
# `tidy-sweep.sh` requires bash 4.4 -- `wait -n` is 4.3, and expanding an empty
# array under `set -u` stops erroring at 4.4 -- and macOS ships a 2007 bash 3.2.
# Without this the script exits 2 there and a stock macOS runner goes RED for an
# interpreter that is merely old; with it, ctest reports SKIPPED.
#
# The three-line reason the script prints goes to stderr, and ctest does not show a
# SKIPPED test's output by default -- `CTEST_OUTPUT_ON_FAILURE` covers failures
# only -- so in a CI log the reason is in `LastTest.log` rather than on screen. The
# STATE is what carries in CI; the explanation is for whoever runs it by hand or
# opens the log. Said here because the difference is invisible until somebody goes
# looking for a sentence that is not there.
#
# And the guard has to be here rather than at configure time, which is the
# tempting repair and the wrong one: the floor is a property of whichever `bash`
# the machine has at RUN time, and a macOS runner can carry 3.2 at /bin/bash and
# 5.x from Homebrew. `if(NOT WIN32)` -- which `local-gate-selftest` above uses
# for a genuinely platform-shaped reason -- cannot tell those apart, and dropping
# the registration on macOS would put it back to a check that does not exist and
# does not say so. Skipped, absent, unstarted and failed are four states.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` still applies, but NOT for the reason its neighbours carry
# (#1259). Those say "the script is POSIX and what it checks is
# platform-independent", which for this one is contradicted by the workflow:
# `.github/workflows/build.yml:1116` runs `bash scripts/tidy-sweep.sh
# --self-test` inside the `clang-tidy-windows` job, so the subject demonstrably
# does run on Windows and is meant to.
#
# The real reason is the one at line 390: the bash 4.4 probe is deliberately not
# asked on Windows (#1355), because PATH's `bash` there can be a WSL launcher --
# measured, it printed `C:/Windows/System32/bash.exe is 5.2` for a Linux
# interpreter. `PACE_BASH44` is therefore empty on Windows and there is no
# interpreter to register with; registering PATH's `bash` anyway would run the
# sweep's scope computation against a Linux view of a Windows checkout, which
# answers a different question rather than the same one less well.
#
# So the workflow invocation IS this check's Windows home, and it is not a gap:
# the property is covered on Windows by CI and on every other platform by ctest.
# Said here because the two lines look like a contradiction until you know which
# question each one answers.
if(NOT WIN32)
    if(PACE_BASH44)
        # `${PACE_BASH44}` and not `${PACE_BASH}` -- see the derivation above
        # and the exemption row in `check-script-interpreter.cmake`, which names this
        # script and only this script.
        #
        # `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77` STAYS. The interpreter is known to clear the floor here,
        # so this arm should not skip -- but the script exits 77 for its own reasons too,
        # and a registration that scores those a FAILURE is #499 exactly.
        #
        # What this covers grew with #257: the self-test now drives `CanaryVerdict`
        # as well as the scope computation, so the guard that stops a whole branch
        # being reported clean by an analyser that never ran is exercised on every
        # platform and from `local-gate.sh` -- rather than only where a full sweep
        # with a working clang-tidy was already happening. The registration itself
        # did not have to change, which is why #257's first two acceptance clauses
        # were already met when it was picked up.
        add_test(
            NAME "tidy-sweep-selftest"
            COMMAND ${PACE_BASH44} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/tidy-sweep.sh" --self-test
        )
        # `PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` replaces the exit status as the verdict, and that is
        # the point: an EARLY STOP -- a `return 0` planted at the top of `SelfTest`, a helper
        # that returns before its block -- exits 0 having judged nothing, and ctest scored
        # it green on the exit code alone (measured). The script prints the line only once
        # every case ran, the case count agreed with `SelfTestCases`, and nothing failed.
        # `PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED` is the tree's one spelling for a `cmake -P`
        # check's verdict and does not apply: this is a bash self-test naming its own pass.
        set_tests_properties("tidy-sweep-selftest" PROPERTIES
            LABELS "hygiene"
            SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
            PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "TIDY SWEEP SELF-TEST PASSED"
            TIMEOUT 60
        )
    else()
        # No interpreter clears the floor. Registered anyway, and SKIPPED -- absent is not
        # skipped, and dropping the row is what makes a permanent gap indistinguishable
        # from a platform that was never meant to run it.
        pace_register_skipped_test("tidy-sweep-selftest"
            "no bash >= 4.4 on this host, so tidy-sweep.sh's scope computation and canary verdict were not exercised")
    endif()
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The sccache-backend caveat. Fails when a file recommends pointing sccache at
# pace-kv -- which is what naming either of the two `SCCACHE_*` backend
# variables is -- without saying, within 40 lines, what that costs under MSVC and
# clang-cl.
# scripts/check-sccache-backend-caveat.cmake carries the tables and prints them
# on failure.
#
# compile-cache-caveat, below, pins the warning the vendored CMake module emits
# when IT falls back to sccache. This is issue #170: the people README.md pitches
# the backend to never configure that module -- they have their own build system
# and set the variable themselves -- so that warning cannot reach them, and the
# configuration they are being sold is by definition ONE cache shared across
# every checkout and machine, which is the maximal form of the same hazard. Four
# surfaces said it with no caveat and README.md said the opposite of it.
#
# A rule about prose, enforced rather than remembered, for the reason net-boundary
# is: the next page pitching this arrives from an author who never saw the caveat
# on the others, and nothing about that fails, warns, or shows up in review.
#
# It searches every source root and JUDGES only markdown. A non-prose file that
# names a variable is neither judged nor waved through: it has to appear in the
# script's exemption table saying what owns it instead. `--help` is the live case
# -- its caveat is prose inside a C++ string literal, which a source scan finds
# just as happily in a comment beside it, so the `--help qualifies its sccache
# examples with the MSVC caveat` case in CliParser_test.cpp owns it and asserts
# against the RENDERED usage string.
#
# Not labelled "smoke", for the reason the three above are not: it reads source
# files and reports, so it belongs in the default `ctest` set and must survive
# `ctest -LE smoke`. It has no skip path -- the source tree is present in an
# exported tarball exactly as it is in a checkout.
#
# It is the slowest of the script checks, because it READS every candidate file
# to find the few that name a variable -- which is what makes it a discovery
# rather than a list of places to look. What that costs is not stated here. This
# comment used to say "~0.2-0.5 s against their ~0.1-0.2 s ... still far inside
# the timeout", and that was the most confident of THREE figures the tree held
# for this one check: a quiet WSL 9p checkout measures ~4.5 s, and two lanes
# measured 53-57 s against the 60 s ceiling while several gates ran at once
# (#479). All three were honestly taken and none carried its conditions, so each
# read as a fact about the check when all three are facts about a filesystem.
# The check now measures itself and prints the cost, the band it lands in and
# the headroom on EVERY run, so the margin is an observation anybody can act on
# rather than a claim written once. `PaceSccacheScanCostBands` in the
# script carries the conditions table; do not copy a number back out of it.
#
# The bound below is a BACKSTOP AGAINST PATHOLOGY, not a deadline, and the
# distinction is the whole reason it can be this large. This check cannot wedge:
# it opens no socket, spawns no subprocess, waits on nothing and has no
# unbounded loop -- it reads N files and exits. So there is no hang for a
# wall-clock bound to detect here, and the only thing it can catch is a
# filesystem slower than the widest measured band, which is a condition and not
# a defect. Do not tighten it as though tightening a real deadline: that is how
# this check arrived at a 60 s ceiling it spends 7% of on a quiet machine and
# 95% of under the load this repository routinely runs at.
#
# It is defensible only while a run that is going long SAYS SO. The script
# narrates progress with its measured per-file cost once a run passes
# `PaceSccacheScanNarrateAfterSeconds`, and ctest captures the output of a
# test it kills, so a kill at this bound arrives with the reason attached. It
# narrates through both phases -- per scan root while walking, per hundred files
# while reading -- and the one window it cannot narrate inside is a single
# `file(GLOB_RECURSE)` call, which is uninterruptible and cannot be subdivided
# without reintroducing the per-pattern traversals #502 removed.
#
# The script refuses a budget below twice that threshold, so the two cannot drift
# apart quietly: a run killed before narration could start is exactly the bare
# timeout #479 is about, a signal that cannot say which kind of failure it was.
#
# ONE variable, passed to the script AND used as the TIMEOUT, because the
# headroom the script prints has to be headroom against the number ctest
# actually enforces. Two numbers that agree today is precisely how this file came
# to hold three figures for one check.
set(PaceSccacheCaveatBudgetSeconds 180)
add_test(
    NAME "sccache-backend-caveat"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCAN_BUDGET_SECONDS=${PaceSccacheCaveatBudgetSeconds}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-sccache-backend-caveat.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("sccache-backend-caveat" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene;docs-subject"
    TIMEOUT ${PaceSccacheCaveatBudgetSeconds}
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# `sccache-backend-caveat` must be SEEN to report its own cost, and seen to stay
# QUIET when there is nothing to report.
#
# The narration is the entire answer to a `ctest` timeout on that check, and it
# only ever runs on a filesystem slow enough to trigger it -- which is to say it
# would have been merged untested and stayed untested on every machine where it
# is not needed. A fixture that has never completed has told you nothing.
#
# The NEGATIVE cases are the load-bearing ones: a check that narrates
# unconditionally passes a test that only proves narration fires, and a frozen
# clock reporting `0 ms/file` is a broken instrument reporting the best possible
# filesystem. Both rot in silence. The case table owns the count and prints it;
# it is deliberately not restated here.
#
# Synthesised trees rather than the real source root, for the reason
# `glob-traversals-selftest` uses them: driving the real tree would double this
# check's cost on exactly the machines #479 is about, and a synthesised corpus
# lets the checkpoint interval be small enough to exercise in milliseconds.
add_test(
    NAME "sccache-backend-caveat-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/sccache-backend-caveat-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-sccache-backend-caveat-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("sccache-backend-caveat-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    # Not derived from the budget above, and not a bare number by accident: this
    # drives the check against a 26-file synthesised tree, one `cmake -P` spawn
    # per case over a corpus 30x smaller than the real one. The case COUNT is not
    # restated here -- the case table owns it and the selftest prints it on every
    # run, and the previous version of this comment said "six" and went stale in
    # the same change that moved the table to ten. 120 s is the same backstop
    # every other selftest here carries, and it stays orders of magnitude clear of
    # a per-case cost measured in seconds on the bridged filesystem the check
    # itself takes ~4.5 s on -- which is exactly the margin the check under test
    # now reports rather than asserts.
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Service accounts. Fails when the account a binary compiles into its ServiceSpec
# is not the one the macOS package creates, or is created by no postinstall, or is
# never removed by the uninstaller.
# scripts/check-service-accounts.cmake carries the table and the reason each row is
# in it, and prints the whole table on failure.
#
# The failure it guards is silent AND macOS-only: launchd accepts a plist naming a
# UserName it cannot resolve, bootstraps the job, and simply never spawns it -- so
# the install-time refusal in ServiceControl.cpp is the only thing standing between
# an operator and a service that reports installed and never runs. Issue #87 was the
# other half of that refusal missing: `pace-node` named `pace-node`
# and the package created only `_pace-kv`.
#
# Registered here rather than left to the macOS packaging job precisely because it
# is macOS-only in effect: a check only one platform can run reports after the
# mistake has been pushed. This one reads the source tree, so every platform runs it.
#
# Not labelled "smoke", for the reason repository-hygiene is not: it starts no
# daemon, opens no socket and needs no compiler, so it belongs in the default
# `ctest` set and must survive `ctest -LE smoke`. It finishes in milliseconds, and
# like net-boundary it has no skip path -- the source tree it reads is present in an
# exported tarball exactly as it is in a checkout.
add_test(
    NAME "service-accounts"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-service-accounts.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("service-accounts" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The shipped systemd units start somewhere, and for the compile worker that is
# not a cosmetic question. systemd starts a service in `/` when the unit names no
# `WorkingDirectory=`, and a dispatched compile builds
# `-fdebug-prefix-map=<the worker's directory>=<what the client asked for>`. A
# prefix-map rule appends the unmatched tail, so a `<from>` of `/` matches EVERY
# absolute path in the object and keeps the rest of it -- `/usr/include/stdio.h`
# becomes `.usr/include/stdio.h`. The cache key is correct, so the object is
# stored and shared: a wrong object under a right key. Issue #674.
#
# Registered here rather than left to the packaging job for the reason
# service-accounts above is: the failure is only reachable where the real unit
# runs, which needs root and a live systemd, and a job only one arrangement can
# run is a job that reports after the mistake has been pushed -- worse here,
# because the packaging job is not a required context and so reports to nobody
# (#684). No end-to-end fixture substitutes either: every one of them starts the
# node from the fixture's own working directory, which is exactly how #674 reached
# master with a dispatch e2e already green.
#
# It also reads where the node's DISK TIER can live: the unit's `CacheDirectory=`,
# judged against every `cache_dir` the docs and the shipped reference configuration
# cite. The unit shipped without one while the docs named /var/cache/pace-node,
# a path ProtectSystem=strict left read-only, and the state directory -- the one
# persistent directory the service could write -- is refused by the node as a
# cache_dir, so following the docs gave no disk tier and the obvious workaround gave
# no node.
#
# `docs-subject` as well as `hygiene`, because half of what it judges is prose: a
# page that starts citing a path the unit does not create is a docs-only change,
# which the code-gated jobs skip -- so `doc-subject-checks.sh` runs it from the
# style job (#687).
#
# Not labelled "smoke", for the reason node-config-reference is not: it reads
# shipped files and compares strings, so it belongs in the default `ctest` set and
# must survive `ctest -LE smoke`. Every file it reads ships in an exported tarball,
# so it has no skip path.
add_test(
    NAME "node-working-directory"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-node-working-directory.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("node-working-directory" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene;docs-subject"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The check above must be SEEN to refuse each thing it claims, and seen to stay
# quiet on the shapes that only look like violations.
#
# It is registered because that check looks for the ABSENCE of a directive, and
# every way of getting such a check wrong makes it pass MORE often rather than
# less: a pattern that also matches a commented-out line, a reader that takes the
# first assignment where systemd takes the last, a specifier left unresolved. All
# three are cases, and each was confirmed to take exactly the case that names it
# red -- reading the first assignment takes `reset` and `last-wins` red in
# opposite directions, which is what a count alone could not have told anybody.
#
# Two cases exist to stop the opposite defect: a unit that spawns no compiler and
# names no working directory must PASS, and a later assignment overriding an
# earlier `/` must PASS. Without them a check that objected unconditionally would
# satisfy every other case.
add_test(
    NAME "node-working-directory-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/node-working-directory-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-node-working-directory-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("node-working-directory-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A non-ASCII argument, from the command line into main(). Runs each executable
# that has an option table with an unrecognised flag spelled with U+00FC, and
# requires the diagnostic to echo those exact UTF-8 bytes back.
#
# It is the only proof there CAN be of the fix for issue #155, because the defect
# is in what the OS hands a process: Windows transcodes argv through the process's
# active code page, so nothing inside a process can observe what was lost. A Catch2
# case can assert the code page a test binary ended up with (and
# Platform/NarrowText_test.cpp does); only running a real executable says what an
# argument BECAME on the way in. Verified to distinguish the two states -- against a
# binary built without the manifest the same argument arrives as `67 72 FC 6E` and
# this fails.
#
# Registered here rather than beside one binary because it names four, per
# .agent/rules/testing.md. Each row is guarded on its target existing: the test
# client and the launcher are gated by their own options in src/apps.
#
# Not labelled "smoke": each run is one process that rejects an argument and exits
# before binding anything, needing no daemon, no socket and no compiler -- so it
# belongs in the default `ctest` set and must survive `ctest -LE smoke`.
#
# **Its verdict does not depend on the console `ctest` was started from, and that
# is a property somebody has to keep.** It did depend on it until #1134: the check
# read the child's output through `OUTPUT_VARIABLE`, whose default `ENCODING AUTO`
# decodes using the CONSOLE's code page, so a correct binary passed from a UTF-8
# shell and failed from a CP437 one -- three default-set tests green or red by
# which shell ran them, with CI green throughout because its runner is UTF-8. The
# comparison is taken in raw BYTES now. If a red ever appears here, it is about
# the binary; do not make it go away by changing a code page, and read the
# script's header before reaching for `ENCODING`, one value of which reports a
# PASS for the very defect this checks for.
foreach(_utf8ArgvTarget IN ITEMS pace-kv pace-cc pace-node pacectl compile-cache-testclient)
    if(TARGET ${_utf8ArgvTarget})
        add_test(
            NAME "utf8-argv-${_utf8ArgvTarget}"
            COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
                "-DEXE=$<TARGET_FILE:${_utf8ArgvTarget}>"
                "-DNAME=${_utf8ArgvTarget}"
                -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-utf8-argv.cmake"
        )
        set_tests_properties("utf8-argv-${_utf8ArgvTarget}" PROPERTIES
            FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
            LABELS "hygiene"
            TIMEOUT 60
        )
    endif()
endforeach()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Compiler-cache auto-install fallback. Configures the tiny fixture project in
# compile-cache-fixture/ once per way the fetch can be declined, and requires
# each one to end in a status message and a clean fall-through.
#
# What it defends is the property that makes cmake/portable/CompileCache.cmake safe to
# vendor: it is copied into repositories that never asked for Pace, so a
# cache it cannot download has to cost them a slower build and nothing else. A
# configure that aborts because GitHub was unreachable would be a far worse
# failure than the missing cache it is reporting.
#
# Not labelled "smoke" for the same reason as repository-hygiene: it starts no
# daemon and opens no listening socket, so it belongs in the default `ctest`
# set. It does invoke the compiler (a project has to configure), which is why
# it is given more than that test's minute.
#
# The compiler, generator and make program are passed in rather than rediscovered
# so the fixture is configured the same way this build was, and the script's
# sandbox is the only thing that differs.
add_test(
    NAME "compile-cache-autoinstall"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/compile-cache-autoinstall"
        "-DPACE_CXX_COMPILER=${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}"
        "-DPACE_MAKE_PROGRAM=${CMAKE_MAKE_PROGRAM}"
        "-DPACE_GENERATOR=${CMAKE_GENERATOR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-compile-cache-autoinstall.cmake"
)
# Windows and a host without uname both report by printing `SKIP: ` and exiting
# 0 — see the script header for why the sandbox it needs is POSIX-only, and
# repository-hygiene above for why a `cmake -P` script cannot return 77.
set_tests_properties("compile-cache-autoinstall" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "SKIP: "
    TIMEOUT 600
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Compiler-cache caveat reporting. Configures the same fixture once per selection
# outcome and requires the module to say so when it picks a launcher that can
# silently produce a wrong build -- and to stay quiet when it picks one that
# cannot.
#
# Issue #153: sccache replays a cache hit's dependency list with the paths of the
# build that STORED it, so a second checkout sharing the cache records
# dependencies pointing into the first and then rebuilds nothing when a header
# changes. Measured here as 1097 recorded dependency edges into the wrong
# worktree and `ninja: no work to do` after a real edit. The module cannot fix
# somebody else's replay; what it owes a developer is a word about it, and that
# word is what this pins.
#
# Both halves matter. A warning that fired for every launcher would be one nobody
# reads, so the silence is asserted too.
#
# Needs no sandbox -- it names the launchers through the find_program cache
# variables rather than hiding them -- so unlike compile-cache-autoinstall it
# runs on Windows as well and has no SKIP path. Labelled "hygiene" for the same
# reason: it starts no daemon and opens no socket, so it belongs in the default
# ctest set.
# Which half of the contract this build asserts: the hazard is MSVC's and
# clang-cl's, so the module carries the caveat on exactly that condition and the
# check has to expect the noise there and the silence everywhere else. Resolved
# here rather than re-derived in the script, so the two cannot drift.
set(_pace_caveat_msvc_like OFF)
if(CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER_ID STREQUAL "MSVC" OR CMAKE_CXX_SIMULATE_ID STREQUAL "MSVC"
   OR CMAKE_C_COMPILER_ID STREQUAL "MSVC" OR CMAKE_C_SIMULATE_ID STREQUAL "MSVC")
    set(_pace_caveat_msvc_like ON)
endif()

add_test(
    NAME "compile-cache-caveat"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/compile-cache-caveat"
        "-DPACE_CXX_COMPILER=${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}"
        "-DPACE_MAKE_PROGRAM=${CMAKE_MAKE_PROGRAM}"
        "-DPACE_GENERATOR=${CMAKE_GENERATOR}"
        "-DPACE_MSVC_LIKE=${_pace_caveat_msvc_like}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-compile-cache-caveat.cmake"
)
# A shell that is not a Developer shell has a cl.exe that exists and cannot
# compile, and this is the first of these checks that runs on Windows at all --
# so it reports that by printing `SKIP: ` rather than blaming the caveat logic
# for a toolchain nobody set up. repository-hygiene above says why a `cmake -P`
# script cannot return 77 instead.
set_tests_properties("compile-cache-caveat" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "SKIP: "
    TIMEOUT 600
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# compile-cache-dispatch: configure's `[cache] dispatch:` line is true of the build.
#
# pace-cc dispatched a miss only when the environment of whatever ran the BUILD named
# PACE_SCHEDULER, so a developer who set it in one shell and built from another compiled
# everything locally while configure said only that pace-cc was the launcher -- measured:
# 341 misses, `NOT_CONFIGURED` on each. The module now decides at configure, bakes it into the
# launcher and says so; this asserts the decision over every source combination, and then
# configures the fixture with a stand-in launcher it builds and compares the printed line with
# the `PACE_SCHEDULER=` every generated LAUNCHER line carries. Both states, both sources,
# reconfigures with and without a -D from shells with and without the variable, and ninja's own
# regeneration -- where a first version of the fix turned dispatch off, and a retarget turned an
# explicit -DPACE_SCHEDULER= back on. PACE_ADDR is held by the same rule and pinned
# the same way.
#
# Runs on Windows as well -- the stand-in is compiled, not a script -- and reports a toolchain
# that cannot build it with `SKIP: `, as compile-cache-caveat does -- unless a decision failed,
# which is FAILED on every host, because the decisions need no toolchain. "hygiene" for that
# check's reason: no daemon, no socket.
add_test(
    NAME "compile-cache-dispatch"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/compile-cache-dispatch"
        "-DPACE_CXX_COMPILER=${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}"
        "-DPACE_MAKE_PROGRAM=${CMAKE_MAKE_PROGRAM}"
        "-DPACE_GENERATOR=${CMAKE_GENERATOR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-compile-cache-dispatch.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("compile-cache-dispatch" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "SKIP: "
    TIMEOUT 600
)

# How ctest SCORES the check above when its wiring cannot run. ctest lets SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION
# outrank FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION, so a decision failure printed beside the skip marker scored
# `***Skipped` -- measured. This runs the real check with a planted decision failure and no
# usable compiler, under the two patterns the registration above carries -- read back from this
# build tree through `ctest --show-only=json-v1`, never restated -- through a nested ctest, and
# requires FAILED at both exits before the wiring and SKIPPED for the clean controls. No skip
# pattern of its own: a verdict pin that could be scored skipped would repeat the defect.
add_test(
    NAME "compile-cache-dispatch-verdict"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/compile-cache-dispatch-verdict"
        "-DPACE_CTEST_COMMAND=${CMAKE_CTEST_COMMAND}"
        "-DPACE_BUILD_DIR=${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_MAKE_PROGRAM=${CMAKE_MAKE_PROGRAM}"
        "-DPACE_GENERATOR=${CMAKE_GENERATOR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-compile-cache-dispatch-verdict.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("compile-cache-dispatch-verdict" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 900
)

# The other half: that it can actually install one. Serves a synthetic release
# over file:// through PACE_AUTO_INSTALL_DOWNLOAD_BASE — the same seam an
# organisation uses to install from an internal mirror — so the happy path is
# covered without the network, and without spending the GitHub API budget that
# a fleet of CI runners behind one address has to share.
add_test(

        "-DPACE_MAKE_PROGRAM=${CMAKE_MAKE_PROGRAM}"
        "-DPACE_GENERATOR=${CMAKE_GENERATOR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-compile-cache-install.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("compile-cache-install" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "SKIP: "
    TIMEOUT 600
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# What the configure says when the daemon it just staged dies on startup (#1538).
#
# The staging test above is green whether or not that is explained: its stand-in exits 3
# with nothing on either stream, so it reaches this very branch and asserts only that a
# daemon was MENTIONED. `_pace_auto_start_pace_kv` spawned under `OUTPUT_QUIET
# ERROR_QUIET` and reported `(127)` -- a number that reads as *not found* for a binary
# the module has just staged and knows the path of, while the sentence that explains it
# sat on the child's stderr.
#
# Same mirror technique, and deliberately a separate registration rather than a section
# of the staging test: what is under test here is the FAILURE path, and folding it in
# would make one red answer two questions.

        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/compile-cache-daemon-diagnostic"
        "-DPACE_CXX_COMPILER=${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}"
        "-DPACE_MAKE_PROGRAM=${CMAKE_MAKE_PROGRAM}"
        "-DPACE_GENERATOR=${CMAKE_GENERATOR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-compile-cache-daemon-diagnostic.cmake"
)

    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
