)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# End-to-end distributed-compilation test. Needs THREE real processes -- a
# scheduler, a worker and the launcher -- which is what makes it a script rather
# than a Catch2 case. What it asserts is that an object compiled in another
# process on another port is byte-identical to a locally compiled one, that a
# dispatched result still populates the cache, that a worker registered for a
# different toolchain is never chosen, and that every refusal path still ends in
# a successful build.
#
# Registered HERE and not beside the launcher, and that is load-bearing rather
# than tidiness: src/apps processes its table in order, so at the point
# src/apps/pace-cc is configured the pace-node target does not
# exist yet and a $<TARGET_FILE:> guard on it silently skips the test. src/tests
# is added after src/apps (see the top-level CMakeLists), so every target this
# needs is defined by the time this runs -- which is also why
# .agent/rules/testing.md names this file as the home for script-driven tests.
#
# Both platforms run it, but they do NOT assert the same contract, and that is
# stated rather than left as an apparent omission. The Windows script covers the
# four cases that turn on the MSVC driver -- an equivalent object, still a cache,
# a C translation unit staying C, and fingerprint isolation -- because the driver
# is the only Windows-specific thing on this path: `/E` for the dispatch
# preprocess and `/TP`//`/TC` for the language are assertions about a driver
# nothing else exercises, and if either is wrong every dispatched TU fails and is
# retried locally, so the build is green and never once helped. Everything the
# POSIX fixture additionally covers -- failover, the role gate, concurrency, the
# driver's own language default, graceful stop -- is platform-neutral code already
# pinned by unit tests.
#
# The one exception, and the reason that last clause is the load-bearing half: the
# unreachable-cache case (issue #236) is asserted on BOTH platforms, because what
# it pins is control flow in `RunCached`, and `main.cpp` is in no test target at
# all. There is no unit test standing behind it, so a fixture that exists on one
# platform pins nothing for somebody editing that function on the other. A case is
# duplicated when the thing it guards has no other guard -- not when it is merely
# convenient to repeat.
#
# "Equivalent" and not "byte-identical" on Windows, and that difference is
# measured: both MSVC drivers stamp the clock into the COFF header, and `cl`
# additionally records the absolute path of the OBJECT inside the object, so a
# worker's output can never be byte-identical to a local build's. Every section
# carrying code or data is, and the fixture's own comparison is what says so --
# which is why that comparison has a self-test of its own below.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The compile worker reads its configuration file (#291), driven through the
# real binary.
#
# `ApplyNodeConfiguration` is unit-tested on every platform, which is a different
# claim from "the process reads a file": `PurgeExpired` was correct and tested
# and had no production caller at all. This asserts the composition in `main`.
#
# It also asks the three questions only a process can answer -- absent,
# unreadable and malformed -- each of which must produce its OWN named outcome
# rather than the nearest neighbour, since the exit code and one line of stderr
# are everything an operator gets.
#
# NOT labelled "smoke": it opens no socket, starts no daemon and needs no
# compiler. `--print-surfaces` renders the resolved configuration and exits, so
# every case is a process that lives for milliseconds.
if(NOT TARGET pace-node)
    message(STATUS "node-config-file-e2e: compile node not built -- skipping")
elseif(WIN32)
    # POSIX-only, the way the fixtures below record for themselves: a PowerShell
    # twin would be a translation of this script rather than new coverage, and the
    # one case that needs a platform -- an unreadable file -- is a POSIX mode.
    message(STATUS "node-config-file-e2e: POSIX-only fixture -- skipping on Windows")
else()
    add_test(
        NAME "node-config-file-e2e"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/node-config-file-e2e.sh"
            "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-node>"
    )
    set_tests_properties("node-config-file-e2e" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# exit-codes-e2e: what each daemon EXITS with, through the real binaries.
#
# Who reads an exit decides it -- a supervisor a start's 1 or 78, an operator a
# one-shot command's 2, a container runtime a probe's 0 or 1 -- and the tables that
# say so are unit-tested. The ROUTING in each `main` is not, since neither is in a
# test target: a site handing the refusal a start's outcome for an install's command
# line stayed green under every Catch2 case. Names both executables, so it lives here.
#
# NOT labelled "smoke": every case is a refusal that exits before anything is opened,
# bound or registered.
if(NOT TARGET pace-kv OR NOT TARGET pace-node)
    message(STATUS "exit-codes-e2e: pace-kv or the compile node not built -- skipping")
elseif(WIN32)
    # REGISTERED and skipped, never omitted (#1355), for `e2e-helpers-selftest`'s reason: the
    # bounded runner every case goes through is the shared POSIX library.
    pace_register_skipped_test("exit-codes-e2e"
        "every case is bounded by scripts/lib/e2e-common.sh's run_bounded, and that library is POSIX; the tables it drives are unit-tested on every platform")
else()
    add_test(
        NAME "exit-codes-e2e"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/exit-codes-e2e.sh"
            "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>" "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-node>"
    )
    set_tests_properties("exit-codes-e2e" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 300
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# fleet-dashboard-e2e: the leader's dashboard, over a real socket.
#
# Registered HERE rather than beside the node for the reason `dist-compile-e2e`
# is: `src/apps` walks its app table in order, so a test registered beside one
# binary cannot name another, and a `$<TARGET_FILE:>` guard on a target
# configured later does not fail -- it silently skips the test forever.
#
# What it covers that the unit tests cannot: that the route rows reach the accept
# loop over a real bound port, that `/metrics` on that same port is byte-for-byte
# unaffected by the credential (the assertion protecting every existing scraper),
# and that a node registering with its own scheduler reaches the rendered page.
if(NOT TARGET pace-node)
    message(STATUS "fleet-dashboard-e2e: compile node not built — skipping")
elseif(WIN32)
    # POSIX-only for now: the fixture is
    # `/dev/tcp` and bash, and a PowerShell twin is a translation rather than new
    # coverage.
    message(STATUS "fleet-dashboard-e2e: POSIX-only fixture — skipping on Windows")
else()
    # Socket-activate the node for real, which nothing else here does -- and that
    # absence is why #770 reached master: `ParseSocketActivation` is unit-tested and
    # passes under the bug, while the packaging job that caught it is not a required
    # context. Two instruments that cannot fail the same way, with nothing between
    # them. Skips 77 where `systemd-socket-activate` is absent, and says which mode it
    # ran in so a green run is never silently the skip.
    add_test(
        NAME "node-socket-activation-e2e"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/node-socket-activation-e2e.sh"
            --node "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-node>"
    )
    # Serial for `fleet-dashboard-e2e`'s reason: it starts a real node and reads a
    # well-known default port, so it must not race the other server-starting fixtures.
    set_tests_properties("node-socket-activation-e2e" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "smoke"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        RUN_SERIAL TRUE
        TIMEOUT 600
    )
    # The same handover with no `--advertise`: the node must advertise what it reads off the
    # socket, which is the stock package's configuration.
    add_test(
        NAME "node-socket-activation-derived-e2e"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/node-socket-activation-e2e.sh"
            --node "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-node>" --derive-advertise
    )
    set_tests_properties("node-socket-activation-derived-e2e" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "smoke"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        RUN_SERIAL TRUE
        TIMEOUT 600
    )

    add_test(
        NAME "fleet-dashboard-e2e"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/fleet-dashboard-e2e.sh"
            --node "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-node>"
    )
    # Starts a real node with three listeners, so it must not race the other
    # server-starting smoke tests for ports.
    set_tests_properties("fleet-dashboard-e2e" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "smoke"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        RUN_SERIAL TRUE
        TIMEOUT 600
    )

    # The same fixture over HTTPS, registered only where TLS is compiled in --
    # the shape `tls-smoke` already takes, and for the same reason: a test that
    # could never pass in the default build is dead configuration rather than
    # coverage. It additionally proves the handshake is REAL, by checking that a
    # plaintext request to the TLS port is not served: a port that merely answers
    # would otherwise pass every assertion above it.
    if(PACE_ENABLE_TLS)
        add_test(
            NAME "fleet-dashboard-tls-e2e"
            COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/fleet-dashboard-e2e.sh"
                --node "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-node>"
                --tls-cert "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/testdata/tls/server.crt"
                --tls-key "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/testdata/tls/server.key"
        )
        set_tests_properties("fleet-dashboard-tls-e2e" PROPERTIES
            LABELS "smoke"
            SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
            RUN_SERIAL TRUE
            TIMEOUT 600
        )

        # And the spelling that needs no certificate to exist first. Separate from
        # the case above rather than folded into it: the generated certificate has
        # its own properties to assert -- that it is valid for the names an
        # operator types, that it is NOT valid for others, and that the fingerprint
        # the node logged is the one on the wire -- and a fixture asserting two
        # different things reports which one broke by not saying.
        add_test(
            NAME "fleet-dashboard-self-signed-e2e"
            COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/fleet-dashboard-e2e.sh"
                --node "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-node>"
                --tls-self-signed
        )
        set_tests_properties("fleet-dashboard-self-signed-e2e" PROPERTIES
            LABELS "smoke"
            SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
            RUN_SERIAL TRUE
            TIMEOUT 600
        )
    endif()
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Two compile-node PROCESSES on one host must not share a scratch root (#279).
#
# Registered here rather than beside the node for the reason `dist-compile-e2e` is:
# it names three executables, and `src/apps` walks its app table in order, so a test
# registered beside one binary cannot name another.
#
# What it covers that `ScratchClaim_test.cpp` cannot: those cases prove the claim
# MECHANISM, this proves the WIRING -- that a node actually claims a root before it
# compiles. The defect was never a broken mechanism; there was none, and every unit
# test in the tree passed.
#
# Windows-only for now, the way `fleet-dashboard-e2e` records for itself. The claim
# path for both platforms is covered by the unit tests, which run everywhere; what is
# missing here is the POSIX half of the ORCHESTRATION, not of the guard.
if(NOT TARGET pace-kv OR NOT TARGET pace-node OR NOT TARGET pace-cc)
    message(STATUS "node-scratch-isolation-e2e: daemon, compile node or launcher not built — skipping")
elseif(NOT WIN32)
    message(STATUS "node-scratch-isolation-e2e: Windows-only fixture — skipping")
else()
    add_test(
        NAME "node-scratch-isolation-e2e"
        COMMAND pwsh -NoProfile -File "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/node-scratch-isolation-e2e.ps1"
            -PaceKv "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>"
            -Node "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-node>"
            -Launcher "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-cc>"
    )
    # Starts three nodes and a daemon twice over and drives real compiles, so it must
    # not race the other server-starting smoke tests for ports or for CPU.
    #
    # **This TIMEOUT has to dominate the fixture's OWN bounds, and that is the whole
    # of how it is chosen** (#1157). ctest killing the process produces no classifier
    # output at all -- the one instrument in that fixture that can say whether a host
    # was slow or a node was wedged -- so if this fires first, the most useful thing
    # in the run is lost and what is left is `***Timeout`.
    #
    # It was 900 against a 600 s survey ceiling, which left 300 s for four other
    # waits, two phases, four node startups and four real MSVC compiles. On the run
    # that filed #1157 the survey alone spent 600 s and the test reported at 602.83 s
    # with about 8% of headroom, on a runner measurably ~2x slower than the one the
    # same leg passed on the next day (`dist-compile-e2e` 105.66 s against 53.40 s).
    #
    # 1800 = the survey ceiling this fixture now carries (1200 s, derived beside
    # `$SurveySeconds` in the script) plus the rest of the fixture, measured at
    # 96.20 s on a fast runner and allowed ~200 s on a slow one, plus margin. The
    # margin is cheap because the survey wait no longer reaches its ceiling on a
    # WEDGE -- a node that stops emitting progress lines ends that wait in 180 s --
    # so this budget is only ever spent on a machine that is genuinely working.
    set_tests_properties("node-scratch-isolation-e2e" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "smoke"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        RUN_SERIAL TRUE
        TIMEOUT 1800
    )
endif()

# That fixture's WAIT CLASSIFIER (`Get-WaitVerdict`), against synthesised readings.
#
# Registered outside the block above and gated on nothing but the platform,
# because it starts no daemon, opens no socket, runs no compiler and now spawns no
# process at all -- so it is NOT labelled "smoke" and belongs in the default set,
# the same reasoning `dist-compile-e2e-selftest` and `repository-hygiene` record
# for themselves.
#
# It exists because the classifier was wrong in production and nothing noticed.
# Asked why a worker had not come up in five minutes it answered "the process was
# still WORKING (it consumed CPU throughout)" about a process that had written
# nothing for the whole wait and spent 1.1% of one core, and that verdict carried
# an instruction to raise the budget that #354 explicitly refuses. A fixture's own
# arithmetic is the one thing nothing else in this tree tests.
#
# Not RUN_SERIAL and not slow, and both for the same reason. The first version
# drove real stand-in processes arranged to consume specific amounts of CPU: it
# needed a core to itself, took 53 seconds, and still failed on CI, because the
# band it had to land a figure inside is 0.35s wide while a PowerShell process's
# own startup costs 0.2-0.5s. The decision is now a pure function over a readings
# record and the cases are rows -- both sides of every bound -- in about a third of
# a second. The CASE COUNT is deliberately not written down in this file: it moves
# whenever a case is added, it was stated in three separate comments here and had
# gone stale in all three, and the run derives and prints its own total. See
# `.agent/rules/testing.md`.
# Gated on an INTERPRETER, not on the platform (#837).
#
# `if(WIN32)` was narrower than the thing it guarded. The end-to-end fixture needs
# Windows -- it drives real processes and a real scratch-directory claim -- but its
# decision table does not: `Invoke-SelfTest` is a pure function over synthesised
# readings, which is exactly why it was split out of the fixture in the first place
# (53 s + RUN_SERIAL became 0.3 s). The registration never followed the split, so a
# classifier that had already been wrong in production could only be exercised on one
# platform. GitHub's `ubuntu-*` images carry `pwsh`, so the coverage costs ~1.2 s.
#
# An interpreter that could not run it would turn this skip into a FAILURE, which is worse
# than the `if(WIN32)` it replaces -- so `PACE_PWSH` names pwsh only. This script
# itself passes under Windows PowerShell 5.1 as well -- re-measured 2026-09-10 on
# 5.1.26100.9444 and on pwsh 7.6.5, both green; its sibling
# `dist-compile-e2e.ps1` does not, and they share the gate. See the derivation beside
# `find_program(PACE_PWSH ...)`.
if(PACE_PWSH)
    add_test(
        NAME "node-scratch-isolation-e2e-selftest"
        COMMAND "${PACE_PWSH}" -NoProfile -File "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/node-scratch-isolation-e2e.ps1" -SelfTest
    )
    set_tests_properties("node-scratch-isolation-e2e-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
else()
    # Registered and SKIPPED rather than omitted -- absent is not skipped, and
    # `if(WIN32)` left every non-Windows runner in the absent state for as long as it
    # stood. See `pace_register_skipped_test`, which owns the sentinel.
    pace_register_skipped_test("node-scratch-isolation-e2e-selftest"
        "no PowerShell 7 interpreter found (pwsh, pwsh-preview), so the wait classifier's decision table was not exercised on this host")
endif()

# The editor's save, landing inside one launcher invocation (#1650): a stub compiler that
# forwards to the real one and, while a trigger file exists, overwrites a source either after
# the compile or before a dispatch preprocess. Built for the e2e fixtures below and never
# installed. It is copied there under the real compiler's own file name, because the launcher
# picks its driver grammar by that name.
if(TARGET pace-cc)
    add_executable(mid-compile-edit-stub MidCompileEditStub.cpp)
endif()
# Passed whenever it was built, and a fixture that is not handed it FAILS rather than skips:
# the stub is built exactly when the launcher is, so its absence is a broken registration.
set(_pace_e2e_edit_stub_posix "")
set(_pace_e2e_edit_stub_windows "")
if(TARGET mid-compile-edit-stub)
    set(_pace_e2e_edit_stub_posix --edit-stub "$<TARGET_FILE:mid-compile-edit-stub>")
    set(_pace_e2e_edit_stub_windows -EditStub "$<TARGET_FILE:mid-compile-edit-stub>")
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# End-to-end compile-cache test. Starts a real daemon, drives real compiles
# through the launcher, and asserts miss/hit, byte-identical object
# reproduction, cross-depth portability and -- since #423 -- that a wrong object
# planted under a correct key is named rather than linked. Those are the
# properties unit tests cannot cover, because they need a compiler and a server.
#
# Registered HERE and not beside the launcher, for the reason `dist-compile-e2e`
# below records and this test learned the hard way: src/apps processes its table
# in order, so at the point src/apps/pace-cc is configured the
# compile-cache-testclient target does not exist yet, and `if(TARGET
# compile-cache-testclient)` there is not a condition -- it is the constant
# FALSE. The fixture would have reported its wrong-object case SKIPPED on every
# machine, in every job, forever, in a sentence that reads exactly like a
# legitimate skip. `check-target-file-guards` cannot catch that: it asks whether
# the guard was WRITTEN, which it was.
#
# Labelled "smoke" so it can be selected (ctest -L smoke) or skipped
# (ctest -LE smoke); a missing prerequisite exits 77 and reports as skipped
# rather than failed.
#
# Both platforms assert the same contract except the planted-wrong-object case,
# which is POSIX-only because it needs compile-cache-testclient -- the only thing
# that can STORE a chosen value under a chosen key. Every other way of planting
# one goes through the launcher, which derives the key from the source and so
# cannot be made to disagree with itself. The mechanism it exercises
# (`HitVerification`) is platform-neutral and unit-tested on every platform; what
# the fixture adds is the end-to-end path, and one platform proving that is what
# the rule about duplicating a case asks for.
if(NOT TARGET pace-kv OR NOT TARGET pace-cc)
    message(STATUS "compile-cache-e2e: daemon or launcher not built — skipping")
elseif(WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "compile-cache-e2e"
        COMMAND pwsh -NoProfile -File "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/src/apps/pace-cc/run-launcher-e2e.ps1"
            -PaceKv "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>"
            -Launcher "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-cc>"
            ${_pace_e2e_edit_stub_windows}
    )
else()
    # Passed when it was built, and the fixture says out loud which of the two it
    # is running as -- PACE_BUILD_TESTCLIENT is off by default and on for
    # the linux and clang-tidy jobs, so a reader of a local run and a reader of a
    # CI log are looking at different tests and must be told so.
    set(_pace_e2e_testclient "")
    if(TARGET compile-cache-testclient)
        set(_pace_e2e_testclient --testclient "$<TARGET_FILE:compile-cache-testclient>")
    endif()
    add_test(
        NAME "compile-cache-e2e"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/compile-cache-e2e.sh"
            --pace-kv "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>"
            --launcher "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-cc>"
            --compiler "${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}"
            ${_pace_e2e_testclient}
            ${_pace_e2e_edit_stub_posix}
    )
endif()
if(TARGET pace-kv AND TARGET pace-cc)
    # Binds a port and spawns a daemon, so it must not run concurrently with the
    # other server-starting smoke tests. The port is DRAWN per run since #220 --
    # `RUN_SERIAL` stays because the daemon is still a shared machine resource and
    # the compiles are not cheap, not because the port collides any more.
    set_tests_properties("compile-cache-e2e" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "smoke"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        RUN_SERIAL TRUE
    )
endif()

# The port discipline the Windows fixture above now has, driven WITHOUT it (#220).
#
# `run-launcher-e2e.ps1` used to bind a constant `21714`, so a run that did not
# reach its cleanup left a daemon listening for as long as the machine was up and
# every later run failed at `pace-kv exited immediately (exit 1)` -- naming
# neither the port nor the process, and reading exactly like a daemon that cannot
# start. It was diagnosed once by hand from `Get-NetTCPConnection`, after it
# survived a rebase and looked like a regression the rebase had introduced.
#
# The fix draws a port per run and, for an explicitly passed one, PROBES it and
# refuses by name. That decision is what can be wrong, and until this registration
# it was reachable only by running the whole fixture, which needs a built daemon, a
# built launcher and an MSVC toolchain -- so on every machine and every CI leg
# without those it was asserted by nothing. `-SelfTestPorts` drives the decision
# over staged holder records AND against real listeners on this machine, and needs
# none of the three.
#
# Not guarded on `TARGET pace-kv`, deliberately: needing no binary is the whole
# point, and gating it on one would put it back to running only where the fixture
# already ran.
find_program(PACE_PWSH NAMES pwsh)
if(PACE_PWSH)
    add_test(
        NAME "launcher-e2e-ports-selftest"
        COMMAND "${PACE_PWSH}" -NoProfile -File
                "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/src/apps/pace-cc/run-launcher-e2e.ps1" -SelfTestPorts
    )
    set_tests_properties("launcher-e2e-ports-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
    # The same fixture's dead-peer ceiling verdict and its starvation witness (#1739), which that
    # fixture reaches only with a built tree and an MSVC toolchain: driven here with neither.
    add_test(
        NAME "launcher-e2e-starvation-witness-selftest"
        COMMAND "${PACE_PWSH}" -NoProfile -Command
                "Import-Module '${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/lib/E2EStarvationWitness.psm1'; exit (Invoke-E2EStarvationWitnessSelfTest)"
    )
    set_tests_properties("launcher-e2e-starvation-witness-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
else()
    # Absent is not skipped, and dropping the row is what makes a permanent gap
    # indistinguishable from a platform that was never meant to run it.
    pace_register_skipped_test("launcher-e2e-ports-selftest"
        "no pwsh on this host, so run-launcher-e2e.ps1's port decision was not exercised")
    pace_register_skipped_test("launcher-e2e-starvation-witness-selftest"
        "no pwsh on this host, so run-launcher-e2e.ps1's dead-peer ceiling verdict was not exercised")
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
if(NOT TARGET pace-kv OR NOT TARGET pace-node OR NOT TARGET pace-cc)
    message(STATUS "dist-compile-e2e: daemon, compile node or launcher not built — skipping")
else()
    if(WIN32)
        # The readiness waits, and what a green run here CANNOT show (#1213).
        #
        # All six waits in that fixture are on the readiness MARKER rather than on
        # the bind: a socket that is bound is not one that is accepting, and
        # neither is one that is serving.
        #
        # **A passing run of this test is not evidence for that change.** The port
        # answers throughout and the process does eventually log, so a wait on the
        # bind -- or one matching any string at all -- passes this suite exactly
        # as the correct one does. The argument is `claude/batch-node`'s
        # `6ce7ed7f`: two lanes implemented #1213 independently and reached that
        # sentence separately, which is why it belongs in the tree rather than in
        # either transcript.
        #
        # The discriminating evidence is in `-SelfTest`, where the decision is
        # driven against a real listener and staged logs: a node waited on with
        # the DAEMON marker must REFUSE while its port answers, and neutering the
        # wait to return at the bind reddens exactly those cases and no others.
        #
        # An end-to-end run is still worth having -- it says the fixture is not
        # broken -- and one was taken: `dist-compile E2E PASSED` against
        # `out/build/clangcl-release`, 2026-09-11. It is simply not the evidence
        # the change rests on.
        #
        # The client is how the fixture reads the scheduler's own record of a worker
        # whose lease came back `withdrawn` -- one of the two readings a SKIP on a
        # host saturated by other work requires. Optional, and guarded rather than
        # required: without it that reading is absent, and absent confirms nothing,
        # so a withdrawal stays a failure rather than the test disappearing.
        set(_pace_dist_e2e_cli)
        if(TARGET pacectl)
            set(_pace_dist_e2e_cli -Cli "$<TARGET_FILE:pacectl>")
        endif()
        add_test(
            NAME "dist-compile-e2e"
            COMMAND pwsh -NoProfile -File "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/dist-compile-e2e.ps1"
                -PaceKv "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>"
                -Node "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-node>"
                -Launcher "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-cc>"
                ${_pace_dist_e2e_cli}
                ${_pace_e2e_edit_stub_windows}
        )
    else()
        # The client reads the scheduler's record of a worker whose lease came back
        # `withdrawn`, for the reason the Windows registration above gives.
        set(_pace_dist_e2e_cli)
        if(TARGET pacectl)
            set(_pace_dist_e2e_cli --cli "$<TARGET_FILE:pacectl>")
        endif()
        add_test(
            NAME "dist-compile-e2e"
            COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/dist-compile-e2e.sh"
                --pace-kv "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>"
                --node "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-node>"
                --launcher "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-cc>"
                --compiler "${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}"
                ${_pace_dist_e2e_cli}
                ${_pace_e2e_edit_stub_posix}
                --case suite
        )
    endif()
    # Spawns three daemons and several workers and drives real compiles, so it
    # must not race the other server-starting smoke tests for ports or for CPU.
    #
    # TIMEOUT 1200, and the number is the fixture's own ceiling plus headroom
    # rather than a round figure. No warm-run cost is quoted here because none
    # has been measured for this registration; the ceiling is what the bound has
    # to clear, and it is arithmetic. Since #451 the script
    # starts every node through `start_node`, which waits TWICE -- for the bind
    # and then for `compile node ready` -- so the suite's ten nodes contribute
    # twenty bounded waits, and `e2e_wait_seconds 30` is what bounds each of
    # them. With the four standalone `wait_for_port`s, the `scheduling for the
    # fleet` wait, the three registration waits, the 30 s fingerprint poll and
    # the four stop bounds, the script can legitimately spend ~905 s waiting on
    # a runner slow enough that every wait is slow yet none expires.
    #
    # A ctest timeout BELOW that is the failure this repository has already paid
    # for: `dist-compile-e2e ***Timeout 900.10 sec`, in three jobs, naming
    # nothing. The backstop is for a stall the script cannot see, so it sits
    # above the script's own ceiling -- and when `e2e_wait_seconds` or the number
    # of nodes moves, this number moves with it.
    set_tests_properties("dist-compile-e2e" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "smoke"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        RUN_SERIAL TRUE
        TIMEOUT 1200
    )

    # The POSIX fixture's own verdict arithmetic, against synthetic text.
    #
    # The sibling of the PowerShell registration below, and it did not exist: that
    # one was the only self-test either `dist-compile-e2e` had, so the POSIX
    # fixture's decisions -- how a Prometheus series is read, and how a DWARF
    # attribute is read out of whichever of three readers a host has -- were
    # asserted nowhere, on the platform most CI legs run
    # ([#597](https://github.com/LASTRADA-Software/pace/issues/597)).
    #
    # **The two self-tests do NOT cover the same decisions, and #597 asked that they
    # should.** They cannot: the Windows fixture compares COFF objects and this one
    # reads DWARF, so a correspondence by decision is a claim neither file could
    # keep, and asserting one would name coverage that does not exist. What is kept
    # instead is that each fixture's own verdict arithmetic is driven over synthetic
    # input by a check needing no build -- stated in the script's own header rather
    # than left to be inferred from the two registrations sitting next to each other.
    #
    # `if(NOT WIN32)` for `e2e-helpers-selftest`'s reason: the subject is a POSIX
    # shell fixture. Not "smoke" -- it starts no daemon, opens no socket and runs no
    # compiler, so it belongs in the default set beside its Windows sibling.
    if(NOT WIN32)
        add_test(
            NAME "dist-compile-e2e-posix-selftest"
            COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/dist-compile-e2e.sh" --self-test
        )
        set_tests_properties("dist-compile-e2e-posix-selftest" PROPERTIES
            LABELS "hygiene"
            TIMEOUT 120
        )
    else()
        # Said out loud, for the reason the membership guard above gives: a platform
        # guard with no `else()` is a test that does not exist there with nothing in
        # the configure log to say so.
        message(STATUS "dist-compile-e2e-posix-selftest: POSIX-only — skipping")
    endif()

    # The Windows fixture's object comparison, against synthetic COFF input.
    #
    # NOT labelled "smoke", deliberately: it starts no daemon, opens no socket and
    # runs no compiler, so it belongs in the default ctest set -- the same
    # reasoning that puts repository-hygiene there. It exists because a fixture's
    # own logic is the one thing nothing else tests, and this one's cost five CI
    # round trips to find out, the last of them on a control that compared two
    # objects with different NAMES and so reported a reproducible driver as
    # non-reproducible.
    # Gated on an INTERPRETER, not on the platform -- the same fix #837 makes to
    # `node-scratch-isolation-e2e-selftest`, applied to the sibling that block cites as
    # prior art. This one is the same shape exactly: a `-SelfTest` mode its own comment
    # calls a pure decision over synthetic COFF input, gated `if(WIN32)`, so it could only
    # ever run on one platform. Converting one of the two and reporting the constraint
    # enforced is this file's own #379 lesson -- six call sites named, thirty in the tree.
    if(PACE_PWSH)
        add_test(
            NAME "dist-compile-e2e-selftest"
            COMMAND "${PACE_PWSH}" -NoProfile -File "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/dist-compile-e2e.ps1" -SelfTest
        )
        set_tests_properties("dist-compile-e2e-selftest" PROPERTIES
            LABELS "hygiene"
            TIMEOUT 120
        )
    else()
        pace_register_skipped_test("dist-compile-e2e-selftest"
            "no PowerShell 7 interpreter found (pwsh, pwsh-preview), so the object-comparison decision table was not exercised on this host")
    endif()
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# PACE_AUTO_START spawn+probe (issue #90), the real-binary counterpart to
# compile-cache-daemon-staging above. That test proves the staging plumbing
# with fake --version-only stand-ins; a fake has no socket to accept on, so it
# cannot prove _pace_auto_start_pace_kv actually starts something that
# answers a real connection. This drives the real, freshly built pace-kv
# and pace-cc instead: a configure with no daemon reachable spawns one, a
# real translation unit compiled through the configured launcher reports a
# real MISS then a real HIT against it, and a second configure against the
# same address finds that daemon rather than starting another.
#
# Registered here rather than beside the launcher for the same reason
# dist-compile-e2e is: this needs the pace-kv target, so it belongs after
# src/apps has defined it. POSIX only for now, matching
# check-compile-cache-install.cmake's and check-compile-cache-autoinstall.cmake's
# own scoping — the process bookkeeping here is pidfile-based and POSIX's
# --daemon has no Windows equivalent; Windows coverage of PACE_AUTO_START
# is left as follow-up work.
if(NOT WIN32 AND TARGET pace-kv AND TARGET pace-cc)
    add_test(
        NAME "compile-cache-daemon-start"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-compile-cache-daemon-start.sh"
            --pace-kv "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>"
            --launcher "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-cc>"
            --compiler "${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}"
            --source-dir "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
    )
    # Starts a real daemon on a real port, same as dist-compile-e2e, so it must
    # not race the other server-starting smoke tests.
    set_tests_properties("compile-cache-daemon-start" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "smoke"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        RUN_SERIAL TRUE
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# sccache smoke tests. Registered only when an sccache binary is present
# (SCCACHE is the find_program result from cmake/portable/CompileCache.cmake). Each test
# the freshly-built pace-kv, points sccache at it over one wire protocol,
# and asserts a cache hit — the same compile-twice check the CI smoke jobs run,
# via the same scripts/sccache-smoke driver.
#
# They start a real server and invoke the real compiler, so they are slow and
# environment-dependent: the "smoke" label lets you select (ctest -L smoke) or
# skip (ctest -LE smoke) them, and a missing runtime prerequisite exits 77 to
# report as skipped rather than failed.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# TLS smoke test. Registered only in TLS-enabled builds. Starts the freshly
# built pace-kv with the checked-in self-signed cert, connects a real TLS
# client, and asserts a RESP PING is answered +PONG over the encrypted channel
# — end-to-end coverage of the TlsSocket handshake/pump. A missing runtime
# prerequisite exits 77 (reported as skipped).
#
# The daemon target is part of the guard, not only the TLS option: a
# `$<TARGET_FILE:>` naming a target that does not exist is a hard generate
# error, so `-DPACE_BUILD_DAEMON=OFF` failed the configure outright
# instead of skipping this. Every other daemon-starting block here already
# tests `TARGET pace-kv`; these two were the omissions.
if(NOT TARGET pace-kv)
    message(STATUS "tls-smoke: daemon not built — skipping")
elseif(PACE_ENABLE_TLS)
    if(WIN32)
        add_test(
            NAME "tls-smoke"
            COMMAND pwsh -NoProfile -File "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/tls-smoke.ps1"
                -PaceKv "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>"
                -Cert "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/testdata/tls/server.crt"
                -Key "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/testdata/tls/server.key"
        )
    else()
        add_test(
            NAME "tls-smoke"
            COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/tls-smoke.sh"
                --pace-kv "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>"
                --cert "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/testdata/tls/server.crt"
                --key "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/testdata/tls/server.key"
        )
    endif()
    # RUN_SERIAL for the reason every other daemon-starting test here carries it:
    # this one binds a port and spawns pace-kv, so under `ctest --parallel` it
    # would race the sccache smokes and compile-cache-daemon-start for it.
    set_tests_properties("tls-smoke" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "smoke"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        RUN_SERIAL TRUE
    )
endif()

if(NOT TARGET pace-kv)
    message(STATUS "sccache smoke tests: daemon not built — skipping")
elseif(SCCACHE)
    # EMPTY by default, and that emptiness is half of #183's port fix rather than a
    # tidy-up: `sccache-smoke.sh` draws a free port per run and a `--port` passed
    # here OVERRIDES that draw, so defaulting this to 11611 and passing it
    # unconditionally -- which is what it used to do -- leaves the draw unreachable
    # from the only caller that matters. The fixture then reads as converted while
    # every run still binds one constant, which is the shape a diff cannot show.
    # Kept as a cache entry so somebody who needs to pin it still can.
    #
    # Both drivers draw now, so an empty value here means "draw one" on either
    # platform rather than only on POSIX (#183).
    set(PACE_SMOKE_PORT "" CACHE STRING
        "TCP port the sccache smoke tests bind pace-kv to; empty draws one per run")
    set(_smoke_port_arg "")
    if(PACE_SMOKE_PORT)
        set(_smoke_port_arg --port ${PACE_SMOKE_PORT})
    endif()

    if(WIN32)
        set(_smoke_driver pwsh -NoProfile -File "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/sccache-smoke.ps1")
    else()
        set(_smoke_driver ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/sccache-smoke.sh")
    endif()

    foreach(_proto IN ITEMS memcached redis)
        add_test(
            NAME "sccache-smoke-${_proto}"
            COMMAND ${_smoke_driver}
                --pace-kv "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>"
                --protocol ${_proto}
                ${_smoke_port_arg}
                --compiler "${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}"
        )
        # They still drive one shared sccache server, so they must not run
        # concurrently with each other -- the per-run draw settles the port
        # collision, not the server one.
        set_tests_properties("sccache-smoke-${_proto}" PROPERTIES
            LABELS "smoke"
            SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
            RUN_SERIAL TRUE
        )
    endforeach()
else()
    message(STATUS "sccache not found — skipping sccache smoke tests")
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# migrate-storage-e2e: `pace-kv --migrate-storage` over both storage shapes.
#
# What it covers that the unit tests cannot: `MigrateConfiguredStorage` and
# `StorageShardPaths` live in `main.cpp`, which is in no test target -- and the
# property that actually decides whether the feature works for an operator is
# that the flag enumerates the SAME files the daemon opens. A conversion walking
# a different set would exit zero and leave the daemon still refusing to start on
# the store it missed.
if(NOT TARGET pace-kv)
    message(STATUS "migrate-storage-e2e: daemon not built — skipping")
elseif(WIN32)
    # POSIX-only fixture, the way `fleet-dashboard-e2e` records for itself: it is
    # bash and `mktemp`, and a PowerShell twin would be a translation rather than
    # new coverage. The paths under test are covered on Windows by the manual
    # check recorded in the pull request.
    message(STATUS "migrate-storage-e2e: POSIX-only fixture — skipping on Windows")
else()
    add_test(
        NAME "migrate-storage-e2e"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/migrate-storage-e2e.sh"
            --pace-kv "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>"
    )
    # Starts a real daemon to create the stores, so it must not race the other
    # server-starting smoke tests.
    set_tests_properties("migrate-storage-e2e" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "smoke"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        RUN_SERIAL TRUE
        TIMEOUT 300
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# pacectl-e2e: the client against a real daemon.
#
# Registered HERE and not beside the app because it names two targets, and
# `src/apps` walks its table in order: a `$<TARGET_FILE:>` guard on a target
# configured later does not fail, it silently skips the test forever.
#
# What this reaches that `pacectl-tests` cannot is the exit codes against a
# live server. The unit tests drive every handler over a scripted exchange and can
# prove the mapping from an outcome to a code; only a real daemon proves that a
# miss, a refused credential and a closed port arrive as three different outcomes
# in the first place. That discrimination is the whole reason the outcome table has
# six rows, and it is the property an operator's script depends on.
#
# NOT marked POSIX-only, unlike its neighbours above, and that is a measurement
# rather than an assumption: it was run to completion on Windows through Git Bash
# before this registration was written. It uses `mktemp`, `/dev/tcp` through
# `wait_for_port`, and the shared helpers -- all of which Git Bash supplies.
if(NOT TARGET pace-kv OR NOT TARGET pacectl)
    message(STATUS "pacectl-e2e: daemon or client not built — skipping")
else()
    # The node is OPTIONAL -- `PACE_BUILD_NODE` can be off -- and the eight cases
    # that need none are worth running anyway, so it is an ARGUMENT rather than a
    # condition on the whole registration.
    #
    # Resolved inside `if(TARGET ...)` rather than through a `$<TARGET_EXISTS:>`
    # generator expression, and that is the checker's rule rather than a style
    # preference: `target-file-guards` asks whether the QUESTION was asked, in an `if`
    # frame it can see. A generator expression asks it at a moment nothing can scan,
    # so a reference wearing one is indistinguishable from an unguarded one -- which
    # is #390's failure shape, where a guard that was never a guard read as compliance.
    set(_pace_cli_e2e_node "")
    if(TARGET pace-node)
        set(_pace_cli_e2e_node "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-node>")
    endif()
    add_test(
        NAME "pacectl-e2e"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/pacectl-e2e.sh"
            --pace-kv "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>"
            --cli "$<TARGET_FILE:pacectl>"
            # Empty when the node is not built, which the script reads as *skip cases
            # 11-13 and SAY SO*.
            --node "${_pace_cli_e2e_node}"
    )
    # Starts real daemons on drawn ports, so it must not race the other
    # server-starting smoke tests.
    set_tests_properties("pacectl-e2e" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "smoke"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        RUN_SERIAL TRUE
        TIMEOUT 300
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# fetch-transfer-bound: the dependency-fetch bound is watched refusing a stall.
#
# A dependency `git clone` that stops delivering never ends, because nothing
# bounds it (#526). `cmake/FetchTransferBound.cmake` states the bound, the
# measurements behind it and the reasoning for its numbers; this registration
# deliberately restates none of that, because a figure others cite lives in one
# place they point at.
#
# Registered here rather than beside a binary because it names two executables
# -- git and cmake -- and neither is a target of this project.
#
# Python and not the usual .sh/.ps1 pair, which is the exception this file
# otherwise does not make: the fixture needs a LISTENING SOCKET that accepts a
# connection and answers nothing, plus process-tree control to kill a wedged
# git without draining pipes its grandchildren hold (#239). POSIX shell can
# express neither, so a shell version would be a PowerShell implementation with
# a stub beside it rather than two implementations of one rule.
#
# It contacts nothing outside 127.0.0.1 and needs no daemon, no compiler and no
# network, so it is `hygiene` like the other checks that need none of those.
# The port comes from `bind(0)`: the per-run allocation rule exists because a
# fixture CHOOSING a port cannot see one already held as an outbound
# connection's local endpoint, and letting the kernel choose sidesteps that
# question rather than answering it.
#
# Python3 is resolved once, above `tidy-blind-spots-table`, which is its first
# consumer in this file; the argument for `find_package` over `find_program` lives
# there. Reused here rather than asked again.
if(NOT Python3_Interpreter_FOUND)
    # A WARNING and not a STATUS. The outcome here is that a guard standing
    # behind a rule which has already cost three lanes forty minutes each is
    # ABSENT from this build tree, and absent is not the same as passing.
    message(WARNING
        "fetch-transfer-bound: no Python 3 interpreter found, so the dependency-fetch "
        "transfer bound (#526) is NOT guarded in this build tree")

    # ...and the WARNING alone is what #1570 is about. It is written once, at
    # configure, into a log nobody reads twice; the RUN then contains no row at
    # all, so `ctest -N` returns a smaller total and every count derived from it
    # is quietly about a different set. Absent and skipped are two states and the
    # run could only express one of them.
    #
    # These three are registered here so the total does not move. That -- not the
    # three tests -- is the subject: the assertion is that `ctest -N` returns the
    # SAME number with and without a python3 on PATH, which is the only form of
    # the claim a count cannot be wrong about.
    pace_register_skipped_test("fetch-transfer-bound"
        "no Python 3 interpreter found, so the dependency-fetch transfer bound (#526) is NOT guarded in this build tree")
    pace_register_skipped_test("fetch-transfer-bound-selftest"
        "no Python 3 interpreter found, so the transfer-bound verdict machinery (#1469) is NOT guarded in this build tree")
    pace_register_skipped_test("msvc-arm64-coro-stack-slots-selftest"
        "no Python 3 interpreter found, so the MSVC ARM64 coroutine probe's self-test (#1274) is NOT guarded in this build tree")
else()
    add_test(
        NAME "fetch-transfer-bound"
        COMMAND "${Python3_EXECUTABLE}"
            "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-fetch-transfer-bound.py"
            --source-dir "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
            --git "${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
            --cmake "${CMAKE_COMMAND}"
            # The numbers as THIS TREE resolved them. They are cache variables,
            # and a `cmake -P` probe has no cache -- so the guard reading the
            # module on its own would always see the defaults, and would have
            # reported "the bound is 120s" for a tree configured with
            # -DPACE_FETCH_SILENCE_SECONDS=0, which is the one tree where
            # the answer matters. Passed from here, where they are resolved.
            --configured-window "${PACE_FETCH_SILENCE_SECONDS}"
            --configured-floor "${PACE_FETCH_MIN_BYTES_PER_SECOND}"
    )
    # 77 is this file's script-driven convention for "a prerequisite is
    # missing", which here means git or cmake could not be run at all. TIMEOUT
    # is the outer supervisor rather than the measurement: the script bounds
    # every wait it makes, and this is what catches it failing to.
    #
    # Since #1469 it carries a SECOND meaning: a run that could not judge its own
    # legs because the host was too slow for the phase deadline to mean anything.
    # That collapse is ctest's vocabulary and not a choice -- a test is passed,
    # failed or skipped, and `SKIP_RETURN_CODE` takes one value -- so the two are
    # told apart by the OUTPUT, which names one `INCONCLUSIVE:` and the other a
    # missing prerequisite. One status cannot carry two meanings, which is the limit
    # #1128 ran into on the Catch2 side.
    set_tests_properties("fetch-transfer-bound" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        TIMEOUT 300
    )

    # The verdict machinery, driven against synthesised readings rather than against
    # a real stall: this needs no git, no cmake, no listener and no clock, so it runs
    # in the DEFAULT set on every platform and in seconds.
    #
    # It is registered SEPARATELY and not folded into the check above for the reason
    # #1469 exists: the check above cannot be made to say INCONCLUSIVE on a healthy
    # developer machine, so the outcome that matters would be the one nobody ever
    # watches fire. Here every verdict is reached by construction, including the
    # measured `Windows-cl-debug` run the ticket was filed from, entered as data.
    add_test(
        NAME "fetch-transfer-bound-selftest"
        COMMAND "${Python3_EXECUTABLE}"
            "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-fetch-transfer-bound.py"
            --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("fetch-transfer-bound-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )

    # The MSVC ARM64 coroutine probe's own self-test (#1274).
    #
    # Found by extending `check-selftest-registered` to `.py`, which had never
    # looked at Python at all: five cases judging the probe's control-flow model,
    # written carefully, cited in the rules file, and run by nothing. That is
    # #596's shape exactly -- an unrun self-test reads as coverage from every
    # direction and is silent about being unrun.
    #
    # The PROBE still runs nowhere and the file says why: it wants `/FAs` listings
    # from an ARM64 MSVC build. Its self-test wants none of that -- it builds
    # synthetic listings and judges a pure function -- so it runs wherever Python
    # does, which is the whole reason it could sit unregistered without anybody
    # noticing a gap.
    add_test(
        NAME "msvc-arm64-coro-stack-slots-selftest"
        COMMAND "${Python3_EXECUTABLE}"
            "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/probes/msvc-arm64-coro-stack-slots.py"
            --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("msvc-arm64-coro-stack-slots-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# An `IMetricsSink::Counter` becomes an index in one place, `CounterIndex`. Fails when
# anything in `src/` -- tests included -- casts a counter to a number anywhere else, when
# the converter's own file holds a different number of conversions than its row allows,
# and when the scan matches nothing at all. scripts/check-counter-index-seam.cmake carries the
# table and states what the pattern cannot see (#1366).
#
# Half of the rule is a type: `CounterCells` has no integer subscript, so a table of that
# type cannot be indexed by a cast, whatever the operand is spelled. This covers the other
# half -- a new table keyed by the enum that is not a `CounterCells`, indexed by an ordinary
# cast that no compiler remarks on.
#
# Default set, no skip path: it reads the source tree and compares strings.
add_test(
    NAME "counter-index-seam"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-counter-index-seam.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("counter-index-seam" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And `counter-index-seam` must be SEEN to refuse each thing it claims -- a hand-rolled cast,
# `std::to_underlying`, a test source, a second conversion in the allowed file, a stale row,
# a scan matching nothing, a tree it cannot read -- and to accept a comment spelling the cast
# and a cast of `counters.size()`.
#
# Shown to bite, one arm at a time: disarming the vacuity refusal reddens exactly `none`,
# the stale arm exactly `stale`, the count exactly `extra`, the word boundary exactly
# `plural`, the `to_underlying` spelling exactly `underlying`; not stripping comments reddens
# `prose` and every case whose converter stand-in carries a commented cast.
add_test(
    NAME "counter-index-seam-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/counter-index-seam-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-counter-index-seam-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("counter-index-seam-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A unit compiled against a different `IMetricsSink::Counter` fails to LINK (#1361).
#
# Builds a fixture project with this build's compiler and generator, in Debug and Release:
# a control against a byte-identical copy of the header must link, and a unit against a
# copy with one enumerator inserted before `Last` must fail naming `RequireExtent`.
# No single-TU test can observe a cross-TU skew, and what the guard claims is what a
# LINKER keeps, so it is watched with the tool rather than reasoned about: the header
# records which shapes of the guard linked a skewed unit on which compiler.
#
# Invokes the compiler, so it is given minutes; starts no daemon and opens no socket, so it
# is in the default set with the other hygiene checks. `SKIP: ` where the fixture cannot
# configure at all -- a Windows shell that is not a Developer shell has a `cl.exe` that
# cannot compile, the reason compile-cache-caveat gives.
add_test(
    NAME "counter-extent-link"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/counter-extent-link"
        "-DPACE_CXX_COMPILER=${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}"
        "-DPACE_MAKE_PROGRAM=${CMAKE_MAKE_PROGRAM}"
        "-DPACE_GENERATOR=${CMAKE_GENERATOR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-counter-extent-link.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("counter-extent-link" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "SKIP: "
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 600
)

# And `counter-extent-link` must be SEEN to refuse: the guard removed from the header (the
# skewed unit links), the definition renamed (the control cannot link, which must not read as
# the guard working), and no `Last,` to place the skew at. Shown to bite, one arm at a time:
# reading a successful skewed link as a pass reddens exactly `noanchor`, dropping the control
# exactly `nodefinition`, dropping the anchor refusal exactly `noplace`.
add_test(
    NAME "counter-extent-link-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/counter-extent-link-selftest"
        "-DPACE_CXX_COMPILER=${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}"
        "-DPACE_MAKE_PROGRAM=${CMAKE_MAKE_PROGRAM}"
        "-DPACE_GENERATOR=${CMAKE_GENERATOR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-counter-extent-link-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("counter-extent-link-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "SKIP: "
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 600
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Every `execute_process` that runs an external program during configure carries
# a TIMEOUT.
#
# A configure that blocks in a subprocess writes NOTHING -- no compile starts, no
# artefact is touched -- so it is indistinguishable from a hung build, and the
# only evidence it leaves is two mtimes far apart. `git` is the sharp case:
# `cmake/Version.cmake` runs it at every configure because the tag is the only
# version source, and "it is only `git describe`" reads as a pure local
# computation while git in fact takes repository locks and may be racing
# `gc --auto`.
add_test(
    NAME "configure-subprocess-bounds"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-configure-subprocess-bounds.sh"
)
set_tests_properties("configure-subprocess-bounds" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And it must be SEEN to refuse. Four cases, both directions: a bounded call
# passes, an unbounded one is caught, a COMMENT mentioning execute_process is not
# a call site, and an empty scan REFUSES rather than reporting a clean tree --
# two empty lists agree perfectly.
add_test(
    NAME "configure-subprocess-bounds-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-configure-subprocess-bounds.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("configure-subprocess-bounds-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Every `gh` listing goes through the shared reporter, or says why it does not.
#
# A listing that came back AT its `--limit` is a real answer about a set that is
# not the whole set, and nothing about the rows distinguishes it -- so the cap
# reads as the total (#724). Measured wrong twice here, both times in the
# safe-looking direction, which is why neither reading invited a second look.
# Raising the limit is not a fix: it moves the cliff and hides that there is one.
add_test(
    NAME "gh-listing-seam"

)
set_tests_properties("gh-listing-seam" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# Six cases, both directions -- including the two ways this check could pass

add_test(
    NAME "gh-listing-seam-selftest"

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A pull request whose COMMITS name a ticket its BODY does not would merge and
# leave that ticket open (#974).
#
# Only the SELF-TEST is registered, and only the decision is tested. The live half
# needs the GitHub API and a pull request number, neither of which a build has --
# the same split `rulebook-open-work` makes, and for the same reason: a check that
# cannot reach its subject must report that as its own outcome rather than as a
# pass, and in ctest the honest outcome would be a skip on every run. So the live
# arm is a workflow step, where the number and the token exist, and the DECISION is
# a pure function over (body, commits) driven here against staged records.
#
# Eight cases, both directions, and the batch case is the one that matters: a body
# naming one of two tickets must REFUSE, because that is #959 exactly -- it closed
# #826 and left #866 open, which is the shape a count cannot see.
add_test(
    NAME "pr-closing-keywords"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-pr-closing-keywords.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("pr-closing-keywords" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Every cache-flow fall-back names which continuation it takes (#910).
#
# `Warn` returns nullopt -> a plain compile with NO STORE; `WarnAndCarryOn` falls
# through to the MISS path, whose STORE REPAIRS the entry. The question both answer
# is "does the cache now hold a value that must be REPLACED?", and getting it
# backwards once cost a permanently dead cache: a `CompileValueVersion` bump does
# not move the key, so a foreign-generation value stays under the key the new build
# computes and is never overwritten.
#
# Nothing else can catch it -- both spellings type-check, `main.cpp` is in no test
# target (#909), and the symptom is a slow build rather than a failure.
add_test(
    NAME "cache-flow-continuations"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-cache-flow-continuations.sh"
)
set_tests_properties("cache-flow-continuations" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# Both rules, in both directions: for the continuations, the defect itself (the
# undecodable arm choosing no-STORE) and the two ways this check could pass while
# guarding nothing -- the helpers renamed away so it matches nothing, and its
# subject file gone; for the one door to the cache, a call outside it, the routes
# around it (a built exchange, the callee as a pointer), a stale route row, and its
# own reader failing. No count here: the self-test prints how many cases it ran,
# and a count beside the list is a second claim that goes stale (round 10, M6).
add_test(
    NAME "cache-flow-continuations-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-cache-flow-continuations.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("cache-flow-continuations-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The Tools page names exactly the binaries a user installs (#1035).
#
# `docs/tools/index.md` is the overview a reader lands on to find what they can run,
# and what belongs there is the INSTALLED set -- the targets a package actually puts
# on a machine. Everything else under `src/apps/` is contributor tooling shipped to
# nobody. Nothing connected the two, so `compile-cache-testclient` -- default OFF, no
# `install()` rule, in no package -- sat there as the third peer of the two real
# launchers and was published to users for months (#1030).
#
# Both directions, because they are different mistakes: a tool documented but not
# installed is that defect, a reader told to run something no package contains; a
# tool installed but not documented is the quieter mirror, a binary users get and
# cannot find.
#
# Labelled `docs-subject` as well as `hygiene`: its subject IS prose, and prose
# drifts by being edited, which is the whole reason that label exists (#687).
add_test(
    NAME "tools-page-installed-set"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tools-page-installed-set.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("tools-page-installed-set" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene;docs-subject"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And that check must be SEEN to refuse each thing it claims to refuse.
#
# Eleven synthetic trees. Both mismatch directions; the four ways the scan can stop
# working, each with its own repair (the app table restructured, an app the table
# names but the tree lacks, every install rule gone, the page's heading style
# changed or the page emptied); and the three BRACKET arms -- a violation alone, the
# same violation behind an unbalanced `[`, and the bracket with no violation. The
# third is not decoration: without it a check that refused every bracket would pass
# the second for the wrong reason.
#
# Two of those cases earned their place immediately. The app-table one found that the
# check matched its row SHAPE anywhere in the file rather than inside the named table,
# so its own "matched no rows" guard could never fire; and writing a literal `[` into
# the fixture's own row table merged seven rows into one element, which is the
# bracket-vulnerable reader this check exists to avoid, biting the fixture that tests
# for it.
add_test(
    NAME "tools-page-installed-set-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/tools-page-installed-set-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tools-page-installed-set-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("tools-page-installed-set-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# No C++ source constructs from `std::istreambuf_iterator` (#1029).
#
# GCC 14 at -O2 and above inlines that iterator's `sgetc` and reports
# `-Werror=null-dereference` inside <streambuf>. A false positive, and one this
# project cannot silence, since warnings are errors and the rule is to fix them at
# the source. The house spelling is `Cc::ReadFileBytes` or `out << in.rdbuf()`.
#
# In the DEFAULT set, where every developer meets it, because only an OPTIMISED
# build can see the real failure: -Wnull-dereference is in neither -Wall nor
# -Wextra, `-fsyntax-only` cannot reach it, and both times this shipped the author
# had green MSVC, clang-tidy, ASan and UBSan runs. The second instance also hid a
# byte-identical sibling behind it -- a build stops at the first failure -- so
# fixing the named site alone would have relocated the failure one file along,
# where it reads as a regression introduced by the fix (#172).
#
# The check exists rather than a comment because the comments were already there:
# `git grep -n "istreambuf_iterator" -- 'src/'` at ed295468 found 8 occurrences in
# 8 files, one use and SEVEN comments warning against it, one of them the doc
# comment on the helper header that exists to be the one spelling. #970's shape
# exactly, where the same argument produced a scan rather than more prose.
add_test(
    NAME "istreambuf-iterator"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-istreambuf-iterator.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("istreambuf-iterator" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# And that check must be SEEN to refuse a use, and seen to stay QUIET over prose.
#
# Eleven synthetic trees. The red arm names file AND line; the quiet arm covers all
# three comment shapes this tree actually uses (`//`, `///`, and a `/* */` block
# without leading stars); a file that both explains the rule and breaks it is
# refused, which is the shape a partial fix leaves behind; and both fail-closed
# arms are driven -- the token gone from the tree entirely, and no C++ under src/.
#
# Two properties are asserted that a fixture like this usually misses. The
# enumeration MODE is asserted on both sides, with the git path staged for real via
# `git init` plus `git add`, because a synthetic tree is not a git repository and
# every case would otherwise exercise the directory walk while CI exercises git --
# which is how `check-catch-skip-return-code` had six green self-test cases and a
# red CI run with no contradiction between them. And one case plants `;`, `\`, `[`
# and `]` ABOVE a violation and asserts the exact `file:line`: that case failed on
# the first implementation, which used the house `pace_read_lines` splitting
# idiom, because a line ending in a backslash merges with the next one there -- the
# one case that reader's own comment claims it prevents. The merged element began
# with `//`, so the comment stripper ate the real code and the use was reported
# nowhere. A filename-only assertion passes under that bug.
add_test(
    NAME "istreambuf-iterator-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/istreambuf-iterator-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-istreambuf-iterator-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("istreambuf-iterator-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
)

# No first-party C++ calls a range algorithm `Core/Ranges.hpp` wraps directly --
# `std::ranges::iota` is `Ranges::Iota`, `std::ranges::fold_left` is `Ranges::FoldLeft`.
#
# AppleClang's own libc++ has no `std::ranges::iota`, and the one job that compiles with it,
# `Package (macOS .pkg)`, is not a required context: PR #1392 went red there with every
# required check green. So a direct call merges and breaks packaging afterwards, which is why
# this is in the DEFAULT set. The wrapped set is DERIVED from the header's selection blocks,
# the file set from git minus the third-party roots, and the header and its test are the only
# exemption.
add_test(

        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-ranges-seam.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("ranges-seam" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# No first-party C++ walks an enum's enumerators by hand now that `Enumerators<Enum>()`
# exists (#1476). Ten sites spelled the same walk before #1441 -- `views::iota` from zero to
# `EnumeratorCount<Enum>` plus a `static_cast<Enum>(index)` in the body -- and NOTHING refused
# it, which is why there were ten: the guidelines forbid C-style loops and ask for range
# views, and every one of those ten obeyed both, because `views::iota` IS a range view. The
# defect was walking indices to reach enumerators, and no existing check could see it.
#
# In the DEFAULT set, since the shape builds everywhere and fails no leg. The refused shapes
# are line-local; a `static_cast` near an `EnumeratorCount` is deliberately NOT one of them,
# measured -- six such sites in this tree are bounds checks on a value off a wire and every
# one is correct. The seam's own existence is the ANCHOR: with `Enumerators` gone the check
# refuses rather than reporting a clean tree.
add_test(
    NAME "enumerator-walks"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-enumerator-walks.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("enumerator-walks" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# `pace_tracked_files` is what every enumerating `check-*.cmake` now reads its file set
# through (#1485), so a defect in it is a defect in all of them at once. Consolidating eight
# byte-identical copies removed eight chances of a silent miscount and ADDED one, and a single
# point of failure with no guard on it has moved the risk rather than removed it.
#
# In the DEFAULT set: it starts no daemon, opens no socket and needs no compiler, and the
# defect class it guards -- an enumeration covering a different set than the rule it enforces
# claims to -- is invisible in every downstream verdict, which all stay green.
#
# It needs git: two of the three modes are git's, and it REFUSES rather than reporting zero
# failures when there is none, because a run that silently exercised only the directory walk is
# the very shape (the mode under test not being the mode in use) this repository has paid for.
add_test(

# The OTHER single point of failure in `scripts/lib/CheckCommon.cmake`: eight checks read C++
# through `pace_strip_comment_line`, so a defect there is a defect in all of them at once,

# a request literal whose `Accept: */*` contains a `/*` opened a block comment nothing closed,
# and the 51 lines after it, a `for (;;)` among them, were invisible to a scan that reported a
# clean count over lines it had never read (#1452 found it; the shape is #1494).
#
# In the DEFAULT set: a pure function over one line, no daemon, no socket, no compiler, no git.
#
# It runs its case table against the shipped library AND against six copies with one decision
# removed from each, because every case in it passes over a stripper that returns its input
# unchanged. A neuter that provokes nothing is reported as this file's own failure.

    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
