# A retained wall clock is a `WallClockRef`, never a raw `IWallClock` pointer or
# reference member (#1032). The TYPE is the guard -- `WallClockRef` refuses to be built
# from an rvalue, and the `static_assert` pairs in `Core/WallClockRef_test.cpp` and
# `AdminEndpoint_test.cpp` are what pin that, including the FORWARDER case a deleted
# rvalue overload on the storing type would have passed.
#
# This scan covers the one thing the type cannot: a new class declaring a raw
# `IWallClock const* _wall` and never reaching for `WallClockRef` at all. An opt-in set
# of guarded constructors reads identically to complete coverage (#492), so it is
# deny-by-default with one stated exception, and it refuses when it finds no
# `WallClockRef` anywhere -- two empty lists agree perfectly.
#
# **What it costs, so a slow run is diagnosed rather than guessed at.** ONE `awk` pass
# over the whole file set plus two `sed`s to split the result -- a handful of processes
# regardless of how many sources there are. Measured 2026-09-09, and the conditions are
# half the figure: **687 files, ~0.46 s per run, spread ~9 ms over three runs**, Git Bash
# on a 32-core Windows host, tree on local NTFS. On a hosted Windows runner, **2.11 s and
# 2.44 s** on the `Windows-cl-debug` and `Windows-cl-release` legs -- four cores at
# `CTEST_PARALLEL_LEVEL: 4`, the values `build.yml`'s `env:` block carried when these
# figures were taken. **Those two numbers are PINNED here rather than pointed at**, and
# the never-restate rule does not reach them: a condition that TRACKED its source would
# make this measurement claim a parallelism it was never taken under, the day somebody
# edits that file -- a false condition welded to a real figure, by an unrelated change,
# with no signal. Citations get linked; measurements get pinned. Treat those as two
# rows, never as "the scan is fast".
#
# **It was not always this shape, and the history is the reason the budget exists.** It
# used to spend THREE processes per file -- an `awk` to strip block comments and two
# `grep`s over the stripped text -- so 687 sources cost 2061 processes and ~60 s here.
# When this change was in flight master still carried that shape, which made master its
# own control under identical conditions: six hosted readings -- four runs of
# `Windows-cl-debug`, plus the three Windows legs of one run -- span **97.39 to 114.64 s
# against this 120 s budget**. It took required contexts red repeatedly, including on a
# diff that changed nothing but a comment;
# [#1150](https://github.com/LASTRADA-Software/pace/issues/1150) carries that
# record and it is deliberately not tallied here, a hand-kept count beside a moving fact
# being a second source of truth.
#
# **Read the spread against the margin; that is what a ratio hides.** Those six readings
# span **17.25 s**, and the slowest left **5.36 s** of headroom. A fluctuation of a size
# already observed between those very runs is therefore more than three times the margin
# of the slowest one -- so which leg crosses 120 s is not a property of the leg, and
# every leg sat within one ordinary fluctuation of the budget. A scan whose cost tracks
# the FILE COUNT rather than the diff lands back there if the per-file process ever
# comes back, which is why the shape is written down and not just the number.
#
# Two responses are tempting and both are wrong. **Raising the budget** makes a slow
# host pass and explains nothing, which is what #354 refuses -- and a budget is what
# separates a slow host from a wedged process. **Attributing a timeout to whatever
# branch is under it** needs the file-set delta checked first: this scan reads the tree,
# not the diff, so a change adding a handful of files moves its cost by well under the
# run-to-run spread. That check is two commands and it settles attribution before
# anybody reads a diff for a cause that is not in it.
add_test(
    NAME "wall-clock-borrow"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-wall-clock-borrow.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
)
set_tests_properties("wall-clock-borrow" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# Eight cases -- four properties in BOTH file-set modes, because a synthetic tree is not
# a git repository and a self-test that only ever built one would exercise the walk path
# while CI exercises git. Each case asserts the mode it ran in.
#
# Both directions are shown red by neutering, and the first attempt failed that: the
# comment case passed with comment handling REMOVED, because the match is anchored at the
# start of a line and a `//` comment can never reach it. What the stripping is actually
# for is a declaration quoted inside a `/* ... */`, which does sit at the start of its
# own line. The fixture is that now, and removing the handling reddens exactly it.
add_test(
    NAME "wall-clock-borrow-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-wall-clock-borrow.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("wall-clock-borrow-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
)

# clang-tidy's HeaderFilterRegex takes every tracked first-party header in each spelling a
# supported build opens it by -- POSIX, all-backslash, and MSVC's backslash include directory
# with a slash include -- and no spelling of a dependency path. Registered on EVERY platform
# and needing no build: the question is about Windows, and a check that ran only where the
# answer was already right would report nothing where it is asked.
#
# Before this, `.clang-tidy` spelled every separator `/`, and a Windows tidy run discarded
# every header finding while reporting clean. The self-test plants that filter (red, naming
# both backslash spellings) and the widening a separator fix tempts, `[/\\]src[/\\].*` (red,
# naming catch2), beside the configured one (green) -- and it reads the configured one, so it
# goes red too when `.clang-tidy` regresses. Its predicate and spellings are
# `scripts/lib/header-filter.sh`, which `local-gate.sh`'s coverage check shares: this asks at
# the real root on every platform, the gate asks the same thing before its tidy sweep.
add_test(
    NAME "tidy-header-filter"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tidy-header-filter.sh"
)
set_tests_properties("tidy-header-filter" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)
add_test(
    NAME "tidy-header-filter-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tidy-header-filter.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("tidy-header-filter-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# The macOS package fixture's failure-path diagnostic, driven against a SYNTHESISED
# log tree (#1055).
#
# The fixture itself needs root and a real .pkg, so ctest does not run it -- but its
# diagnostic is a pure function of a plist and a log file, and that part runs
# anywhere. It has to be tested this way: the dump fires only when the fixture has
# already failed, which was 2 of 30 completed runs, so a green CI run says nothing
# about it and waiting for it to fire is waiting for the bug to happen again.
#
# Seven cases over the states a naive `cat` renders identically -- no plist, no
# `StandardErrorPath` key, log absent, log present and EMPTY, log with content, and
# a truncated dump that must say so and must keep the END. Shown red by neutering:
# the naive implementation fails five of the seven, and dropping either single
# distinction fails exactly its own case.

)

# How close each `hygiene` test ran to its OWN timeout, as a distribution (#1143).
#
# The class this measures is not any one check: five unrelated hygiene checks failed
# together in one contended run, and all five appear in the TOP SEVEN of a run where
# none of them failed. A test at 95% of its budget PASSES, so nobody looks at it, and
# it is one slow runner from red -- which is invisible to every instrument that
# reports only what failed.
#
# What is registered here is the DECISION, not the measurement. Ranking a real suite
# needs a build directory and a quiet box, and a run on a shared one measures the box;
# `headroom_percent` needs neither and is where the state collapse would live. Its
# `no-budget` answer is the row that matters: a test carrying no TIMEOUT is one the
# ranking cannot place, and folding it into 0% would sort it as the safest thing in
# the table. Driven in both directions, including the two #1143 rows verbatim.
#
# The reporting half is deliberately not a ctest: it exits 0 whatever it finds, so
# there is no verdict for ctest to carry, and a number that can fail a build is a
# number people tune rather than read.
add_test(
    NAME "hygiene-headroom-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/hygiene-headroom.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("hygiene-headroom-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# What the node's option table says RELOADS, against what the shipped artefacts claim
# (#1026). `OptionSpec::reloadable` decides it and nothing derived either artefact from
# the table; it had already drifted, hand-corrected from four to five to seven in one
# branch, and #403, #293, #404 and #405 each added a row.
#
# Labelled `docs-subject` as well as `hygiene`: its subject IS prose, and prose drifts
# by being edited, which is the whole reason that label exists (#687).
#
# Both directions. A new row the docs omit costs a reader a stale page; a doc naming a
# setting as reloadable that the table denies costs an OPERATOR, who edits the shipped
# file, sends SIGHUP, sees no error and believes a setting is in force that is not.
# Cost: one `grep -l` across the tracked set rather than one `grep` per tracked file.
# Measured 2026-09-09 on the same host and shell as `wall-clock-borrow` above: ~0.64 s
# per run, spread ~26 ms over three runs, against the 120 s budget below. On a hosted
# Windows runner, **1.92 s and 2.08 s** on the `Windows-cl-debug` and
# `Windows-cl-release` legs, under the conditions stated for its sibling above. It used
# to spend a process per tracked file -- about a thousand -- and cost ~18 s here and
# **36.01 to 45.89 s** across those same six hosted readings, rising to 55.70 s in the
# run where its sibling timed out. On the shared 120 s budget that is 30-38% against its
# sibling's 81-96%, which is the whole of why one fired and the other did not
# ([#1150](https://github.com/LASTRADA-Software/pace/issues/1150)).
add_test(
    NAME "reloadable-docs"

)

# cannot contain a tool guarded nowhere, which is exactly the pre-bug state, and at
# `ed295468` such a set holds no `jq` at all. Optional tools are a short seed unioned

# row is a silent false negative.

# Key material must live in a container that zeroes its storage (#324).
#
# Registered because fixing the eight holders is not fixing the defect: a ninth is one
# declaration away and looks exactly like the eight did. It earned this row on its first
# real run -- a careful hand census of this same tree found SIX holders, and the scan then
# named two more in `FleetHarness.hpp` that the census had walked straight past.
#
# In the DEFAULT set rather than `smoke`, because it needs no daemon, socket or compiler,
# and a check that runs on one leg is a check that is not consulted.
#
# It reads NAMES, and what that cannot see is written on the script rather than implied
# here: a credential under a name its table does not carry is invisible to it. The
# compensating rule is that every row must still MATCH something -- a term that has quietly
# stopped matching is a guard that has quietly stopped guarding, and reads identically to a
# clean tree (#492). That arm is not decoration, and `clusterKey` is the worked example of it
# firing in BOTH directions: the table's first draft carried that row, the check refused the
# real tree because the row matched nothing, and the row came out. #1298 then gave the tree a
# `clusterKey` -- `EnrollReading` holds the secret an enrolling node is handed -- so the row
# went back in, and the same arm is what made #178 PR 6 take it out again with the key's last
# holder rather than leave a row guarding nothing. The reasoning for the row lives on the script beside the row itself and is not
# restated here; what this comment owes is the reason the ARM exists, which is unchanged.
#
# ## What this costs, on which shell, and what that means for the 300 s below (#1331)
#
# #1331 reported this at 296 s against the budget and timing out under `ctest -j3`, and
# asked for a baseline before anything was changed. Here it is -- taken twice, because
# the subject moved between the two and that is the most useful thing in it.
#
# Timed against `time.monotonic()`, never `SECONDS` or `date`, which are `CLOCK_REALTIME`
# and step on these hosts in both directions. Three standalone runs per row.
#
#                                        13baf8ca              563beb83
#                                   3 identifiers,        4 identifiers,
#                                    36 mentions           66 mentions
#   Linux, WSL2 5.15, ext4          3.68/3.60/3.56 s      6.56/6.52/6.40 s
#   Linux, WSL2 5.15, 9p /mnt/d     8.71/8.88/8.68 s     13.57/13.86/13.99 s
#   Windows 11 26200, Git Bash,
#     D: = ReFS                   261.40/287.31/354.80 s  620.45/496.42/444.45 s
#
# These conditions are PINNED, not pointed at. They are two states of one machine on
# 2026-09-12, and a measurement whose conditions track their source gets silently
# re-attributed by an unrelated edit to conditions it was never taken under. Each column
# names the commit it describes for the same reason.
#
# ## Read the two columns together, because the second is the point
#
# `13baf8ca` was the baseline #1331 asked for. `563beb83` is the same host four merges
# later: #1333 added a `clusterKey` row, which took the scan from 3 identifiers to 4 and
# from 36 matched mentions to 66, and the cost went up 1.6-1.8x with it. Nobody did
# anything wrong; the cost is simply proportional to (identifiers x files), so every row
# added here is paid for again on every run.
#
# ## The variable is the SHELL, not the filesystem
#
# This is the answer everyone reaches for here and it is wrong. ext4 to 9p is 2.1x, while
# Linux-on-9p to Windows-on-ReFS is 33-46x *with the filesystem arguably worse on the
# Linux side*. The scan makes 9193 external process spawns at `13baf8ca` -- measured by
# wrapping the tools on PATH and counting, not derived -- and a spawn costs 21.28 ms under
# Git Bash against 0.62 ms under WSL2, 34x. 9193 x 21.28 ms is 196 s, most of that
# column's runtime. Somebody optimising the filesystem would get nothing.
#
# ## The conclusion this comment used to draw was WRONG, and the correction is the
# ## most useful thing here
#
# It said: *"CI is nowhere near it -- 6.5 s against 300 s is 2.2 %. There is nothing to
# fix for the leg that gates a merge... The Linux legs are the ones that gate."*
#
# **Both sentences are false, and everything needed to predict that was in the paragraph
# above them.** The 6.5 s was measured on a LINUX leg and generalised to "CI". But the
# finding directly above is that the dominant term is the SHELL -- 21.28 ms per spawn
# under MSYS against 0.62 ms under WSL2 -- and GitHub's WINDOWS runners run that same
# MSYS shell. So the Windows *local* column was always the better predictor of the
# Windows *runner* than the Linux runner figure was, and `Windows-cl-release` and
# `Windows-clangcl-release` are both among the fourteen REQUIRED contexts. It read as
# settled because two of three conditions agreed; the third was the one that mattered.
#
# What the Windows CI leg actually did, run over run (measured from the job logs, not
# modelled), at the identifier count each head carried:
#
#   13baf8ca  177.70 s   3 identifiers
#   563beb83  210.06 s   4 identifiers
#   6e9b2456  231.14 s   4 identifiers
#   c6effe67  262.05 s   4 identifiers
#   8a9e6837  259.39 s   4 identifiers   <- master, 86.5 % of the budget
#   20931ad5  ***Timeout 300.01 s        <- blocked a merge on a required context
#
# The cost function explains the LEVEL rather than the rise. Dividing each leg's time by
# the process creations that head's tree implies gives 13.5-16.8 ms per creation across
# all five -- a model validated five times on a remote machine it was never fitted to.
# What it does NOT explain is the climb; see the note under the budget below.
#
# ## What the fix did, and what it is MEASURED IN -- which took two tries
#
# Two changes. `CodeLines` ran once per (file, identifier) and is a property of the FILE
# alone, so it is hoisted -- files outer, identifiers inner. And one pass now finds the
# files that mention any identifier at all, so the per-file work runs on those rather than
# on all of them: 19 files of 765 here.
#
# The pre-filter is safe for a reason worth stating, because it is the only interesting
# thing about it: a file with no whole-word mention of any identifier contributes zero to
# every per-identifier count and can produce no finding, so excluding it changes no number
# this check reports. It is a SUPERSET filter -- comments are not stripped in that pass,
# so a file mentioning an identifier only in a comment is still opened and still
# contributes zero once `CodeLines` has run.
#
# ### The first measurement of this used the wrong unit
#
# Counting TOOL spawns (by wrapping them on PATH) said the hoist alone was a 3.06x
# reduction -- 12,506 to 4,091 -- and predicted 69-86 s on CI. The CI leg measured
# 167.35 s against master's 252.25 s on the same leg: 1.51x.
#
# The gap was the instrument. A PATH shim cannot see a `$( )`, and a command substitution
# forks a subshell. MEASURED under Git Bash: `x=$(true)` costs 12.97 ms against 21.2 ms
# for an entire `grep`, while a builtin costs nothing. So a fork is most of a spawn, and
# the hoist had INCREASED the fork count while cutting tool spawns. The model and the
# counter agreed perfectly because they were the same incomplete idea counted twice; CI
# was the third, independent count, and it is the one that disagreed.
#
# The honest unit is PROCESS CREATIONS -- tool spawns plus subshell forks:
#
#                       tool spawns   forks   creations
#   original               12,506     3,061     15,567
#   hoist only              4,091     3,826      7,917
#   hoist + pre-filter        364        97        461
#
# Each reconciles with the structure: 19 candidates x 4 identifiers plus 66 mentions x 4
# owning types plus two `find`s and two `sort`s is 364, and the forks are one per
# `$( )` the loops execute.
#
# Two explanations were checked and REFUTED before that one was found, both by direct
# measurement rather than argument: awk is not expensive (22.9 ms against grep's 21.3),
# and a herestring is not expensive (21.8 against 21.2 reading the file directly).
#
# Local Git Bash on ReFS, same `time` instrument, 765 files x 4 identifiers: 267 s before,
# 18.5 s after. The residue is no longer process creation -- it is reading the 765 files
# once, which is what the check is for.
#
# ## The budget stays at 300 s
#
# Raising it is the move this project's timeout rule exists to refuse: the budget is the
# only thing that made any of this visible, and a number large enough for the old cost
# would have hidden the LEVEL as well as the crossings. Lowering it would redden a tree
# for a cost nobody pays. The scan got cheaper instead.
#
# And note what the CI series does NOT show, since the obvious reading is wrong: between
# `563beb83` and `8a9e6837` the scan's process count moved 0.13 % while the leg's time
# rose 23.5 %. Only the first step -- the `clusterKey` row, +35.7 % -- was the scan
# growing. The rest was the runner, whose per-creation cost varies 25 % across those five
# runs. That is why the LEVEL was worth attacking: runner variance is not fixable from
# here, and at 259 s a run needed to land only 16 % above the mean to cross 300 s.
#
# So: if you are on Windows and this entry times out, it is the shell and the identifier
# count rather than your branch -- **and it IS blocking you**, because the Windows legs
# are required contexts. Do not wait it out; the cost is proportional to
# (files x identifiers), so check whether your branch added either.
add_test(
    NAME "credential-containers"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-credential-containers.sh"
)
set_tests_properties("credential-containers" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
    TIMEOUT 300
)

# Six cases, both directions, and the ones with teeth are the negatives.
#
# Case 1 asserts the PASSING direction, which is the arm nobody writes: #1031 was a guard
# that refused every tree and stood for two days, because a guard nobody has watched ACCEPT
# is not known to work.
#
# Cases 3 and 4 are the two silent failures -- a vocabulary term that has stopped matching,
# and an empty population, both of which a naive check reports as clean.
#
# Case 6 pins the two FALSE POSITIVES this check produced on its first run against the real
# tree: `std::string` matched as a RETURN type on a line whose parameter was a span, and
# `std::string` matched inside `std::string_view`. Both are one mistake -- a pattern is
# broader than its author reads it as -- and both point at REFUTING a correct declaration,
# which is the direction that gets acted on because a finding looks like work.
add_test(
    NAME "credential-containers-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-credential-containers.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("credential-containers-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
    TIMEOUT 300
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The lane reaper's own self-test (#877, #591).
#
# `scripts/reap-my-gate.sh` exists because `pkill -f "local-gate.sh"` matches every
# lane's gate on this machine -- each worktree runs the same three script names, so a
# name match is GUARANTEED to hit somebody else's process, and it did twice in one
# night. The victim's gate then prints neither `LOCAL GATE PASSED` nor `GATE FAILED:`,
# so it reads as a crash in their own tree rather than as somebody else's kill.
#
# **The self-test IS the deliverable here, not evidence for it.** A reaper is defined
# by what it SPARES, and sparing is invisible: a reaper that killed everything would
# pass any test asserting only that the target died. So both directions are driven,
# and the sparing half is driven LIVE -- real processes, a real process table -- since
# the three traps the script is built around (`pgrep -f` is a regex whose `.` matches a
# `-`; `pgrep -f` matches its own argv while `ps | grep '[l]...'` does not; two
# worktrees here are one token apart) are all properties of real tools that a staged
# table cannot exhibit. The decision function itself is pure and injectable, so the
# branches no machine can arrange -- a process whose cwd is unreadable, and one that is
# the reaper's own ancestor -- are driven over staged facts in the same run.
#
# Registered with the FLAG PASSED rather than merely with the script named: a
# registration in the script's plain mode satisfies every grep for coverage and runs no
# case at all, which is how twelve cases guarding the apt sweep never executed once
# (#1160). `selftest-registered` refuses the plain shape for exactly that reason.
#
# Judged by its EXIT STATUS, like `local-gate-selftest` and `tsan-gate-selftest` above:
# a shell script chooses its own exit code, so the `cmake -P` rule -- that a verdict is
# read from OUTPUT because `message(WARNING)` exits 0 -- does not reach it, and
# `script-check-signals` scopes itself to `cmake -P` registrations accordingly. The
# `FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` is therefore REDUNDANT rather than load-bearing, and is
# here because the two channels cannot disagree by construction: only `_reap_expect`
# prints that marker, and only on a mismatch that also increments the counter the exit
# status is taken from. It cannot fire on a healthy run -- the tally line says `failed`
# in lower case and carries no colon.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason `local-gate-selftest` carries it: the script is POSIX.
# It USED to be load-bearing as well. Under Git Bash, `gate_ppid_of` asked
# `ps -o ppid=`, which MSYS2's `ps` answers with a usage error and no parent, so the
# ancestry walk stopped at `$$`, the caller was not protected, and the reaper killed its
# own invoker -- `and the WRAPPER that invoked it -- a candidate by cwd -- survives:
# expected 'alive', got 'gone'`, on 20 of 20 Git Bash runs. The ancestry and the process
# table are now read from `/proc/<pid>/stat`, which MSYS2 writes as Linux does; the
# self-test asks the parent lookup against `$PPID` so the next such platform fails a row
# of its own. Git Bash is exercised through `local-gate.sh --self-test`, which drives
# this self-test and runs there -- and there the reaper now REFUSES by design: on
# Cygwin/MSYS a parent of 1 is a native process, so no chain is walkable to its root,
# the real-process half is SKIPPED (exit 77) and only the native-intermediary refusal
# and the staged rows run.
#
# Measured 2026-09-29, three runs each, as a table of conditions rather than a number:
#
#   host                          checks  outcome        duration     clock
#   WSL2/Ubuntu-24.04, nice -n 10     23  passed         5.56-5.63 s  /proc/uptime (monotonic)
#   Git Bash (MSYS2), Windows 11       17  skipped (77)   1.14-1.25 s  date +%N (wall)
#   Git Bash, BEFORE the I1 refusal   23  passed         9.8 s        the reviewer's, wall
#
# Most of the WSL figure is the fixture's own 0.5 s stage delay (three staged
# processes) and the reaper's 2 s TERM grace. The 2026-09-10 figures (14 cases, 2.69 s
# quiet, 6.87 s under two other lanes' gates) predate the staging waits and are
# superseded. `TIMEOUT 60` is the figure its two neighbours carry rather than a new one,
# a margin of ~10x over the slowest reading here; a wall reading on this host can step,
# so treat the Git Bash rows as an order of magnitude, not a budget.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "reap-my-gate-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/reap-my-gate.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("reap-my-gate-selftest" PROPERTIES
        FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "REAP SELF-TEST FAILED:"
        LABELS "hygiene"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The worktree-pointer repair's own self-test (#336).
#
# `git worktree add` writes ABSOLUTE pointers in the dialect of whichever git created
# the worktree, so a tree created from Windows is unreadable to WSL git -- which then
# resolves the two paths CONCATENATED and reports a repository that exists nowhere.
# `local-gate.sh` needs git for its formatter list, its header-filter census and its
# test-registration walk, so in such a worktree every one of those answers NOTHING and
# the compiler-specific defects the gate exists to catch reach CI instead.
#
# What the self-test pins that no argument can: the repair is asserted against REAL
# git, by staging a linked worktree, rewriting both pointers relative, and MOVING the
# whole arrangement -- because "both gits read it" is a claim about a tool and is
# checked against the tool. It also pins the direction that looks like a fix and is
# not: `git worktree repair` rewrites the pointers absolute in the dialect of whichever
# git runs it, so it reproduces the defect from one side and creates it on the other.
#
# And the case with teeth is the last one, which asserts that a repair leaving the tree
# STILL UNREADABLE refuses -- having written the files. Writing the pointers is not the
# property; git reading the result is, and a check that stopped at "the files were
# written" would report success for a worktree nobody can use.
#
# Registered with the flag passed and judged by exit status for the reasons given on
# `reap-my-gate-selftest` above. `if(NOT WIN32)` is measured here too, and what fires
# is the self-test's own POSITIVE CONTROL: under Git Bash it reports `git wrote an
# ABSOLUTE pointer, which is what makes this necessary: expected 'yes', got 'no'`.
# Windows git writes `D:/...`, which is absolute and does not begin with `/`, so the
# control correctly says the whole section would be vacuous in that shell rather than
# letting it pass over a fixture whose premise does not hold. A control doing its job
# is the reason to guard the registration, not a reason to loosen the control.
#
# The script's two SKIP arms -- no git, and a git that could not stage a linked
# worktree -- return 77 and this registration reads it (#1231). They exited 0 having
# said so on the tally line until then, which is a `SUCCEED` where a skip belongs: the
# arm that fires is the one where the REAL repair, staged against real git, did not
# run, so the green arrived exactly on the hosts with the least coverage.
#
# The two halves land together and neither works alone. A script returning 77 with no
# `SKIP_RETURN_CODE` here is scored a FAILURE -- a false red, and whoever meets it
# deletes the 77 rather than suspecting the registration (#499's shape). A
# `SKIP_RETURN_CODE` here with a script that still returns 0 changes nothing at all.
#
# A failure recorded BEFORE the skip point still fails: the arms return 1 in that
# case, never 77, or a run that skipped the git half and failed the pure half would
# report as skipped.
#
# Measured 2026-09-10 on this branch, WSL2/Ubuntu-24.04: 23 cases, 0.06 s wall, neither
# skip arm taken. `TIMEOUT 60` is its neighbours' figure; the margin is enormous and
# the alternative is inventing a number nobody can check.
#
# Registered on EVERY platform. It was `if(NOT WIN32)` while its fixture's positive
# control knew only the POSIX spelling of an absolute pointer: Windows git writes
# `gitdir: C:/...` even under Git Bash, so the self-test failed one case on every
# Windows host and nothing registered it there to say so. The control now asks the
# script's own `pointer_is_absolute`, and Windows is where the defect this script
# repairs is CREATED, so it is the last platform to leave unchecked.
add_test(
    NAME "repair-worktree-pointers-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/repair-worktree-pointers.sh" --self-test
)
# `PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` as well, for `tidy-sweep-selftest`'s reason: an EARLY
# STOP exits 0 having judged nothing (a planted `return 0` printed nothing and ctest
# said Passed, measured), and the script prints its pass line only once the count of
# checks matched `RepairSelfTestChecks` and none failed. The skip arms' lines end
# `-- SKIPPED: ...` and cannot match it, and SKIP_RETURN_CODE still decides a skip.
set_tests_properties("repair-worktree-pointers-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "REPAIR SELF-TEST FAILED:"
    PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "REPAIR SELF-TEST PASSED"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The consumer of `check-merge-queue-contexts.sh`'s `RequiredContexts` table:
# which required contexts are blocking THIS pull request, by name (#542), and
# which of them reported a verdict and then had it thrown away with nothing
# queued to replace it (#903).
#
# APPENDED AT THE END OF THIS FILE ON PURPOSE, rather than beside the check whose
# table it reads. Several lanes append registrations here at once, and an
# append-only conflict resolves by keeping both blocks -- a resolution anybody can
# perform correctly, where an interleaved insertion is one nobody can. Proximity
# to `merge-queue-contexts` would read better and is the wrong trade while the
# file is contended; the coupling is stated here instead, and it is real: this
# tool READS that script's table rather than restating it, so a rename there is a
# failure here.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason `merge-queue-contexts` carries the same guard:
# the script is POSIX, and a Windows counterpart would be a translation rather
# than new coverage. What it checks is platform-independent and the Linux and
# macOS jobs run it.
#
# No `FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION`: that rule is about a `cmake -P` check, whose
# `message(FATAL_ERROR)` is heard through the diagnostic channel and whose
# `message(WARNING)` exits 0. This is a bash check behind `run-check.sh`, which
# passes the child's status through untouched and prints a terminal marker saying
# which of four things happened to it -- so the exit status IS the verdict here,
# and adding an output pattern beside it would assert a second one.
if(NOT WIN32)
    # The plain mode asserts the WIRING: the tool exists, it names the shared
    # table rather than restating it, and the table still yields rows -- counted
    # two ways, since two empty lists agree perfectly. That last one is not
    # ceremony: the text readers of that table used to admit its COMMENT lines as
    # context names, which put eight contexts no job produces into every
    # consumer's required set, while the authoritative count taken from bash's own
    # view of the array stayed right and hid it.
    add_test(
        NAME "pr-required"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-pr-required.sh"
    )
    set_tests_properties("pr-required" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )

    # And the verdicts, against synthesised check-run records. Every state #542
    # names is reachable from a file, so this needs no pull request, no network
    # and no clock -- which is why the DECISION takes a record and the acquisition
    # is a separate mode, the split `ci-merge-group-report.sh` uses.
    #
    # Both directions, and the positive one first: a fully green record must
    # report MERGEABLE, or every refusal below is evidence of nothing.
    add_test(
        NAME "pr-required-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-pr-required.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("pr-required-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
endif()

# The launcher's merged identity probe (#1237): `-###`'s first line must be
# byte-identical to `--version`'s, on every driver this machine has that the
# launcher would merge for.
#
# **This is what makes a fingerprint bump unnecessary, so it is not a test of
# #1237's diff and it does not retire with that ticket.** The banner is folded into
# the cache key and into the toolchain fingerprint a worker advertises, so a driver
# whose two spellings disagree stops every client matching every worker with no
# counter moving -- #226's failure mode. The alternative was to bump the fingerprint
# unconditionally, discarding every stored object to record a change that, if the
# lines really are identical, did not happen. Checking the property costs a few
# spawns; assuming it costs a fleet, and paying for it costs every stored object.
#
# NO `FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION`, and that is the decision rather than an omission:
# the verdict channel differs by RUNNER. A `cmake -P` check is judged by its OUTPUT
# and must carry one; a script behind `run-check.sh` is judged by its EXIT STATUS
# and must not. Adding one here would make a refusal depend on wording.
#
# `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77` is load-bearing. A host with no such driver cannot answer
# the question, and that is a third outcome beside pass and fail -- without this
# property ctest scores the skip as a FAILURE, which is #499's false red. It is the
# script-driven 77 convention and not the Catch2 `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 4`: a shell
# script chooses its own exit code, where a Catch2 binary exits with Catch2's fixed
# codes -- 4 when every case skipped, 42 on any failure.
#
# TIMEOUT 300 against a measured 3.0 s (WSL2 Ubuntu, 8 clang drivers and 6 GCC scope
# controls) and 3.4 s (Git Bash, 3 drivers), both 2026-09-11. The margin is for a
# loaded hosted runner, not for the work.
#
# Not guarded by `if(NOT WIN32)`, unlike its POSIX-script neighbours, and that is
# deliberate: `clang-cl` is one of the two flavors the launcher merges for and it
# exists essentially only on Windows. Skipping this leg there would take the
# ClangCl arm out of every run of the check, on the one platform where it lives.
add_test(
    NAME "banner-probe-identity"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-banner-probe-identity.sh"
)
set_tests_properties("banner-probe-identity" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
    TIMEOUT 300
)

# The check's own verdicts, driven over staged records, plus its acquisition control.
#
# The split exists because ACQUISITION cannot be staged: finding drivers, spawning
# them and capturing two streams needs a machine with compilers on it, so a check
# whose verdict logic were reachable only that way would be tested only where it was
# already going to run. That is `tidy-sweep.sh`'s `CanaryVerdict` argument (#257).
#
# Four verdict families, both directions each: the comparison, the name
# classification (a faithful copy of `ClassifyCompilerImpl`, asserted to accept
# `clang-format` exactly as production's rule does), this file's own narrower
# spawn-candidate rule, and the three-state run verdict -- where `RunVerdict 0 0` is
# `skip` and never `pass`, which is the arm no machine here would ever exercise.
#
# And the ACQUISITION CONTROL runs in both modes: two stand-in drivers, one whose
# spellings differ and one whose spellings agree, through the same capture function
# the real drivers use. Both arms are needed and they fail in opposite directions.
# It is not decoration -- the first version of this check word-split driver paths on
# spaces, spawned twelve nonexistent programs on a box with three compilers,
# captured two identical "No such file" messages and reported
# `12 driver(s) checked, 0 differing`. Every arm agreed perfectly, which is what a
# broken instrument looks like as well as what a real pattern looks like.
add_test(
    NAME "banner-probe-identity-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-banner-probe-identity.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("banner-probe-identity-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
    TIMEOUT 300
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A CMake `macro()`'s parameters are TEXT, substituted into the body before it runs,
# so `if(param STREQUAL "x")` compares the literal string `param` and the branch is
# dead. A `function()`'s parameters are real variables, so the identical line is
# correct there -- which is what makes this silent. CMake diagnoses nothing, and
# moving a body between the two forms changes its meaning with no test going red.
#
# `check-corrupt-store-diagnostics-selftest.cmake` had BOTH verdict arms dead this
# way: every case fell through to a substring check, and the two `want-pass` cases
# pass an empty substring, so they asserted NOTHING while reporting green.
#
# The scan fails CLOSED in three directions rather than reporting a quiet zero: an
# empty file set is refused, a `macro(` definition it cannot parse is refused BY NAME
# rather than leaving that body unexamined behind the same all-clear, and a tree in
# which it recognised no macro at all is refused -- a scan that has stopped
# recognising its subject otherwise vouches for every macro in the tree without
# reading one.
#
# In the default set with the other hygiene checks: it reads files and compares
# strings. Measured at 1c985538 on Windows 11 / Git Bash / native NTFS / CMake
# 4.3.1, minimum of five runs: **318 ms** over 74 files. The TIMEOUT below is not
# that measurement -- it matches the neighbouring hygiene checks and leaves room for
# a DrvFs checkout under WSL, where the per-file open, not this check, is the cost.
#
# No `SKIP_RETURN_CODE`, and its absence is deliberate: a status a registration does
# not claim is a status CTest is free to reinterpret (#1128). This check exits 1 on a
# refusal and has no skip path to want one.
add_test(
    NAME "macro-bare-parameters"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-macro-bare-parameters.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("macro-bare-parameters" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And the check above must be watched REFUSING and ACCEPTING, against 17 synthesised
# trees -- 8 it must accept, 9 it must refuse, each refusal asserting the phrase the
# refusal carries rather than the verdict alone. A guard nobody has watched accept is
# not known to work, which is #1031's lesson and cost two days.
#
# Two of those cases are about this file's own subject. A self-test for a scan over
# CMake source holds violating CMake source as DATA, and a line walk cannot tell code
# from a multi-line string containing code -- so the check exempts a REGION
# (`# macro-bare-parameter-scan: data-begin` / `data-end`), never a FILE, the trade
# `check-e2e-helpers.sh` already settled for the bash-3.2 scan. Regions must BALANCE:
# an unterminated `data-begin` silences every line below it while the run reports
# clean, which is this check's own subject one level up.
#
# The harness is a `function()` and MUST stay one. As a `macro()` its `want` parameter
# textually rewrote `${want}` inside the staged case bodies, so `if(${want} STREQUAL
# "yes")` reached the disk as `if(pass STREQUAL "yes")` -- valid CMake, not a
# violation, and the accepting verdict each case wanted. Four cases staged a corrupted
# subject and every one was green. No assertion phrased in the harness's own
# parameters can see that, because the substitution rewrites the expectation in
# lockstep with the subject; the file-scope staging assertion counts a literal `${`
# instead, which names no parameter and so survives. Armed, it reads 0 of 13 where a
# healthy run reads 11 of 16.
#
# In the default set: it writes small files and runs `cmake -P` seventeen times.
# Measured at 1c985538, same conditions, minimum of three runs: **864 ms**.
add_test(
    NAME "macro-bare-parameters-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/macro-bare-parameters-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-macro-bare-parameters-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("macro-bare-parameters-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The suite runner's verdict table (#1212).
#
# `scripts/run-suite.sh` exists because a test binary that CRASHES reports zero
# failures in every summary format there is, so a mutation harness reading the
# count scores a crash as *the mutation was caught* -- indistinguishable from a
# real result and pointing the wrong way. Its answer is drawn from three readings
# at once: the exit status, whether the report is COMPLETE, and how many cases it
# carries.
#
# What is registered here is the DECISION and not the acquisition. The
# acquisition needs a built binary and a crash that happens a few times in
# twelve, which is exactly the population a verdict table must not be reachable
# only from -- the same split `tidy-sweep.sh --self-test` and
# `node-scratch-isolation-e2e-selftest` record for themselves.
#
# Two of its ten cases are the reason the table has three inputs rather than one:
# `139 failing assertions with a complete report` and `killed by SIGSEGV with a
# truncated one` carry the SAME exit status -- as they did on Catch2 3.6.0, which
# spent its status on the failure count -- and only completeness tells them apart.
#
# `if(NOT WIN32)` for the reason `e2e-helpers-selftest` carries it: the script is
# POSIX, and a Windows counterpart would be a translation rather than new
# coverage. The property is platform-independent and the Linux and macOS jobs run
# it.
#
# APPENDED at the end of this file rather than beside a sibling: several lanes
# add registrations here at once, and an append-only conflict resolves by keeping
# both blocks, which is a resolution anybody can perform correctly.
if(NOT WIN32)
    add_test(
        NAME "run-suite-selftest"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-suite.sh" --self-test
    )
    set_tests_properties("run-suite-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Every Catch2 test BINARY is sanitized or exempt with a written reason (#1209).
#
# `tsan-scope-hygiene` above asks whether a scoped test CASE carries a tag the
# gate selects. That is a question about cases inside binaries the gate already
# runs, so it could not see `pace-cc-tests`, which was in no row at all: the
# launcher does not link the Pace library, so no tag expression could ever
# have reached it.
#
# Derived, not tabulated. The set comes from the `catch_discover_tests(`
# registrations under `src/`, and the covered set from the gate's own `TARGETS`
# table read through `check-tsan-scope.cmake` -- so this check holds a copy of
# neither. That matters because #1209's own hand census listed four test binaries
# where the tree registers six.
#
# In the default set: it reads twelve CMakeLists.txt files and spawns nothing.
add_test(
    NAME "tsan-binaries"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tsan-binaries.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("tsan-binaries" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# That check's own decisions, driven over synthetic trees.
#
# `tsan-binaries` runs it against THIS tree, where a green run is equally
# consistent with the check working and with its reader having quietly stopped
# matching -- and the subject IS a binary nobody noticed, so those two produce the
# same clean line. The comment-stripping pair is the sharp case: a reader that
# strips nothing and a reader that strips everything both pass the refusing arm,
# and only the accepting twin separates them.
#
# The trees are staged from the shipped exemption rows and from the real
# `scripts/tsan-gate.sh`, including one case that DELETES a gate row and requires
# the binary it named to go uncovered -- which is what proves the check reads the
# gate rather than carrying its own idea of what runs.
add_test(
    NAME "tsan-binaries-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/tsan-binaries-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-tsan-binaries-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("tsan-binaries-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The local gate's optional-target set matches CI's (#1144, #1197).
#
# A ctest total is comparable only against the same tree, the same platform AND
# the same target set. `PACE_BUILD_TESTCLIENT` and
# `PACE_BUILD_BENCHMARKS` default OFF, so one commit gives two different
# totals depending only on which `-D` flags were passed -- and the smaller number
# does not look wrong, the tree just looks smaller.
#
# #1144 turned them on in `scripts/local-gate.sh` and kept them in a table, then
# cited `ctest -R gate-target-set` as already refusing the drift. That test did
# not exist anywhere in the tree. This is it.
#
# Both sides are derived -- the gate's set from `gate_target_flags`, CI's from the
# `-D` flags in `build.yml` -- plus a third derivation that catches what neither
# can see alone: a flag name that gates nothing, since a misspelled `-D` sets an
# unused cache variable and changes no target.
#
# In the default set: it reads four files and spawns nothing.
add_test(
    NAME "gate-target-set"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-gate-target-set.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("gate-target-set" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# That check's own decisions, driven over synthetic trees.
#
# `gate-target-set` runs against THIS tree, where the two sides agree -- so it
# prints the same clean line whether the comparison is live or the reader has
# stopped matching, and the defect it exists for is invisible on a tree that has
# never drifted.
#
# Each case copies the four real files and mutates one, asserting its anchor
# matched EXACTLY once -- count rather than presence, so `missing` and `not
# unique` both fire. A mutation that anchored on nothing stages an unchanged tree
# and the case then passes or fails for a reason unrelated to the rule.
#
# One case caught a defect in the check while it was being written: the app-table
# marker was a prefix, so `set(PACE_APPS_RENAMED` still matched it and the
# renamed-table case could not fire.
add_test(
    NAME "gate-target-set-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/gate-target-set-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-gate-target-set-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("gate-target-set-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The tracked mutation harness and its self-test (#1252).
#
# `.agent/rules/testing.md` makes *prove the test can fail* an acceptance step
# for much of this repository's work, and until #1252 every lane wrote its own
# harness for it. A derived census over every session scratchpad and every
# worktree found 54, diverged three ways on restore alone, and four separate
# defects had been paid for separately -- the worst of which does not fail
# loudly: it reports that a test did NOT redden under mutation, on which reading
# the author's correct next move is to DELETE the test, since not reddening is
# #355's disqualifying property. A harness bug destroys the test that would have
# caught the next regression while every signal says the test is bad.
#
# Only the SELF-TEST is registered. The harness is a tool a developer points at
# a subject; running it here would need a subject, a build and a verdict command
# that this tree cannot supply generically, and a check that can only run where
# a full build is already going is unreachable for the population it is for
# (#257's argument for `tidy-sweep.sh`'s canary).
#
# The case that matters is `stale-binary`, which is #1252's clause 2 and the
# clause the ticket calls the whole ticket: the restore-rebuild rule needs a test
# that FAILS when the rebuild is deleted. It drives a COPY of the harness with the
# post-restore rebuild textually removed and requires it to abort naming a stale
# binary; `stale-binary-ctl` runs the unmodified harness over the same staged tree
# and requires a clean pass, without which "aborts when the rebuild is deleted"
# and "aborts on this tree" would be one passing test.
#
# Python, and it reuses the `find_package(Python3)` above rather than asking
# again -- a third spelling of "find python" would disagree with the other two
# about which interpreter it means, which is the reason that block gives for not
# using `find_program`. Verdict from the EXIT CODE and not
# FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION: that property belongs to `cmake -P` checks, whose
# `message(WARNING)` exits 0, and this script chooses its own status the way
# `fetch-transfer-bound` does.
#
# It needs no compiler, no daemon, no socket and no network -- the staged subject
# is a two-line "compiler" deriving a binary from a source -- so it is `hygiene`.
if(NOT Python3_Interpreter_FOUND)
    # A WARNING, not a STATUS. The outcome is that the guard on the tool this
    # repository discharges "prove the test can fail" with is ABSENT from this
    # build tree, and absent is not the same as passing.
    message(WARNING
        "mutation-harness-selftest: no Python 3 interpreter found, so the tracked "
        "mutation harness (#1252) is NOT guarded in this build tree")

    # Registered as a SKIP so the row exists either way (#1570). The WARNING above
    # is kept rather than replaced: it is the louder of the two, and a configure
    # warning and a skipped row answer different questions -- one is read once by
    # whoever configured, the other by every count taken off `ctest -N` afterwards.
    pace_register_skipped_test("mutation-harness-selftest"
        "no Python 3 interpreter found, so the tracked mutation harness (#1252) is NOT guarded in this build tree")
else()
    add_test(
        NAME "mutation-harness-selftest"
        COMMAND "${Python3_EXECUTABLE}"
            "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-mutation-harness-selftest.py"
    )
    set_tests_properties("mutation-harness-selftest" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        TIMEOUT 300
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Every `RequiredContexts=(` declaration says at its own site what it is (#1360).
#
# Four files under `scripts/` declare that array and three are self-test fixtures. The
# 11-row one in `check-merge-group-report.sh` is a strict SUBSET of the live 14-row table
# whose every row is a real context name, which is the shape that reports "11 of 11
# passed" and trips nothing -- a lane's hand-rolled reader did exactly that, on a pull
# request that had already merged.
#
# `ci-pr-required.sh` already refuses when not ONE check run matches a required name, and
# that guard cannot catch this: 11 of 11 matched real contexts. Its real protection is
# that it pins the source FILE, so the defect is choosing the wrong file -- which nothing
# inside a reader can detect.
#
# Needs no compiler, no daemon, no socket and no network; it reads `scripts/`.
add_test(
    NAME "required-context-table"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-required-context-table.sh" --root "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
)
set_tests_properties("required-context-table" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# Sixteen cases, both directions for every rule. The ACCEPTING arms are not decoration:
# a guard that refuses every tree passes every negative case, and one stood here for two
# days (#1031). Case 13 is the accepting arm of the data-REGION mechanism, which is the
# one part of this check that can reduce its own coverage.
add_test(
    NAME "required-context-table-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-required-context-table.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("required-context-table-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The markup escaping convention has one home and nothing else spells its entities
# (#1359).
#
# Three targets implemented it — the fleet page's table, the launcher's `EscapeHtml`
# switch and the plist writer's `XmlEscape` switch — and the two that AGREED were the
# library and the launcher, while the one sharing a target with the table was the one
# that differed. Converging them put the escaper in `Core/Markup.hpp`, which is where
# `Platform/` can reach it without depending on `Distributed/`.
#
# WHY THIS SHAPE, because the obvious guard is vacuous here. #1334 shared a TABLE, so a
# consumer could keep its own walk and drift from the rows — which a test walking the
# table catches. This shared the FUNCTION, so that drift cannot happen and such a test
# would keep passing however many new escapers appeared beside it. What survives
# consolidation is somebody writing a NEW escaper, and the only instrument that sees
# that is the census that found the three sites originally: look for the emitted
# ARTEFACT rather than for the implementation shape.
#
# In the DEFAULT set: no compiler, no daemon, no socket, and the failure it guards is
# silent — a second escaper works fine until the day the two disagree.
add_test(
    NAME "markup-entities"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-markup-entities.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("markup-entities" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# And that check must be seen to refuse a second escaper AND to accept the prose.
#
# Fifteen synthetic trees. The accepting arm is the load-bearing half: this check's
# whole subject is string literals that appear correctly in a comment explaining the
# rule, in four test files asserting what the escaper emits, and in one page of
# hand-escaped documentation — so a version that refused those could be satisfied only
# by deleting a comment or an assertion, and would be deleted instead.
#
# The retired `&apos;` has its own arm, refused BY NAME rather than as an ordinary
# duplicate: its return is the drift in the exact shape it took last time. Both
# fail-closed arms are driven, and so is a STALE exemption row — an opt-out whose
# subject has gone is worse than none, because it reads as a considered decision while
# covering nothing.
#
# Two defects the check had before this fixture existed are cases here so they cannot
# come back: `&lt;` and `&gt;` each contain a `;`, CMake's list separator, so a list of
# the full spellings is silently ragged; and the table is indented inside `Detail`, so
# a home signature anchored at column 0 found nothing. Both were caught by the check's
# own fail-closed guards rather than by review.
add_test(
    NAME "markup-entities-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/markup-entities-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-markup-entities-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("markup-entities-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 240
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Nothing but `TextCell` itself builds a `CellKind::Text` cell (#1356).
#
# That enumerator means *these bytes are valid UTF-8*, and `--format=json`
# interpolates a cell's text into a document that has to be UTF-8 end to end.
# `TextCell` is where the property is established -- it asks `IsValidUtf8` and
# answers with a base64 `Binary` cell when the answer is no.
#
# The question used to be asked by a SECOND function whose doc comment claimed to
# be "the one place the UTF-8 question is asked, so no command can forget to ask
# it". That sentence was false as written and its falseness WAS the defect: four
# sites called that function and about twenty-nine called `TextCell`, three of them
# with bytes off the wire -- a memcached key, `ME`'s key, a meta flag value -- so a
# non-UTF-8 KEY rendered as raw bytes. The fix folded the question in, which is the
# stronger half of the rule that a guard folded INTO an operation is
# self-enforcing while one called ALONGSIDE needs a scan.
#
# This is the residual the folding cannot reach: `Cell` is an aggregate, so
# `Cell { .kind = CellKind::Text, ... }` compiles anywhere and asks nothing. In the
# DEFAULT set because it needs no compiler, no daemon and no socket, and because
# the failure it guards is silent -- a wrong key renders as a plausible one.
add_test(
    NAME "cli-text-cell"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-cli-text-cell.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("cli-text-cell" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# And that check must be seen to refuse a bypass AND to accept the reads.
#
# Seventeen synthetic trees. The accepting arm is not the decorative half here:
# `kind == CellKind::Text` differs from an assignment by one character and
# `case CellKind::Text:` is how every renderer consumes the model, so a check that
# refused either would be satisfied only by deleting a renderer -- and would be
# deleted instead. #1356's acceptance clause asks for both directions in as many
# words.
#
# Three bypass spellings are driven separately, because they reach one hole and a
# pattern that saw only the designated initialiser would report clean over the
# positional aggregate. Both fail-closed arms are driven too: the factory no longer
# writing a shape the scan recognises, and the enumerator renamed so nothing reads
# it -- each of which produces a result indistinguishable from a clean tree unless
# it is reported.
#
# The enumeration MODE is asserted on both sides, with the git path staged for real
# via `git init` plus `git add`: a synthetic tree is not a git repository, so every
# case would otherwise exercise the directory walk while CI exercises git. And one
# case plants `;`, `\`, `[` and `]` ABOVE a violation and asserts the exact
# `file:line`, since a filename-only assertion passes when lines merge.
add_test(
    NAME "cli-text-cell-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/cli-text-cell-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-cli-text-cell-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("cli-text-cell-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
)

# pacectl asks neither the environment nor the terminal anything itself (#134 §9.11).
#
# `live-stats` is HANDED what a terminal can draw (`TerminalCapabilities`) and reads the
# environment only through `Platform/Environment` from `main`. A `getenv`, `isatty`,
# `ioctl` or `GetConsoleMode` under `src/apps/pacectl/` draws the right frame on the
# machine it was tested on and passes every behavioural test, so the clause is a scan.
# Exemptions are rows with reasons in `scripts/cli-ambient-read-exemptions.txt`, and a row
# that no longer describes the tree is refused. It fails closed: the same scanner must
# recognise a read in `src/Pace/Platform/`, where these calls live.
#
# DEFAULT set: no compiler, no daemon, no socket.
add_test(
    NAME "cli-ambient-reads"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-cli-ambient-reads.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("cli-ambient-reads" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# And that check must be seen to refuse a planted read of each token, to accept prose, and
# to refuse a stale, reasonless or malformed exemption row -- in both enumeration modes,
# since a synthetic tree takes the directory walk while CI takes git.
add_test(
    NAME "cli-ambient-reads-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DGIT_EXECUTABLE=${GIT_EXECUTABLE}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/cli-ambient-reads-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-cli-ambient-reads-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("cli-ambient-reads-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
)

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# vendor/ is still byte-for-byte what was imported (#134).
#
# A copy under vendor/ is third-party source taken verbatim from upstream, and
# it is only worth having while it stays diffable against upstream -- `git diff
# <import>..HEAD -- vendor/monocypher` is the patch that gets contributed back. An edit
# applied in place breaks nothing here, which is exactly why nothing else would
# catch it: the build stays green and the loss surfaces months later, when
# somebody tries to upstream a fix and finds the diff full of local noise.
#
# In the default set with the other hygiene checks: it hashes files and compares
# strings. No compiler, no daemon, no socket -- and no git either, so it answers
# in a source export too, which is why the manifest holds SHA-256 rather than git
# blob hashes.
add_test(
    NAME "vendor-verbatim"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-vendor-verbatim.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("vendor-verbatim" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 300
)

# The copy's size, the supporting files beside `tui` and how many are headers were prose maintained by

# local change added a test file). Recomputed from the tree and `vendor/MANIFEST`; a stated figure that
# differs, or a figure phrase the check cannot find, is refused. `docs-subject` as well as `hygiene`:

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# cli-node-flags: `pacectl --help` sends operators to flags that exist.
#
# The client's NOTES name the operator actions that live only in
# `pace-node` -- enrollment and `--print-surfaces` (#1307). That is prose
# in one binary about another binary's option table, and nothing else connects them: a
# flag renamed in `NodeConfig.cpp` would leave the help pointing at nothing while every
# unit test in both binaries stays green. So the check asks both BINARIES, and requires
# every flag a paragraph naming the node cites to be an option row of the node's own
# `--help`.
#
# Registered HERE and not beside either app because it names two targets, and
# `src/apps` walks its table in order: a `$<TARGET_FILE:>` guard on a target configured
# later does not fail, it silently skips the test forever. Both targets are optional,
# and `$<TARGET_FILE:>` on one that was not built is a hard error at generate time, so
# the registration is guarded on both.
#
# Not POSIX-only: it runs two `--help`s and reads their text with CRs stripped. Measured
# before this was written, and no further: through Git Bash, a Windows `pacectl.exe`
# (whose help carries CRs) was captured and judged, and the CRLF case is in the self-test.
if(NOT TARGET pacectl OR NOT TARGET pace-node)
    message(STATUS "cli-node-flags: client or compile node not built -- skipping")
else()
    add_test(
        NAME "cli-node-flags"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-cli-node-flags.sh"
            --cli "$<TARGET_FILE:pacectl>"
            --node "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-node>"
    )
    set_tests_properties("cli-node-flags" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 60
    )
endif()

# And the verdicts, over staged help texts: both directions, a flag named only in
# another row's prose, a prefix of a real flag, CRLF input, and every way the reader
# can fail to LOOK -- since an empty citation list agrees with any node perfectly.
add_test(
    NAME "cli-node-flags-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-cli-node-flags.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("cli-node-flags-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Which files are this project's own is ONE answer: `scripts/lib/third-party-roots.txt`
# (#1370).
#
# Importing `vendor/` for #134 needed five tree-wide enumerators told about it one at a
# time, and three of the five were found by review rather than by the import. An
# enumerator that had not been told INCLUDED the vendored tree, so upstream code was
# linted, formatted, scanned and counted as first-party, silently and green. So the roots
# are one data file, read by a bash 3.2 reader and a `cmake -P` reader, and every script
# that lists the repository's files asks it and names what it declined.
#
# The set of enumerators is DERIVED, never listed: the check finds every site spelling a
# tree-wide walk (`git ls-files` with no directory, a root-level `GLOB_RECURSE`, `find` or
# `grep -r` over the repository root) and refuses one in a file that calls no reader and
# has no exemption row carrying a reason. A stale exemption, a roots file that is missing,
# empty or malformed, a root naming no directory and a root `.clang-format-ignore` does
# not name are all refused. The rule text travels beside each spelling's count.
#
# In the default set: it reads files and compares strings.
add_test(
    NAME "third-party-roots"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-third-party-roots.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("third-party-roots" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# And that check must be SEEN to refuse: one planted enumerator of each spelling that does
# not read the roots, each refused by `path:line`; the shapes its first versions got wrong
# (a literal-name recursive glob, a comment read as a call, a CMake pathspec on the next
# line, a continued shell line); every exemption and roots-file refusal; and the two
# readers driven over the same files, required to agree. The bash reader runs under the
# bash every other script test here uses.
add_test(
    NAME "third-party-roots-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/third-party-roots-selftest"
        "-DPACE_BASH=${PACE_BASH}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-third-party-roots-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("third-party-roots-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A Catch2 assertion made off its test case's thread ends the process (#1211).
#
# `OffThreadAssertionGuard.cpp` is an INTERFACE source of the Catch2 target (the top-level
# CMakeLists.txt), so every executable that links Catch2 compiles it. Two registrations
# prove it, and they answer different questions:
#
#   * `off-thread-assertion-guard-coverage` reads the GENERATED link lines and refuses an
#     executable that links Catch2 without the guard's object -- the attachment's claim,
#     checked against the artefact rather than trusted.
#   * `off-thread-assertion-canary` asks the RUNTIME: a Catch2WithMain program that asserts
#     on its own thread and then on a helper thread, whose gate requires the accept first
#     and then the guard's own words at a non-zero exit. Not guarded to Debug, because the
#     guard is no `assert`: it acts in every build type, so it is watched in every one.
add_executable(off-thread-assertion-canary OffThreadAssertionCanary.cpp)
target_link_libraries(off-thread-assertion-canary PRIVATE Catch2::Catch2WithMain)
add_test(
    NAME "off-thread-assertion-canary"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_CANARY=$<TARGET_FILE:off-thread-assertion-canary>"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/off-thread-assertion-gate.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("off-thread-assertion-canary" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)
add_test(
    NAME "off-thread-assertion-guard-coverage"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DBUILD_DIR=${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}"
        "-DCONFIG=$<CONFIG>"
        -DANCHOR=PaceTest
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-off-thread-assertion-guard.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("off-thread-assertion-guard-coverage" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "SKIP: "
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)
add_test(
    NAME "off-thread-assertion-guard-coverage-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/off-thread-assertion-guard-coverage-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-off-thread-assertion-guard-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("off-thread-assertion-guard-coverage-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# No executable raises a modal error dialog on Windows (#1389).
#
# `cmake/ErrorPopups.cmake` attaches `ErrorPopupsAtStartup.cpp` to every executable by
# walking the build system. Two registrations prove it, and they answer different
# questions:
#
#   * `error-popup-coverage` reads the GENERATED files -- the walk's manifest, every
#     `CTestTestfile.cmake` and `build.ninja` -- and refuses an executable a test launches
#     whose link line lacks the object and which states no reason. On every platform: the
#     suppression only acts on Windows, but a walk that misses a target misses it
#     everywhere, and the Linux gate is where that is cheapest to see.
#   * `error-popup-canary` asks the RUNTIME. It links `Catch2::Catch2WithMain`, the
#     population the defect lived in, fails a Debug assert, and its gate requires the
#     process to END with the assertion's text inside a bound. Only an MSVC Debug build has
#     the dialog, so elsewhere it is registered as a skip naming that.
add_test(
    NAME "error-popup-coverage"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DBUILD_DIR=${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}"
        "-DCONFIG=$<CONFIG>"
        -DANCHOR=PaceTest
        "-DVARIABLE=${PACE_ERROR_DIALOG_VARIABLE}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-error-popups.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("error-popup-coverage" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "SKIP: "
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)
add_test(
    NAME "error-popup-coverage-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/error-popup-coverage-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-error-popups-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("error-popup-coverage-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)
# No MSVC-style link in a debug-info configuration is incremental (cmake/IncrementalLink.cmake
# says why, and what it costs). `incremental-link` reads the GENERATED build.ninja -- the
# module's configure output is its claim, the link line the artefact -- and SKIPs by name on a
# toolchain or configuration with no `/INCREMENTAL` to judge. Its self-test stages both
# directions, since a guard nobody has watched refuse is not known to work.
add_test(
    NAME "incremental-link"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DBUILD_DIR=${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}"
        "-DCONFIG=$<CONFIG>"
        "-DMSVC_STYLE=$<BOOL:${MSVC}>"
        -DANCHOR=PaceTest
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-incremental-link.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("incremental-link" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "SKIP: "
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)
add_test(
    NAME "incremental-link-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/incremental-link-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-incremental-link-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("incremental-link-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)
if(MSVC AND CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE STREQUAL "Debug")
    add_executable(error-popup-canary ErrorPopupCanary.cpp)
    target_link_libraries(error-popup-canary PRIVATE Catch2::Catch2WithMain)
    add_test(
        NAME "error-popup-canary"
        COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
            "-DCANARY=$<TARGET_FILE:error-popup-canary>"
            -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/error-popup-gate.cmake"
    )
    set_tests_properties("error-popup-canary" PROPERTIES
        FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
    # The PRODUCT policy, in both directions: this canary is built as a product binary is,
    # and its gate runs it with the variable and without.
    add_executable(error-popup-product-canary ErrorPopupProductCanary.cpp)
    set_target_properties(error-popup-product-canary PROPERTIES PACE_ERROR_POPUPS_POLICY "on-request")
    add_test(
        NAME "error-popup-product-canary"
        COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
            "-DCANARY=$<TARGET_FILE:error-popup-product-canary>"
            "-DVARIABLE=${PACE_ERROR_DIALOG_VARIABLE}"
            -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/error-popup-product-gate.cmake"
    )
    set_tests_properties("error-popup-product-canary" PROPERTIES
        FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
else()
    pace_register_skipped_test("error-popup-canary"
        "only an MSVC Debug CRT raises the assert dialog this canary proves gone, and this build is not one")
    pace_register_skipped_test("error-popup-product-canary"
        "only an MSVC Debug CRT has an assert report mode to route, and this build is not one")
endif()

# `vendor-verbatim` told a declared local change from an accidental edit only in its refusal
# TEXT: regenerating the manifest silenced it for any edit, declared or not (found landing
# #1375, the first local change this tree carries). The manifest now records each file's
# upstream hash and a regeneration carries it forward, and every file that differs from
# upstream must be named by a row of VENDOR.md's "Local changes" table. This drives synthetic
# trees through edits, regenerations, declarations and re-syncs, and asserts WHICH verdict each
# gets. Each case has been watched to fail under a mutant of the check that removes what it pins.
add_test(

        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/vendor-verbatim-selftest"

)
set_tests_properties("vendor-verbatim-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)
