# Code this project does not build under its own directory-scoped flags is sanitized in the
# OBJECTS, not only on a compile line (#134). Until the target-by-target block after
# `include(Sanitizers)` in the top-level CMakeLists.txt, the vendored TUI was declared before the
# directory-scoped sanitizer options and built with none, while the configure printed "Enabling".
# This reads the objects of each such target for the undefined init reference each enabled
# sanitizer leaves, beside a CONTROL object compiled in the same configuration with
# `-fno-sanitize=all`, which must come back without it -- so the reader is watched saying "absent"
# as well as "present". ASan and TSan only: UBSan leaves no per-object init to read, and MSVC has no
# `nm`.
#
# Two sources of such targets. Monocypher (#178), from the targets cmake/Monocypher.cmake names,
# unconditionally. And core-cpp (#1596), whose libraries that block instruments from its
# `CORE_CPP_TARGETS` property: read here through the ones this build is sure to compile. core-cpp's
# targets are EXCLUDE_FROM_ALL, so a library nothing links has no objects to read, and `core::tui`
# -- behind the terminal wait on the pool -- is the one `pacectl` links.
if((ENABLE_SANITIZER_ADDRESS OR ENABLE_SANITIZER_THREAD) AND NOT MSVC AND CMAKE_NM)
    set(_fcVendoredObjectTargets ${PACE_MONOCYPHER_TARGETS})
    if(TARGET core::tui)
        get_target_property(_fcCoreCppTui core::tui ALIASED_TARGET)
        list(APPEND _fcVendoredObjectTargets ${_fcCoreCppTui})
    endif()
    set(_fcVendoredObjects "")
    foreach(_fcVendoredObjectTarget IN LISTS _fcVendoredObjectTargets)
        list(APPEND _fcVendoredObjects "$<JOIN:$<TARGET_OBJECTS:${_fcVendoredObjectTarget}>,|>")
    endforeach()
    list(JOIN _fcVendoredObjects "|" _fcVendoredObjects)
    add_library(sanitizer-absent-probe OBJECT SanitizerAbsentProbe.cpp)
    target_compile_options(sanitizer-absent-probe PRIVATE -fno-sanitize=all)
    set(_fcSanitizerInitSymbols "")
    if(ENABLE_SANITIZER_ADDRESS)
        list(APPEND _fcSanitizerInitSymbols "__asan_init")
    endif()
    if(ENABLE_SANITIZER_THREAD)
        list(APPEND _fcSanitizerInitSymbols "__tsan_init")
    endif()
    list(JOIN _fcSanitizerInitSymbols "|" _fcSanitizerInitSymbols)
    add_test(
        NAME "vendor-sanitized"
        COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
            "-DPACE_NM=${CMAKE_NM}"
            "-DPACE_SYMBOLS=${_fcSanitizerInitSymbols}"
            "-DPACE_INSTRUMENTED=${_fcVendoredObjects}"
            "-DPACE_UNINSTRUMENTED=$<JOIN:$<TARGET_OBJECTS:sanitizer-absent-probe>,|>"
            -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-sanitized-objects.cmake"
    )
    set_tests_properties("vendor-sanitized" PROPERTIES
        FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# No first-party unit enables an instruction set for the whole unit (#1442): by a flag in a compile
# database, or by a target pragma in the tree. Each self-test drives its check in both directions and
# prints how many cases ran. The Package jobs ask the flags check through
# scripts/check-instruction-set-flags.sh, whose header says why; its self-test is the wrapper's.
add_test(
    NAME "instruction-set-flags-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/instruction-set-flags-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-instruction-set-flags-selftest.cmake"
)
# TIMEOUT 300 against a ~9.5 s warm run, which is deliberate and is not slack. The
# check spawns `cmake -P` per case and stages a compile database for each, so its cost
# is processes rather than work, and it loses to contention rather than slowing down
# gracefully: measured 9522 ms and 9994 ms warm on an idle host (its neighbours are
# target-pragmas 5176 ms, target-pragmas-selftest 1174 ms, the wrapper self-test 632 ms
# and the flags check itself 683 ms), and observed exceeding 60 s inside
# `scripts/local-gate.sh`, whose `ctest --parallel 8` runs it beside the other
# process-heavy hygiene checks while a build finishes. A budget is the wrong place to
# notice that, so the figure is stated here rather than left to be re-derived.
set_tests_properties("instruction-set-flags-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 300
)

# And the routes a compile database cannot see (#1447): the environment, a driver configuration
# file, a compiler's own default. Every executable compiles src/tests/InstructionSetBaseline.cpp
# (cmake/InstructionSetProbe.cmake), so every leg's BUILD is the passing direction; this drives the
# refusing one with this build's own compiler, which is the direction no build shows.

        "-DAPPLE_HOST=${APPLE}"
        "-DPROBE=${PACE_INSTRUCTION_SET_PROBE}"
        "-DSCRATCH=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/instruction-set-probe-selftest"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-instruction-set-probe-selftest.cmake"
)

    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# `pace-bench` states the build its figures came from (#1439).
#
# A timing is a quantity UNDER CONDITIONS, and the condition that decides whether it is
# a cost at all is the build. That binary used to print nothing about it, and a Debug
# bench prints a plausible number -- so while reviewing #1420 an MSVC `cl-release` log
# was read as "a Debug-build signature", and the only tell was the build directory's
# name.
#
# TWO registrations, because they answer two different questions and only one of them
# can be answered from inside the binary:
#
#   * `bench-build-banner` runs the DECISION -- `ReasonFor` and the renderers over
#     staged readings, both ways, since this host can only ever produce one arm.
#   * `bench-build-banner-streams` runs the BINARY and reads what came out of it: the
#     listener is registered at all, the banner is on stderr and not on the stdout
#     `bench/inproc_bench.py` parses, every figure is marked, and that document carries
#     no mixed content.
#
# Both guarded on `TARGET pace-bench` as well as on the option, because the two are
# independent and a `$<TARGET_FILE:>` naming a target this configure did not declare is
# a hard error at GENERATE time rather than a skipped test (#390).
if(TARGET pace-bench)
    # A plain `add_test` and deliberately NOT `catch_discover_tests`:
    # `scripts/check-tsan-binaries.cmake` derives "every Catch2 test binary this tree
    # registers" from that call and requires each to be a row of the ThreadSanitizer
    # gate or to carry an exemption whose claim is re-scanned -- and
    # `src/apps/pace-bench/` names `std::thread`, so no exemption there could be
    # honest. Registering this way would put a benchmark binary, scaling tier and all,
    # under TSan in order to run a handful of text-rendering cases.
    #
    # `[buildbanner]` is the only non-hidden, non-benchmark tag in that binary, so the
    # filter selects exactly those cases. The PASS pattern asserts a NON-ZERO number of
    # cases RAN: a tag that has stopped matching prints `No tests ran` and exits
    # non-zero, which reads as a failing subject rather than as an empty filter.
    add_test(
        NAME "bench-build-banner"
        COMMAND $<TARGET_FILE:pace-bench> "[buildbanner]"
    )
    set_tests_properties("bench-build-banner" PROPERTIES
        # `[(]` rather than `\(`: CMake reads `\(` in a quoted argument as an escape and
        # stores a bare `(`, which its regex engine then takes as a capture group -- so the
        # pattern stops requiring the literal parenthesis Catch2 prints. Measured, the
        # first spelling of this line failed on a run whose output contained the sentence.
        #
        # `PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` replaces the exit status as the verdict, which is why
        # the pattern is anchored on `All tests passed`: Catch2 prints that only when every
        # selected case passed, and a run that crashed before printing anything matches
        # nothing.
        PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "All tests passed [(][0-9]+ assertions in [1-9][0-9]* test cases?[)]"
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 120
    )

    # Python, reusing the `find_package(Python3)` above rather than asking again -- a
    # third spelling of "find python" would disagree with the other two about which
    # interpreter it means. The check needs an XML parser: "stdout parses" is a weaker
    # property than it looks, since a stray write between two elements leaves a document
    # that parses and carries text where only elements belong, and no `cmake -P` reader
    # can tell those apart.
    #
    # 77 is this file's script-driven convention for a missing prerequisite, which here
    # means the target is declared and was not built, so nothing ran.
    if(NOT Python3_Interpreter_FOUND)
        pace_register_skipped_test("bench-build-banner-streams"
            "no Python 3 interpreter found, so what pace-bench writes to each stream is NOT guarded in this build tree")
    else()
        add_test(
            NAME "bench-build-banner-streams"
            COMMAND "${Python3_EXECUTABLE}"
                "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-bench-build-banner.py"
                --binary "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-bench>"
        )
        # TIMEOUT is the outer supervisor rather than the measurement. Measured on this
        # tree's slowest configuration -- a clang-debug build with ASan and UBSan on, over
        # a 9p mount -- the whole run at one sample per benchmark cost 11 s; the budget is
        # for a cold two-core runner doing the same work.
        set_tests_properties("bench-build-banner-streams" PROPERTIES
            LABELS "hygiene"
            SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
            TIMEOUT 300
        )
    endif()
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The `[wire]` tag reaches every case in a wholly-wire file (#1285).
#
# APPENDED AT THE END OF THIS FILE ON PURPOSE. Several lanes append registrations
# here at once and an append-only conflict resolves by keeping both blocks, which
# anybody can perform correctly; an interleaved insertion is one nobody can.
#
# TWO registrations, and they pin the population from opposite sides. Neither is
# redundant and neither alone closes the ticket:
#
#   - `wire-tag-reach-count` RUNS the tag and pins how many cases ran. It catches a
#     tag deleted, a case removed or renamed out of the selection, or the binary
#     losing a translation unit -- the population shrinking.
#   - `wire-tag-reach` SCANS the source. It catches a case ARRIVING without the tag,
#     which the run cannot see: an untagged addition leaves the pinned count
#     untouched and the run green. That is exactly how the tag came to reach 69 of
#     119 cases in `CompileCacheWire_test.cpp` without anything going red.
#
# The measured drift, for whoever reads this next: the tag arrived partway through
# that file's life at 94412fe7 and was added in 17 later commits, never backfilled.
# 50 of the 119 cases carried no tag string at all, 43 of them below line 1200 --
# the file's older band. Nothing was wrong with any of those cases; they were simply
# unreachable by the selector everyone believed covered them.
add_test(
    NAME "wire-tag-reach"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-wire-tag-reach.sh" --root "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
)
set_tests_properties("wire-tag-reach" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# Four cases, and the ACCEPTING arm is first rather than last: a guard nobody has
# watched accept is not known to work either, and one that refused every tree would
# pass every refusing case. The third case pins the stated blind spot -- a `[wire]`
# in a full-line comment must not be counted, or a comment could mask a missing tag.
add_test(
    NAME "wire-tag-reach-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-wire-tag-reach.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("wire-tag-reach-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# The run, guarded on the TARGET as well as on anything else that makes it
# interesting: a `$<TARGET_FILE:x>` naming a target that was not built is a hard
# error at GENERATE time, which fails the whole configure and names CMake rather
# than the option the operator set.
if(TARGET PaceTest)
    add_test(
        NAME "wire-tag-reach-count"
        COMMAND $<TARGET_FILE:PaceTest> "[wire]"
    )
    set_tests_properties("wire-tag-reach-count" PROPERTIES
        # The count is PINNED, not merely non-zero. `[1-9][0-9]*` -- the idiom
        # `bench-build-banner` uses -- passes over any population at all, which is
        # the assertion that could not have failed while this ticket was open: 69
        # cases ran, and 69 is non-zero.
        #
        # 275 = 204 (CompileCacheWire) + 26 (RaftWire) + 17 (ClusterState)
        #     + 23 (DiscoveryWire) + 1 (RaftTypes) + 1 (SchedulerService)
        #     + 1 (MachineTicket) + 1 (MachineStanding) + 1 (IdentityKeyLabel).
        # The first four files are wholly wire subject and are the scan's table; the
        # last five carry wire cases among others, which is why the scan cannot cover
        # them and why this number is the only thing that watches them.
        #
        # `[(]` rather than `\(`: CMake reads `\(` in a quoted argument as an escape
        # and stores a bare `(`, which its regex engine then takes as a capture group
        # -- so the pattern silently stops requiring the parenthesis Catch2 prints.
        #
        # `PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` replaces the exit status as the verdict, which is
        # why the pattern is anchored on `All tests passed`: Catch2 prints that only
        # when every selected case passed, and a run that crashed before printing
        # anything matches nothing. No `SKIP_RETURN_CODE` here: every selected case
        # must PASS for the count to be the count, so a run that skipped is a failure
        # of this check, never a state to report.
        PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "All tests passed [(][0-9]+ assertions in 275 test cases[)]"
        LABELS "hygiene"
        TIMEOUT 300
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# `launcher-replay-e2e` exists as a ctest NAME (#1254, absorbing #1228).
#
# It was registered by nothing. `ctest -R launcher-replay-e2e` selected no tests
# and exited **0**, which is the worst shape a check can have: it answers, it
# answers green, and there is nothing there. AGENT.md and two rules files quote
# this fixture as a live instrument -- "builds three times: cache-off control,
# cold (stores), warm (REPLAYS), and runs the replayed binary" -- so a reader
# checking that claim the obvious way was told it passed.
#
# Its single executor tree-wide was `build.yml`'s "A real target, replayed from
# cache" step, which still runs it and is untouched by this block.
#
# WHY AN OPT-IN RATHER THAN AN UNCONDITIONAL RUN. The fixture configures and
# builds three CMake trees of `pace-cc-tests`. This tree's `ctest` presets
# carry no label filter, so a plain `ctest` runs `smoke` too -- registering it to
# run by default would put three full builds into every developer's test run and
# into every CI leg that calls `ctest`, none of which asked for them. The default
# is therefore OFF, and the name is registered EITHER WAY: opted in it is the real
# fixture, opted out it is a skip that says which flag turns it on. What it is
# never again is absent.
#
# So `ctest -N` gains exactly one row and a `ctest` run gains zero builds. That is
# measured rather than asserted -- see the commit -- because "it only registers a
# skip" is precisely the kind of claim that is easy to believe and cheap to check.
option(PACE_RUN_LAUNCHER_REPLAY_E2E
    "Run the launcher replay end-to-end fixture as part of ctest. Costs three CMake builds." OFF)

if(WIN32)
    # POSIX shell throughout, and the Windows launcher path has its own fixture
    # (`run-launcher-e2e.ps1`). Not a skip standing in for a pass: there is
    # nothing here for this platform to answer.
    pace_register_skipped_test("launcher-replay-e2e"
        "this fixture is POSIX-shell only; the Windows launcher path is covered by run-launcher-e2e.ps1")
elseif(NOT PACE_RUN_LAUNCHER_REPLAY_E2E)
    pace_register_skipped_test("launcher-replay-e2e"
        "not opted in -- it configures and builds three CMake trees, so it is off by default. Set -DPACE_RUN_LAUNCHER_REPLAY_E2E=ON to run it here; CI runs it from build.yml's 'A real target, replayed from cache' step regardless")
elseif(NOT TARGET pace-kv OR NOT TARGET pace-cc OR NOT TARGET pace-cc-tests)
    pace_register_skipped_test("launcher-replay-e2e"
        "opted in, but one of pace-kv / pace-cc / pace-cc-tests is not built in this tree, and the fixture needs all three")
else()
    # No `--port`: the fixture draws one. A registration cannot name a port -- a
    # fixture CHOOSING one cannot see a port already held as an outbound
    # connection's local endpoint, and two lanes' suites run at once here.
    # `build.yml` still passes its own, and that path is unchanged.
    #
    # `--canary` is not optional, in CI or here: it plants a deliberately wrong
    # object into the replayed build and requires the suite to go red. Without it
    # a green run is indistinguishable from a fixture that cannot fail.
    add_test(
        NAME "launcher-replay-e2e"
        COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/launcher-replay-e2e.sh"
            --pace-kv "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-kv>"
            --launcher "$<TARGET_FILE:pace-cc>"
            --compiler "${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}"
            --target pace-cc-tests
            --canary
    )
    # `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77` reads the fixture's own `skip`, which is the same 77
    # `build.yml` turns into `::error::replay E2E skipped, nothing verified`. The
    # two callers read it differently ON PURPOSE: in CI a skip means the leg did
    # not do its job, here it means a developer's machine lacks a prerequisite.
    #
    # `RUN_SERIAL` because it binds a port, spawns a daemon and runs three builds.
    # The TIMEOUT is an outer supervisor, not a measurement.
    set_tests_properties("launcher-replay-e2e" PROPERTIES
        LABELS "smoke"
        SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
        RUN_SERIAL TRUE
        TIMEOUT 3600
    )
endif()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A check registered only under a build OPTION is reachable by a CI job (#1254, #589).
#
# `tidy-blind-spots`' own registration block above states this residual in as many
# words -- "nothing yet asserts that some CI job still configures a build this can
# run in" -- and #589 asks the same question from the other side. One derived guard
# answers both; two hand-written lists would be the defect twice.
#
# DERIVED, never restated. The population is walked out of THIS FILE's `if()`
# nesting, so a conditional registration ARRIVING is covered on the day it lands. A
# restated list would catch one going away and be blind to one arriving, which is the
# direction that actually happens -- and a list reads identically whether it is
# complete or merely confident.
#
# It reads `build.yml` through `scripts/lib/workflow-walk.awk`, this tree's one model
# of workflow YAML. A private walk here would be refused by `workflow-walk-sole`.
#
# Needs no compiler, no build, no daemon, no socket and no network.
add_test(
    NAME "conditional-check-reach"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-conditional-check-reach.sh" --root "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
)
set_tests_properties("conditional-check-reach" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# Six cases. The ACCEPTING arm is first and is not decoration -- a guard that refused
# every tree would pass every refusing case. Case 5 is the one that matters most: an
# option ON by DEFAULT needs no job to name it, and a reader missing that route
# reports three healthy `vendor-tui-*` rows as unreachable. An over-report is loud and
# misattributed, which is worse to act on than a silent miss. Case 6 separates
# configuring from RUNNING, which is the whole property.
add_test(
    NAME "conditional-check-reach-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-conditional-check-reach.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("conditional-check-reach-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The clang install says WHICH KIND of failure it was (#1566).
#
# Master run 35172062484 died 36 s in on `Temporary failure in name resolution` and
# was reported as "clang-tsan failed" -- pointing whoever read it at the sanitizer
# and then at the source tree, for a DNS outage on a runner.
#
# What is tested is the CLASSIFIER, a pure function of the failing command's output:
# network-unreachable, package-absent, and a third outcome for a shape matching
# neither, which exits loud rather than being folded into whichever neighbour is
# closer. The acquisition around it needs a network and is not the part that was
# wrong. The last case is a scan asserting no call site has gone back to a bare
# `wget`, with a positive control on the file being read at all -- zero raw sites and
# zero converted ones is also what reading the wrong file reports.
#
# Needs no compiler, no build, no daemon, no socket and no network.
add_test(
    NAME "ci-install-clang-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/ci-install-clang.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("ci-install-clang-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# `status/needs-triage` comes off again (#1563).
#
# The label had three writers and no remover, and reached 97 of 119 open issues --
# at which point a query for it answers "almost everything", which is the same as
# answering nothing.
#
# What is tested is the DECISION, a pure function of (event, labels, actor type).
# The case that makes the actor a parameter at all is the auto-filer's: those
# issues carry `type/bug area/build status/needs-triage` from birth, so by labels
# alone they are indistinguishable from a human's triaged issue -- and a remover
# reading labels only would strip the label off every CI-filed issue within
# seconds, defeating the three writers this deliberately keeps.
#
# Two cases pin the substring anchors, on `opened` rather than `labeled`, because
# that is the event where an unanchored match gives a DIFFERENT answer. On
# `labeled` both readings answer `none` and the case proves nothing -- which is
# what the first spelling of them did.
#
# Needs no compiler, no build, no daemon, no socket and no network.
add_test(
    NAME "ci-triage-label-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/ci-triage-label.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("ci-triage-label-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# `RequiredContexts` against the ruleset the SERVER enforces (#1562).
#
# Every check around that table reconciles it with the WORKFLOWS. Nothing
# reconciled it with the RULESET, which is the only thing that actually blocks a
# merge -- so the tree and the server could disagree indefinitely with every check
# green, each correct about its own side. They DO disagree today.
#
# NEEDS THE NETWORK, so every way of not being able to ask is a SKIP that says
# which way it was: gh absent (127), gh unauthenticated (4), the API unreachable
# (exit 1 with a transport message). An HTTP error is NOT a skip -- that is GitHub
# answering, and an unreachable API and an agreeing set are not the same green.
#
# Through `run-check.sh` like every other script check: a check registered
# DIRECTLY reports a KILLED run and a FAILING one identically (#1079), and
# `run-check-coverage` refuses the bypass. The wrapper forwards the status
# untouched -- measured, a script exiting 77 through it still exits 77 and is
# classified `skipped` on its own terminal line -- so the skip arms below still
# reach `SKIP_RETURN_CODE`. Registering it directly to "protect" the 77 was
# solving a problem that does not exist.
#
# `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77` and `smoke`, since it reaches the network. The self-test
# below needs none of that and runs in the DEFAULT set, which is what keeps the
# decision covered on every machine.
add_test(
    NAME "required-context-ruleset"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-required-context-ruleset.sh" --root "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
)
set_tests_properties("required-context-ruleset" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "smoke"
    SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# Six cases, driven against a staged table and a staged ruleset. The staged table
# is a real `RequiredContexts=(` block so the awk reader parses it rather than
# being bypassed -- the first spelling handed the check a pre-parsed list and two
# cases failed reporting context names with their quotes still attached, which is
# the fixture testing itself rather than its subject.
#
# Case 4 is what makes the one recorded divergence EXPIRE rather than accumulate:
# with #1562 resolved in either direction, the exemption row is refused as stale,
# so deleting it is part of closing the ticket.
add_test(
    NAME "required-context-ruleset-selftest"
    COMMAND ${PACE_BASH} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/run-check.sh" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-required-context-ruleset.sh" --self-test
)
set_tests_properties("required-context-ruleset-selftest" PROPERTIES
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 60
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A staged POSIX-shell stub that a NON-SHELL spawns (#871).
#
# The rule had three prose homes -- this file applies it BY HAND at the
# `tidy-blind-spots-selftest` registration above, and both rules files restate it
# -- and no executor, so it held exactly as far as whoever wrote the next fixture
# had read. `execute_process`, `subprocess` and `CreateProcess` read no shebang;
# Git Bash does, so a fixture that runs happily by hand is one the check cannot
# start at all. That failure has landed here before (#668).
#
# RUNS ON WINDOWS, which is where the defect fires, and `cmake -P` is the portable
# vehicle. The scan stages nothing executable, so it cannot trip its own rule --
# though it IS a candidate by construction, since it carries every shebang it
# matches on, and it carries its own `data` row saying so.
#
# THREE CLASSES, because collapsing them would be an over-report: `data` (never
# executed -- bytes for a scan, or a file a probe must merely FIND), `adapted`
# (stages a form the spawner can start, so it runs everywhere) and `guarded`
# (kept off a platform, and then the REASON is the discriminator -- "Windows
# cannot start a shebang" is a portability excuse that costs coverage, where
# "the launcher sandbox this needs has no Windows equivalent" says there is
# nothing there to answer).
#
# Needs no compiler, no build, no daemon, no socket and no network.
add_test(
    NAME "staged-stub-spawns"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-staged-stub-spawns.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("staged-stub-spawns" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 120
)

# Seven cases, the ACCEPTING arms first and one of them for each accepting class.
# Each asserts WHICH refusal it got rather than merely that it refused.
#
# Its scratch trees go OUTSIDE the repository: staged inside, they would be
# enumerated by every other scan while they exist, and a case that aborted would
# leave artefacts under `scripts/`.
add_test(
    NAME "staged-stub-spawns-selftest"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DPACE_SOURCE_DIR=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"
        "-DPACE_SCRATCH_DIR=${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/check-staged-stub-spawns-selftest.cmake"
)
set_tests_properties("staged-stub-spawns-selftest" PROPERTIES
    FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "${PACE_SCRIPT_CHECK_FAILED}"
    LABELS "hygiene"
    TIMEOUT 180
)
